THE LINE COUNT WAS NEVER THE CEILING, which fix round 1b's subsection below records off the pilot's own re-measurement: the ceilings are the 20 localised parameters per table and the 20 nesting levels `maxLocalisedParams` carries (the nesting figure was read as a table count and re-measured as a level count in fix round 2, below), this tooltip spends 7 and 3, and its widest line by far is the CONSUMER'S own entry at 557 characters against 32 to 66 for the twelve lines under it, six of which are the consumer's own preset labels. What is open is how a long composed line WRAPS beside a label the closed widget truncates, and that is the third open claim in `agents/customizer-design.md`.

### The locale checker, and what it can and cannot be

`CheckLocale` gained a drift guard: the library composes the two sentences, so a text setting whose composed description lacks either is reported. Its first shape ran inside the per-setting loop and produced five identical findings on the example fixture, which at fifty text settings would fill `localeFindingCap = 100` and push every author finding out; it reports once now, first in the report, in the library's own voice. The existing "a text setting needs a description entry to tell the player the format" message was rewritten, because the library supplies the format, the limits and the fallback now, and the consumer's entry says what the setting is FOR.

The guard is fed `textDescription(full, "")` rather than the full composition, and the reason is a hazard nobody predicted: rendering the declared list is the only part that dereferences an item handle, and `CheckLocale` validates nothing by design, so asking it for the full composition turns a plan the planners refuse by name into a panic inside the checker. Constructed and confirmed: `IngredientsSetting("axe-ingredients", []Ingredient{IngredientOf(ItemRef{}, 1)})` then `declaredIngredientEntries` gives `runtime error: index out of range [-1]`.

`testdata/locale/findings.golden` did NOT move, and that is load-bearing rather than incidental: a healthy composition reports nothing new.

**What this rule is not.** It can only fire if this library's own composition drifts, so it is a drift guard rather than a finding a consumer can act on. Two author-facing alternatives were considered and rejected: a keyword scan of the consumer's entry for a promise of refusal (fragile, and silent on any non-English locale file), and a "this label reads like an ingredient list" heuristic over `[string-mod-setting]` values (the same objection, and it is the consumer's own item (g) in the assessment rather than the library's).

| Commit | `testdata/mirror/transcript.golden` | `testdata/ingame/dump-sha256.txt` | `flipped.golden.dat` |
|---|---|---|---|
| the invisible-character and reserved-word policy | unmoved | unmoved | unmoved |
| the ladder merge | unmoved | unmoved | unmoved |
| the player-text fallback | 2 hunks, 5 lines replaced, 47 lines either side | flipped DATA hash only | re-recorded |
| the composed description | 2 hunks, 8 lines replaced, 47 lines either side | both SETTINGS hashes only | unmoved |

Read those cells off `git show 2e5f779 --numstat -- testdata/mirror/transcript.golden`, which is `5 5`, and `git diff --numstat HEAD -- testdata/mirror/transcript.golden`, which is `8 8`; the file is 47 lines before and after both, so neither commit changed its length. The other cells check out as written: 2e5f779 moved the flipped row's data hash and left both settings hashes at `2b44da19...`, and re-recorded `flipped.golden.dat` for the new stored text; the description commit moved both rows' settings hash to `0254345f...` with both data hashes standing, and did not touch `flipped.golden.dat`.

THE COLUMNS ARE THE TWO GATE GOLDENS AND THE SETTINGS BLOB, and the first two commits moved nothing in them because no example guest types a character the new policy touches, declares a duplicate, or carries a colliding ladder. They did not move NOTHING under `testdata/`: the invisible-character and reserved-word commit moved `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` by 159 lines added and 25 removed, which CLAUDE.md calls the language's CONTRACT and which is the file that pins the new policy in both halves. The ladder-merge commit left the corpus alone, because a merge is a plan-time event rather than a parse. The fallback commit's stand-in stores a refused INGREDIENT text and its engine row a refused PACK text, so between the two gates both list kinds are covered on the fallback path; only a data hash moved, because a stored value does not move a setting prototype. The description commit is the mirror image: only the settings hashes moved, identically on both rows, because the settings dump holds prototypes and every composed description changed. The cost dropdown's transcript line did not move, which is the cost preset staying as it was.

### Size, measured over the whole round

`git archive 9754f49` against the working tree, both built at the same absolute scratch path (rustc and TinyGo write source paths into the wasm, so a differing path length reads as a fake delta), packaged with an `fklua` built from a `git archive` of FkLua b88965d, with the harness's own flags. Both trees' `rust/Cargo.lock` are byte-identical, so the delta is FkRecipes code alone.

| guest | wasm bytes | `fk_data_module.lua` bytes | lines |
|---|---|---|---|
| `go/examples/notext` | 665,182 to 716,980 (+7.79%) | 2,542,105 to 2,710,197 (+6.61%) | 63,271 to 68,049 |
| `rust/examples/notext` | 142,129 to 149,774 (+5.38%) | 2,171,601 to 2,293,724 (+5.62%) | 52,980 to 55,772 |
| `go/examples/datastage` | 1,020,119 to 1,087,279 (+6.58%) | 3,647,117 to 3,857,652 (+5.77%) | 95,017 to 101,216 |
| `rust/examples/datastage` | 232,977 to 244,345 (+4.88%) | 3,177,137 to 3,330,780 (+4.84%) | 79,515 to 83,272 |

The round costs about 5 to 8 percent everywhere, and it costs the no-text guests as much as the ones that declare text settings. Go pays more than Rust in both shapes. The BEFORE `datastage` rows reproduce the "Sizes at the same head" paragraph above exactly, which is what makes the two tables comparable; the one figure that differs is the Go wasm, by 356 bytes of DWARF path, which does not reach the packaged Lua.

**The seam holds and its edge is now visible.** Neither `notext` guest links a parser or a renderer: `invisible character`, `is longer than`, `stands alone` and `no item or fluid is named` are all absent from both, and present in the `datastage` control, and a header diff of the Go `notext` module shows six new functions and none of them from `ingredientlist.go`. But three description PROSE constants do reach every guest now, where all seven needles were absent at 9754f49: `Write internal names`, `set aside` and `type: `. Two arrive through the SETTINGS PLANNER, and the attribution this paragraph carried until the fix round below said the LOCALE CHECKER, which is the wrong way round and is corrected here rather than in a footnote, because it is the sentence a later reader would act on. The path is `PlanSettings` -> `settingDescriptions` (`go/settings.go:51` calls it unconditionally) -> `textDescription`, which names `textFormatLine()` and `textFallbackLine` as ordinary code; what decides whether a given setting gets a description is `s.kind.isText()` at `go/customize.go:625`, a RUNTIME branch over a value in the plan and not a link-time seam, so elimination keeps both strings in every guest. The locale checker links nothing anywhere: no example guest calls `CheckLocale` / `check_locale`, so it is dropped whole, and its own sentences (`not in this locale file`, `no line about the format`, `[mod-setting-description] entry`) are 0 occurrences in all four packaged modules at every head measured. That was proven rather than reasoned, in the fix round below: a build with ONLY the planner's naming moved behind the seam, `composedTextLinesMissing` left naming both constants exactly as it does here, produced a `notext` module byte-identical to one where the checker was moved as well. The third needle arrives because the preset head is a constant beside a call through the seam's function value. 234 bytes of string data (66 and 12 for the format line's two literals, 147 for the fallback line, 9 for the preset head) against 168 KB of growth, so it is a linkage observation rather than a size driver, and the comment that claims "naming it here links no part of the language" is true as written and narrower than these needles.

Jump rows (`limit_bytes` 655,355, `hop_bytes` 327,677). NEITHER GATE PACKAGES `notext`, so the rows below are the `datastage` pair alone, and no `notext` jump row exists outside this round's own scratch packaging pass. `go/examples/datastage` went from the recorded 988,326 bytes of widest pre-relay span over 12 stations (the row in the sync-pass section above, at 9754f49) to 1,018,671 over 14; `rust/examples/datastage` from 545,785 with no relay to 577,567, still with no relay. Both AFTER figures are read out of `jq -c '.jumps.data'` over the reports this round's gate runs left in `tmp/mirror/` and `tmp/ingame/`, which agree with each other byte for byte per language. THAT AFTER PAIR IS AT 0077f3c AND IS NOT CURRENT: Fix round 1b's self-product commit moved both spans, and the pair at 1f8e363 is in that round's own subsection below. Block room is unmoved on both: 314,648 for Go (widest block 13,029 in `copyValue`) and 323,877 for Rust (3,800 in `value::not_text`), the same figures as at 9754f49, so nothing is near refusing. An earlier draft of this paragraph also carried 318,831 bytes of block room; that came from the scratch pass, not from a gate report, and this correction pass neither re-took it nor established which guest it belongs to.

### Gates

Every gate in CLAUDE.md's Gates block ran before every one of the four commits, exit codes read directly and never through a pipe, all 0: `gofmt -l .` (no output), `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./...`, `go/examples/notext` `go vet .`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo test`, `RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --workspace`, `scripts/run-mirror.sh` and `scripts/run-ingame.sh`. The engine gate ran on `Version: 2.0.77 (build 84539, mac-arm64, steam)`, re-asked before each run, with `FACTORIO_USERDIR` at its private `/tmp/fkrecipes` default. The suites grew from 175 Rust unit tests to 196, and the corpus from 204 cases to 242. The Rust baseline is 175 rather than the 176 the fallback commit's message says: counted as `#[test]` attributes under `rust/src` at both revisions (`git grep -c '#\[test\]' 9754f49 -- 'rust/src/*.rs'` against the working tree), a method whose current answer, 196, is exactly what `cargo test` reports for the lib target. The corpus figures are `in:|` lines in `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` at the same two revisions.

### What the reviews found, which is why there were four of them

Every one of the four adversarial reviews found something the implementation had got wrong, and two of them found defects that would have shipped silently:

- the invisible-character commit's first shape had a Go per-member test that read the set OUT of the predicate, so narrowing a range simply removed those points from the test: 15 code points dropped and the whole suite stayed green while the two halves diverged. Both halves now carry an independently spelled range table.
- the merge commit's first shape swallowed an author's literal duplicate, turning an engine refusal into a silent `6`.
- the fallback commit's first shape claimed totality in seven places where the property is narrower, and left the Rust half with no refusal-ordering witness at all after a rename.
- the description commit's first shape promised a load in the one case where the library does not deliver one.

Three defects nobody had asked about turned up while taking review findings: the cost and crafting-time checks ran over whatever `readNumber` returned, so an UNREADABLE setting with an illegal declared default logged a player-facing line blaming a stored value that never existed; the declared pack path emitted amounts the engine refuses; and Rust's stands-alone sentence interpolated the player's entry where Go interpolated the literal word, which was invisible until the case fold made `NONE stands alone` possible.

### What BetterBeltBalancer must do to adopt this

Nothing in BBB is required for the library changes to take effect: they are all library-side, and BBB's declarations compile unchanged. BOTH HALVES OF THAT ARE TRUE AND BOTH TOGETHER ARE INCOMPLETE, which the pilot measured: compiling is not the gate a consumer has, and its suite came out at exit 2 on five tests. Fix round 1b's migration subsection below has what went red and what `docs/migration.md` now owes an upgrading consumer. What BBB should now DO, in the assessment's own numbering:

1. **(f) Reconsider `FallbackName`.** `guest/go/tune/tune.go:168` sets it to `iron-plate` and every ladder ends on it, so the vanilla and cheap presets now MERGE rather than refuse: a pack without `transport-belt` gets `6 iron-plate, 2 iron-gear-wheel` and a log line, not a dead game. That is a better outcome than the crash and it is still probably not the balance intended. The ladders can now be checked against what is already chosen, and a ladder whose collapse the author does not want is one rung the author should drop rather than one the library should guess at.
2. **Declare no literal duplicate.** A declared list, choice preset or unit that names one item twice now REFUSES at plan time, where it used to reach the engine. BBB declares none today; a future preset that does will be told by name.
3. **(g) Rewrite `bbb-recipe-cost`'s description, and scope the reassurance to the presets.** The assessment asks for two things here and both still stand. First, the description is byte-identical to published 0.2.2's and never mentions Custom, while its twin `bbb-tech-cost`'s was rewritten and does; it needs the same rewrite, and it needs to mention Custom. Second, `If an option names something your mods do not have, the nearest thing they do have is used instead.` MUST BE SCOPED TO THE PRESETS. This round did not make it true of the field, and could not have: a typed name the game lacks is not substituted with anything, the WHOLE typed text is set aside, and the author's declared list with its ladders applies instead. That is what the library's own new line says in as many words, `A text this mod cannot use is set aside and that default applies instead`. A player who reads the unscoped sentence over the field is told their typo will be quietly nudged onto a neighbour; what actually happens is that everything they typed stops counting. The rewrite is shorter than it was under refusal, but it is still a rewrite: the library now supplies the format, the 2000-character limit and the fallback policy, so the consumer's entry only has to say what the setting is FOR.
4. **(h) Leave the preset labels alone, or rewrite them, but decide it.** The library no longer prints both vocabularies as one run of text: each preset's internal-name list is on its own indented `type:` line under the label. A label spelling display names is now merely unhelpful rather than a trap. If BBB rewrites its labels in internal names, the tooltip reads the same thing twice; if it keeps them, the `type:` line is the answer.
5. **Two changelog sentences the settings screen cannot say**, both now written up for authors in `docs/migration.md`. Adding a `custom` value to a dropdown that older releases lack means a player who runs an older release once has that choice reset, silently and permanently, and the typed text surviving is what makes it look like nothing was lost. And a Custom cost arm moves the technology's prerequisite to the ladder's first existing entry even when all three numbers are untouched.
6. **Re-run the assessment's finding 2 and finding 1 arms.** Both should now be green, and both have a library-side gate behind them, but the pilot is where a real client can confirm the log line reads well on screen and that the composed tooltip's long first line, which is the consumer's own entry rather than anything the library writes, wraps rather than clipping.
7. **(i) `release/2.0` is BBB's alone** and nothing here touches it.

### What this round leaves open on this side

- The fallback is not total: an author declaration that cannot produce a legal result in a given mod set still stops the load, and a player who had typed a working list around it loses that workaround to a typo. The added sentence tells them what to change; the load still stops.
- The 13-line tooltip has not been seen by a client. The seven-line measurement is on the old shape. REFRAMED in fix round 1b, by the pilot's own re-measurement of the composition rather than by a render: THE LINE COUNT WAS NEVER THE CEILING. The two ceilings are 20 localised parameters per table and 20 nesting levels (the second figure re-measured in fix round 2, below) and that tooltip spends 7 and 3, while its widest line by far is the CONSUMER'S own entry at 557 characters against 32 to 66 for the twelve lines under it, six of which are the consumer's own preset labels. What is open in its place is the WRAP, and the closed dropdown's roughly 37-character label truncation beside it; that round's tooltip subsection below carries the figures and the two hovers a client run answers them with.
- Three description prose constants link into a guest that declares no text setting. CLOSED in fix round 1b, by prose rather than by code: the claim carries its adjective everywhere it appears now (no parser, no renderer, no amount formatter, no custom-cost resolver), the cost is measured at 234 bytes of string data across the three, and the mechanism is the settings planner's runtime branch on a setting's kind and not the locale checker, which links nothing in any guest. The alternative, moving the three behind the seam as well, was implemented in full, gated green, measured, and NOT TAKEN: it grows the packaged Lua a player downloads in ALL FOUR guests (+657, +82, +2,013 and +1,315 bytes), and the wasm only in the two that declare text settings (+855 and +128), shrinking it by 1,296 and 273 on the two `notext` fixtures the item was actually about. THIS BULLET SAID "it shrinks the wasm and grows the packaged Lua ... in all four guests" until fix round 1b's last pass, which is false of the wasm in two of the four and is contradicted by the table it summarises; the six deltas above are that table's own two columns subtracted. Both sets of numbers are in that round's subsection below.
- The engine's own behaviour with a NUL inside a load-failure message is unmeasured. The escaping makes it moot, not answered. What a NUL does to a STORED VALUE has two measurements that contradict each other, above, and neither was re-taken here. BOTH CLOSED in fix round 1b, by an engine pass that took both questions on 2.0.77 and a second pass that re-ran its headline arm: the engine DOES cut a load-failure message at a NUL and prints only what precedes it, so the escaping is confirmed to be what makes every message THE INGREDIENT LANGUAGE composes independent of the byte rather than assumed to be; and both stored-value notes turned out to be right about their own range, on opposite sides of a 22-byte cliff, so neither was wrong about what it ran and the design record's inference on top of its row was. That round's NUL subsection carries the instrument, the six arms, the two sweeps and the commands, AND IT OPENED TWO ITEMS OF ITS OWN in its own list: one message producer outside the language still interpolates a stored value unescaped, and below the cliff the engine deletes part of the player's text silently and permanently.
- The declared-pack path's old `maxExactInt` cap is now the measured 65535, but the TYPED path always checked 65535 and the two were inconsistent for a while; nothing else in the library holds two ceilings for one kind of value, and it is worth a look if a third appears.

### Corrections to numbers this round's own commit messages carry

History is linear here and is not rewritten, so two landed commit messages still carry figures and claims the section above now corrects. A reader of the log should take the section as the current answer:

- **3a9de88** says the set "grew from 18 ranges to 28, from 4237 code points to 4287": the new pair is right, the old pair is not, and the old set is 11 ranges and 114 code points. The same message calls 3769 what "the plane-14 tag block contributes": 3769 is the total over three reserved runs and the tag-block range contributes 3759 of it. It says "Seven sites in each half, all of them" of `quotable()`: Go has 11 and Rust 10. It says the ASCII fold is "written out rather than taken from either standard library": only Go writes it out, and Rust's `eq_ignore_ascii_case` is what Go was written to match. And it states the design record's NUL row as settled: the row was not settled as written, and the assessment's finding 10 that contradicts it was not the answer either, because the engine's behaviour is length-dependent and each note measured one side of a 22-byte cliff. Fix round 1b's NUL subsection below is where the measurement is, and the design record's row now carries it.
- **2e5f779** says "Eight sites fall back": the enumeration that follows it is seven, and the code sites are four in Go and five in Rust. Its red proof (a) quotes the old code's refusal as `no item or fluid is named ...`: the flipped row's refused value is a PACK list, so the sentence is `no science pack is named militar-science-pack`. Its gate line reads "cargo test (192, was 176 at the start of the round)": the baseline is 175.
- Nothing in **696387f** is corrected here.

## Fix round 1b

### A resolved list that names the recipe's own product now says so

**The shape is ACCEPTED, and that is measured rather than remembered.** On 2.0.77 (build 84539, mac-arm64, steam), base alone under a private user directory:

```
jq '.recipe["kovarex-enrichment-process"] | {ingredients, results}' script-output/data-raw-dump.json
```

gives ingredients `uranium-235` 40 and `uranium-238` 5, results `uranium-235` 41 and `uranium-238` 2. A jq sweep of the same dump for recipes naming the same type and name in both `ingredients` and `results` returns exactly two of base's 217 recipes: `coal-liquefaction` (the fluid `heavy-oil`, 25 in and 90 out) and `kovarex-enrichment-process`. Only the second is a shape this library could ever emit, because a product here is always an item: `recipeProto` writes `type="item"` unconditionally and `results` is in `recipeOwnFields`, so `Extra` cannot override it. A library that refused this would be refusing something the game itself does, so nothing here refuses. What was missing was the SIGNAL: `planItemWorld` answers for the plan's own products, so a player's typed text could name one and an author could declare one, and the load said nothing at all.

**One log line, in the merge line's voice.** It is not a refusal and it is not an `fkrecipes: ERROR: ` line: that prefix belongs to a stored value the library set aside, and nothing is set aside here.

```
fkrecipes: bbb-balancer-part: bbb-balancer-part is in the list and is also what this recipe makes, so nothing can craft the first one unless something else produces it
```

The subject is the DECLARED recipe name, which is what every merge line already carries; the product is the EMITTED name, because it is a prototype name the game will hold and the resolved ingredient names it is compared against are emitted too. Both are in that one example because the pilot's recipe is legacy and its two names coincide.

**THE MIDDLE CLAUSE WENT, AND THAT IS THE ROUND'S BIGGEST EDIT.** The line first shipped as `... is also what this recipe makes, so this recipe consumes what it makes; nothing can craft the first one unless something else produces it`. Two independent adversarial reviews found the same defect: in every sibling line of this stream the `so` clause reports what the LIBRARY DID (`so the amounts are added: 4 plus 2 is 6`, `so the ingredient is dropped`, `so the plain ingredients apply`, `so the text is ignored`, `so its default applies`), and this was the only line whose `so` clause restated the clause before it. `so this recipe consumes what it makes` is what `is in the list and is also what this recipe makes` had just said. So the clause went and `so` moved onto the consequence, which is the one thing in the sentence the reader could not have worked out. The Rust reviewer's alternative, `so the list is emitted as written`, was REJECTED: a recipe can write a drop line or a merge line and then this one, and the list it is emitted with is then not what anybody wrote.

**FOUR ARMS ADD A RESOLVED LIST AND A CHECK IN ONE WOULD MISS THREE.** Go's `resolve` adds through `IngredientsFrom`, a dropdown's Custom arm, a dropdown on a preset and a plain declared `Ingredients`; Rust's does the same four in a different source order. So the check does not live in an arm at all: `resolution.addRecipe` / `Resolution::add_recipe` is now the one hand-over every arm gives its list to, and it is the only thing in either half that writes the resolved-recipes slice. That also settles the log position for free, in every arm and without any arm knowing it: the line is evaluated on the FINAL list, so it lands after every merge and drop line its own recipe wrote and before the next recipe's first line, which is what `go/data.go`'s and `rust/src/data.rs`'s stated log order requires.

**THE COMPILER DOES NOT ENFORCE THE HAND-OVER, AND SAYING IT DID WAS AN OVERCLAIM.** The first shape of the comment on `addRecipe` said "an arm that does not go through here does not compile a plan at all". A fifth arm that appended to `res.recipes` itself compiles, plans, and is merely silent, which is the whole reason the source property exists. What the language does catch is an arm that adds NO entry: `PlanData` indexes `res.recipes[i]` against `l.recipes` at `go/data.go:79` and a short slice panics there, at run time. Both halves now say that, and both name the source property as the enforcement.

The product itself comes out of one helper, `recipeProduct` / `recipe_product`, which the prototype builder now reads too: two spellings of "what does this recipe make" would let the log line and the `results` field disagree. It covers both declaration shapes, a handle into this plan's items and a `ResultNamed` naming somebody else's, and answers empty for a recipe that declares neither, which `validate` refuses before resolution runs. Its parameter order is `(prefix, l, r)` in both halves: every sibling builder in `rust/src/data.rs` takes the prefix first (`item_proto`, `recipe_proto`, `tech_proto`) and the Rust helper had landed as `(l, prefix, r)`, out of step with its own file and with the Go twin.

**THE KIND IS PART OF THE IDENTITY, AND IT IS TESTED POSITIVELY IN BOTH HALVES.** A product is always an item, an INGREDIENT can be a fluid, and an item and a fluid of one name genuinely coexist: base carries `parameter-0` to `parameter-9` as both. Go asks `ing.kind != kindItem`; Rust had landed on `ing.amount.is_fluid()`, which is the same answer only while `Amount` has exactly two variants. A third variant would make Rust fire where Go stays silent, and `is_fluid`'s `matches!` would not force anyone to visit the site. Rust now asks `!matches!(ing.amount, Amount::Item(_))`, so a third variant is a compile-time visit. The comment claiming the test is done "exactly as it is in `merge_ingredient`" was wrong about the MECHANISM there (an exhaustive `match (e.amount, amount)` whose mismatched arm falls through), and now says what is true: the kind is part of the identity there too.

**AND THE `break` WENT, BECAUSE IT MADE A STATED RED PROOF FALSE.** Both halves stopped at the first matching entry, and the comment beside it, the test comment, and this file all claimed the both-kinds sub-case was what a dropped kind test cannot produce. It is not, under a break: exactly one line comes out either way, and both reviewers verified the sub-case stays green. Measured here too, with the break restored and the kind test dropped: the isolated both-kinds plan passes, exit 0. With no early exit, a list holding an item and a fluid of one name genuinely witnesses the kind test, because without the kind test both entries match by name and the line comes out twice. At most one entry can match anyway, by two independent guards, `mergeIngredient` / `merge_ingredient` on the declared path and the language's own "entries N and M both name" refusal on the typed path (which `resolveIngredientsFrom` / `resolve_text_ingredients` relies on, collecting its entries without merging them). If both guards ever failed, a line appearing twice is a louder symptom than a quieter one.

**The source guard, and what it now actually holds.** A new arm that wrote the slice directly would pass every behavioural test, because a test only asserts about the arms it happens to build a plan through. `TestOnlyTheHandOverWritesTheResolvedRecipes` (Go, over the `go/ast` walk the message properties already use) and `tests::source::only_the_hand_over_writes_the_resolved_recipes` (Rust, over the blanked code the seam property already produces) are that guard, and BOTH were weaker than the sentence that stood here, which called them "each language's cheapest exact rule".

- **Go** flagged only an `*ast.AssignStmt` whose LHS was a bare `*ast.SelectorExpr`. Five compiling, gofmt-clean, vet-clean shapes went straight past it: a composite literal `resolution{recipes: lists}` (the shape a build-it-at-the-end refactor produces), `&res.recipes` taken and appended through a helper, `res.recipes[i] = list` (a post-resolution pass, which would rewrite a list AFTER the check ran on it), a parenthesised LHS `(res.recipes) = ...`, and `for _, res.recipes = range ... {}`. The walk now unwraps `*ast.ParenExpr` and `*ast.IndexExpr` on the LHS and also flags a `*ast.CompositeLit` with a `recipes:` key, a `*ast.UnaryExpr` `&<sel>.recipes`, and the Key and Value of an `*ast.RangeStmt`. Each of the five was injected and watched go red.
- **Rust** counted the literal token `recipes.push(` per module, which is green for `res.recipes.extend([list])`, `res.recipes.append(&mut vec![list])`, `res.recipes.insert(res.recipes.len(), list)`, `let sink = &mut res.recipes; sink.push(list)`, `Vec::push(&mut res.recipes, list)`, an accessor returning `&mut Vec<..>`, a plain `res.recipes = lists`, and, sharpest because it is a plausible refactor rather than a contrivance, lifting the push out of `add_recipe` into a `push_resolved` helper that a fifth arm calls directly, which leaves `data.rs` with exactly one push while `add_recipe` no longer sees every list. It also read `src/` with a non-recursive `read_dir`, so a writer in a future subdirectory module would never have been scanned at all. The rule is now three things at once: the module carries exactly one `recipes.push(`; that push sits INSIDE the brace-matched body of the one named function (`fn add_recipe(` in `data.rs`, `fn recipe_decl(` in `plan.rs`, which is the private constructor the Go twin's row calls `recipe`); and none of a small refused set (`recipes.extend(`, `recipes.append(`, `recipes.insert(`, `recipes.resize(`, `recipes = `, `&mut self.recipes`, `&mut res.recipes`, `Vec::push(&mut`) appears outside that body. The walk is recursive, excluding `src/tests/` exactly as the Go twin's `packageSources` drops every `_test.go`. Each of the eight shapes was injected and watched go red, every one of them `cargo fmt --check` clean and clippy clean with warnings denied.

**What the two properties hold, stated exactly.** Go holds every write reference to a field named `recipes` in the package's non-test files to the two allowed functions, across the five syntactic shapes above; a shape outside those five (a `copy` into an existing slice, say, which cannot grow it) is not covered, and no shape found in review escapes it. Rust holds the count, the position and the refused set above; ONE shape still escapes it and is recorded rather than papered over: `res = Resolution { recipes: lists, ..Default::default() }`, a wholesale reconstruction of the resolution, compiles, formats, lints and leaves the property green. The Go twin catches its equivalent through the composite-literal rule. Closing it in Rust would need a `Resolution {` or `recipes: ` needle, and both have real occurrences today (`fn resolve(...) -> Resolution {`, `impl Resolution {`, the field declaration, and `Lib::new`'s own `recipes: Vec::new()`), so it is a false-positive trade this round did not take.

**Technologies are untouched.** A technology has no product: `techProto` never writes a `results` field, so nothing was added to that path.

**The corpus did not move.** `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` is a PARSE-level contract, an `in:` line and an `ok:`/`refuse:` line against a fixture World, with no recipe, no plan and no log stream in the format. The merge line is the precedent and it is pinned in the PLAN-level suites; these pins sit beside it, in `go/data_test.go` and `rust/src/tests/data.rs`.

**The re-resolution arm now has a pin.** A dropdown on a preset whose chosen plan names nothing this game has falls back to the DEFAULT option's plan, which resolves the same recipe's list a SECOND time; it is the only arm that does, and nothing drove it. `TestThePresetFallbackResolvesTwiceAndTheLineFollowsTheSecond` / `the_preset_fallback_resolves_twice_and_the_line_follows_the_second` pins the whole stream in both halves: the chosen plan's drop line, then the fallback line, then the self-product line the default plan earned, then the two extends.

**The three routes that reach the line, each verified rather than argued.** `docs/usage.md` had claimed "a ladder that collapses onto your own item reaches this as readily as a list the player typed", and that is false. `resolve` hands the REAL World to `resolveIngredients` in every arm and the `planItemWorld` overlay only to the text path, so a declared ladder's rungs are probed against a world that does not hold this plan's items: the rung is simply absent and the ordinary drop line fires. Measured, one plan, `IngredientNamed(2, "tungsten-plate", "steelworks-balancer-part")` on a recipe producing `steelworks-balancer-part`: `fkrecipes: balancer-part: none of tungsten-plate, steelworks-balancer-part is present, so the ingredient is dropped`, and no self-product line. The three routes that DO reach it, each driven on its own plan: an `IngredientOf` handle to the recipe's own result item; a list the player typed, through the overlay; and a ladder landing on the EXISTING item a `ResultNamed` recipe makes, which is somebody else's item rather than one of yours. The doc now names those three and says why a declared ladder is not among them.

**Red proofs.** Each break was made on purpose, the message observed, and the break reverted. The five earlier rows (a fifth arm in each half, each half's hand-over emptied, each half's kind test dropped) stand from the first pass and are not repeated; these are this round's.

**What moved in the goldens.** Two transcripts, both the same plan in the two halves: `go/consumer_test.go`'s `TestLegacyAndGeneratedPrototypesInterlock` and `rust/src/tests/consumer.rs`'s `legacy_and_generated_prototypes_interlock`. Each declares `LegacyRecipe(part, "bbb-balancer-part", ...)` whose one ingredient is a handle to its own result item, so each gains the line as transcript line 0 (log ops come before every extend op). Nothing else in the repository does this, and the sweep is reproducible: `testdata/mirror/transcript.golden` carries FOUR recipe prototypes, `fkrecipes-example-steel-rivet`, `-hardened-steel-plate-quenching`, `-salvaged-steel-rivet` and `-steel-chain`, and each one's `results` name is absent from its own `ingredients` block; `testdata/ingame/flipped.json` names `steel-plate`, `water` and `iron-stick` in recipes producing `fkrecipes-example-hardened-steel-plate` and `fkrecipes-example-steel-rivet`; and neither `datastage` guest nor either `notext` guest declares a self-product. So `testdata/mirror/transcript.golden` and `testdata/ingame/dump-sha256.txt` DID NOT MOVE, and neither did `flipped.golden.dat`. The engine gate's `EXACTLY ONE fkrecipes: ERROR: ` assertion is unaffected, because this line carries no `ERROR` prefix.

**The size fixtures are stale from this commit onward.** `selfProductLine` / `self_product_line` is a new UNCONDITIONAL string, linked into every guest whether or not it declares a text setting, and that includes the two `notext` size fixtures whose whole job is the "a plan with no text setting links no ingredient language" number. The figures recorded for them elsewhere in this file were taken before this line existed. They are re-taken in a later commit of this same round; nothing here measured them. That commit is the subsection below, and it re-takes all four; the slogan is quoted here in the form it had at this commit, and the subsection below is where it gets its adjective.

**Gates.** Every gate in CLAUDE.md's Gates block that this round is asked to run, exit codes read directly and never through a pipe, all 0: `gofmt -l .` (no output), `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./...` (test cache cleaned first), `go/examples/notext` `go vet .`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo test`, `RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --workspace`. `scripts/run-mirror.sh` and `scripts/run-ingame.sh` are not this round's to run; the goldens they read are argued unmoved above. The Rust lib target went from 196 `#[test]` attributes to 203 and the Go suite from 214 `func Test` to 221, seven new tests each half: four arms in one, the `ResultNamed` shape, the fluid negative, the anti-vacuity negative, the order pin, the preset re-resolution pin and the source guard. `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` stands at 242 `in:` lines, the figure it had before the round. The repository-wide dash sweep (`perl -CSD -ne 'print if /[\x{2013}\x{2014}]/'` over `git ls-files`) returns the same three lines it returns at the previous head, all of them `agents/docs-style.md` quoting the characters it bans.

**What a consumer must do.** Nothing. The change is library-side and every declaration compiles unchanged. What a consumer will now OBSERVE that it did not before is one extra log line per recipe whose resolved list names its own product, and a consumer whose transcript is asserted line by line has that line to add. BetterBeltBalancer's own recipe is exactly that shape in the pilot fixture, so it is the first consumer this reaches.

### The claim gets its adjective, and the alternative that would have earned the old one was measured and refused

**WHAT THE CLAIM WAS.** Six sentences in five files overreached, and a seventh, at the end of the self-product subsection above, quotes the slogan on purpose: it is that commit's own record of what it was making stale, so it keeps the wording it had then and gains one clause saying which subsection gives it its adjective. Of the six, five said some form of "a plan with no text setting links no ingredient language" with no adjective on it, and two of those went further than the seam ever did: `README.md` and `docs/usage.md` both ended with "pays nothing for the customizer it does not use". That clause is false, and it was false when it was written. The sixth is about the mechanism rather than the claim, and it had the mechanism backwards.

**WHAT A `notext` GUEST ACTUALLY LINKS, AND BY WHICH PATH.** Three description PROSE constants, 234 bytes of string data between them: `textFormatLine()`'s two literals (66 bytes before the ceiling it renders at run time and 12 after, which is why the GO module reads `in at most  characters.` with two spaces and no number in it). THAT RENDERING IS THE GO MODULE'S ALONE: `grep -cF 'in at most  characters.'` answers 1 in both Go modules and 0 in both Rust ones, which carry two escaped bytes between the same two pieces, where Go's concatenation carries nothing, and read `in at most \192\12 characters.`. Both halves carry the 66 and the 12, so the 234 holds either way. The other two are `textFallbackLine` (147) and `ingredientPresetHead` (9). They arrive through the SETTINGS PLANNER. In Go, `PlanSettings` calls `settingDescriptions` unconditionally at `go/settings.go:51`; that walk hands every text setting to `textDescription` at `go/customize.go:713`; and `textDescription` names `textFormatLine()` and `textFallbackLine` as ordinary code. The only thing standing between those two constants and a plan that declares no text setting is `s.kind.isText()` at `go/customize.go:625`, a RUNTIME branch over a value in the plan. Whole-program elimination cannot read a runtime branch, so it keeps both strings in every guest. RUST REACHES THE SAME PLACE BY A SHORTER PATH AND HAS NO `setting_descriptions` TWIN: `plan_settings` inlines the walk, its runtime gate is `if is_text(s.kind)` at `rust/src/settings.rs:89`, and that arm calls `text_description` at `:98`, which names `text_format_line()` and `TEXT_FALLBACK_LINE` the way the Go one does. Two further strings ride the same frame and nothing can remove them while the composition exists at all: `\10default: ` and `mod-setting-description` are 1 occurrence each in all four packaged modules, at this head and under every variant tried below.

**THE ATTRIBUTION IN THIS FILE WAS INVERTED, WHICH IS THE FINDING THIS COMMIT REALLY CARRIES.** The round-1 paragraph above said the two sentences arrive "because the locale checker builds an unconditional table naming `textFormatLine()` and `textFallbackLine`". That is backwards. The locale checker is in NO packaged guest: neither example guest calls `CheckLocale` / `check_locale`, so elimination drops it whole, and its own finding sentences (`not in this locale file`, `no line about the format`, `[mod-setting-description] entry`) are 0 occurrences in all four `fk_data_module.lua`, at 0077f3c and at this head. It was found by building the alternative below in two halves instead of one: a build with ONLY the planner's naming moved behind the seam, `composedTextLinesMissing` left naming both constants exactly as it does at HEAD, produced a `notext` module BYTE-IDENTICAL to the one where the checker was moved as well (2,710,854 bytes on 0077f3c, all three needles at 0, `cmp` at exit 0). The checker's half of that change was worth exactly ZERO bytes OF MODULE; it is worth 8 bytes of wasm, 715,688 with only the planner moved against 715,680 with the checker moved too, and the wasm is not the artefact a player downloads. An inverted attribution is worse than a missing one: it is the sentence a later reader acts on, and acting on this one would have bought nothing at all.

**THE ALTERNATIVE, BUILT IN FULL AND THEN REFUSED.** Option A was to move the three constants behind the same seam the ingredient language sits behind. It was implemented on 0077f3c, both suites green, `scripts/run-mirror.sh` at rc 0 (`run-mirror: OK`; jump rows Go span 1,018,065 bytes over 14 stations with 314,648 bytes of block room, Rust 577,567 with no relay and 323,877), and measured with the same recipe as the re-take below. THE RUST SPAN AND BOTH BLOCK ROOMS ARE UNMOVED against the unpatched 0077f3c report and the GO SPAN IS NOT: 1,018,065 against that report's 1,018,671 is 606 bytes narrower, which this paragraph called unmoved until its own figures were read back against it. Its patch is 9 files, +204/-50, and it applies to a clean `git archive 0077f3c` (`git apply --check`, exit 0, re-verified while writing this). IT IS NOT IN THIS REPOSITORY. It lives in this round's scratch tree and nowhere else, so when that tree goes the patch goes; what survives is this table.

| guest | wasm before, after | `fk_data_module.lua` before, after | lines before, after |
|---|---|---|---|
| `go/examples/notext` | 716,976 to 715,680 | 2,710,197 to 2,710,854 | 68,049 to 68,052 |
| `rust/examples/notext` | 149,774 to 149,501 | 2,293,724 to 2,293,806 | 55,772 to 55,785 |
| `go/examples/datastage` | 1,087,275 to 1,088,130 | 3,857,652 to 3,859,665 | 101,216 to 101,287 |
| `rust/examples/datastage` | 244,345 to 244,473 | 3,330,780 to 3,332,095 | 83,272 to 83,308 |

It does what it says: all three needles go from 1 to 0 in both `notext` modules, and the wasm SHRINKS by 1,296 and 273 bytes THERE. THE WASM DIRECTION IS NOT THE SAME IN ALL FOUR, which is the row a summary of this table has to carry: the two guests that DO declare text settings grow theirs, by 855 and 128 bytes, and nothing here measured why. It GROWS THE PACKAGED LUA, which is the artefact a player downloads, in ALL FOUR guests: +657 and +82 on the two fixtures it was for, and +2,013 and +1,315 on the pair it was never about. Every one of those six deltas is this table's own two columns subtracted. The two needles it cannot remove stand at 1 in all four modules afterwards, because the composition's frame links either way.

**OPTION B WAS TAKEN, and the reason is a cost comparison rather than a preference.** Option A buys one narrow sentence: "links NOTHING" in place of "links no parser, no renderer, no amount formatter and no custom-cost resolver". It costs bytes on the shipped artefact in every guest, including the two the item was never about; 254 lines of source churn across two languages; and a second seam to keep mirrored in both halves forever. Correcting the sentence costs the sentence. So the three constants stay where they are, every claim that overreached now carries its adjective, and the fixtures stay the place the number is re-taken.

**THE FOUR FIXTURES, RE-TAKEN AT THIS HEAD.** The previous commit added `selfProductLine` / `self_product_line`, an unconditional string in every guest, and said so; these are the numbers it promised.

```sh
S=<scratch>                                     # both repo copies live under this one directory
mkdir -p "$S"/fk "$S"/bin "$S"/out "$S"/repoA
( cd <FkLua checkout> && git archive b88965d ) | tar -x -C "$S/fk"
( cd "$S/fk" && go build -o "$S/bin/fklua" ./cmd/fklua )
( cd <FkRecipes>      && git archive HEAD    ) | tar -x -C "$S/repoA"   # 1f8e363
# The tinygo and cargo flags below are the ones scripts/run-mirror.sh builds with.
# The mod name and version are NOT the harness's, which packages as
# fkrecipes-example 0.1.0; they are the pilot's, and they do not reach the module.
for g in notext datastage; do
  ( cd "$S/repoA/go/examples/$g" && tinygo build -target=wasm-unknown \
      -scheduler=none -gc=leaking -opt=2 -o "$S/out/$g-go.wasm" . )
  ( cd "$S/repoA/rust" && cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -p "$g" )
  cp "$S/repoA/rust/target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/release/$g.wasm" "$S/out/$g-rust.wasm"
  for lang in go rust; do
    "$S/bin/fklua" mod --data-module "$S/out/$g-$lang.wasm" --name better-belt-balancer \
      --version 0.3.2 --author Techrocket9 --factorio-version 2.0 \
      -o "$S/out/mods-$g-$lang" --report "$S/out/report-$g-$lang.json"
    wc -c "$S/out/$g-$lang.wasm"
    wc -lc "$(find "$S/out/mods-$g-$lang" -name fk_data_module.lua)"
  done
done
```

TinyGo 0.41.1, cargo 1.97.1, go1.26.6, jq 1.8.2, 2026-09-09. THAT BLOCK RAN AS PRINTED ON THAT DAY, and the correction subsection at the end of this round says what it needs today, where the host's Go has moved past what TinyGo 0.41.1 accepts: it was run once more, verbatim with the three placeholders filled in, while this subsection was being corrected, and it reproduced ALL FOUR rows of the table below exactly, wasm bytes, module bytes and lines. The name and version it packages under do not reach the module: the same `notext` Go wasm packaged as `better-belt-balancer 0.3.2` and as the harness's `fkrecipes-example 0.1.0` gives two `fk_data_module.lua` that `cmp` at exit 0, 2,719,629 bytes and 68,277 lines either way. Every scratch repo directory involved is 113 characters long, the two of the original pass and the one of that re-run, and so is the one the BEFORE row was taken under, because TinyGo and rustc write the source path into the wasm and a differing length reads as a fake delta. Every figure was re-taken from a SECOND fresh `git archive` at an equally long path and came back identical; all four packaged modules are byte-identical between the two passes, and the two GO wasm files differ in content at the DWARF path while agreeing in size, which is that precaution working.

The round-1 table's two Go wasm figures are 4 bytes higher than the ones taken here (716,980 and 1,087,279 against 716,976 and 1,087,275), which is the DWARF path difference that section already flags; the wasm column above is against the same-path measurement, not against those two. Per language the line growth is the SAME on the no-text fixture as on the guest that declares text settings, and the wasm growth is the same to within two bytes: the two Go guests grew +2,762 and +2,764 bytes of wasm and +228 lines apiece, the two Rust guests +584 and +584 bytes and +174 lines apiece. That is what one unconditional string and the code that emits it looks like when every guest compiles it in, and it is why the fixtures were re-taken rather than argued about.

|---|---|---|
| `invisible character`, `is longer than`, `stands alone`, `no item or fluid is named` | 0, 0 | 1, 1 |
| `Write internal names`, `set aside`, `  type: ` | 1, 1 | 1, 1 |
| `\10default: `, `mod-setting-description` | 1, 1 | 1, 1 |
| `is also what this recipe makes`, `nothing can craft the first one` | 1, 1 | 1, 1 |
| `not in this locale file`, `no line about the format`, `[mod-setting-description] entry` | 0, 0 | 0, 0 |

Every row reads the same at 0077f3c except the self-product pair, which is 0 in all four modules there and 1 in all four here: the previous commit's new string, in both `notext` fixtures exactly as it warned. THE SEAM ITSELF HOLDS AT THIS HEAD, which is the row that matters: the language's four needles are absent from both `notext` modules and present in both `datastage` controls, so every comment that says a no-text plan links none of THE LANGUAGE is true as written and was left alone. A newline is `\10` in a packaged module rather than `\n`, which is why the default-line needle is spelled that way.

**WHAT WAS CORRECTED.** `README.md` and `docs/usage.md` lose "pays nothing for the customizer it does not use" and gain the four-part adjective, the mechanism and the 234 bytes; the README also drops the pre-round fixture figures (2,716,663 / 67,023 and 2,475,085 / 59,491, taken against an `fklua` at a1fcd04) for the row above. The Go one was 2,966 bytes UNDER today's and would have read as roughly right; the Rust one was 172,388 bytes OVER it, about 168 KiB, which is a figure a reader would have budgeted against and been wrong by more than the whole delta this round measured. THE SEAM'S OWN BEFORE AND AFTER DOES NOT GO INTO THE README. A first draft put it there as a second paragraph and it was cut: both halves of that pair are figures from another head, the README carries no current comparand to hang them on, and a public document that quotes two heads in one paragraph reads as one comparison whatever the sentence around it says. It lives in the follow-up section above, beside the commands that took it, and that section now says out loud that no column of it is a figure for the current tree. The README ends on the current-tree pair and the needle evidence. CLAUDE.md's `notext` entry gives the slogan its adjective and keeps its job, which is to say the fixtures exist so somebody can re-take the number. The round-1 paragraph loses the inverted attribution. The round-1 open item closes with the number and the mechanism. `rust/src/tests/customize.rs`'s "a link-time elimination pass has nothing to keep" is scoped to the four things elimination really drops, which is what the first paragraph of its Go twin already said. That twin never overclaimed and needed no correction, but it now carries the same caveat about the description prose, in a second paragraph at `go/customize_test.go:2644-2653`, because a reader of one half should not have to open the other to learn it.

**THE THREE COMMENTS THAT POINTED A MAINTAINER AT A STALE ORACLE.** `go/examples/notext/main.go`, `go/lib.go` and `rust/src/ingredient_list.rs` each carry the seam's before-and-after figures, and two of them told the reader to COMPARE a fresh packaging against them. Those figures were 2,716,663 / 67,023 and 2,475,085 / 59,491, which are 2,966 bytes under and 172,388 bytes over what this head packages, so following the instruction would have looked like a leak or a saving that is neither. The figures stay where they are and now carry the head and the `fklua` that produced them (c7a806e, a1fcd04) and a sentence saying they belong to that head; the ORACLE instruction points at this subsection's four-fixture table instead, and the follow-up table above carries the same pointer, so the instruction and the current numbers are one hop apart wherever a maintainer enters. `rust/src/ingredient_list.rs`'s datastage bill (3,656,235 to 3,679,378, 89,943 to 90,520 lines) was in the present tense over a `rust/examples/datastage` that is 3,340,028 bytes today; it is past tense now and says which head it is about. All three are comment-only edits.

**WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT TOUCHED.** Every comment that names THE LANGUAGE rather than the whole cost: `go/customize.go:732-733`, `rust/src/settings.rs:1062-1064`, `rust/src/ingredient_list.rs:107-109` and `:194-195`, the first paragraph of the Go seam test at `go/customize_test.go:2644-2647`, `agents/customizer-design.md:226` and `:262`, the merge-line comments in `go/data.go` and `rust/src/data.rs`, `rust/src/plan.rs:873-877` and `rust/src/tests/customize.rs:1923-1927`. The needle table above is what says they are still true. There is nothing to red-prove, because nothing that runs changed.

### The NUL, measured: a 22-byte cliff, and each of the notes that disagreed was right about its own range

**WHY IT WAS TAKEN.** Fix round 1 left this open in its own list: the design record's probe row said a NUL truncates a stored setting value inside the engine and the truncation is persisted, BetterBeltBalancer's migration assessment said finding 10's NUL survived a successful load and the engine's rewrite byte for byte and cut a refusal message in half, both said 2.0.77, and nothing had re-measured which was right. EACH NOTE WAS RIGHT ABOUT THE CASE IT RAN, and each was wrong to state it without a range. The engine's behaviour is LENGTH-DEPENDENT, and the two notes measured opposite sides of a sharp boundary at 22 bytes. What was wrong outright is the inference the design record drew on top of its row, that a NUL typed on the settings screen therefore never reaches the guest at all; that is false for every value from 23 bytes up to the 48 the sweep reached, and the short-string signature says above it too, so it is gone from both files.

**THE INSTRUMENT, AND IT WAS VERIFIED BEFORE IT WAS TRUSTED.** A Lua-only probe mod: info.json, a settings.lua declaring one `string-setting` in `startup` with `allow_blank = true`, and a data.lua logging `#v` and a byte-by-byte hex rendering built with `string.byte` rather than `gsub`, so the reading is 8-bit clean rather than something a pattern match could quietly shorten. Run under `factorio -c CFG --mod-directory MODS --dump-data` on `Version: 2.0.77 (build 84539, mac-arm64, steam)`, re-asked of the binary before the runs, with private user directories throughout. Every value was installed BY FILE with `fklua modsettings write` and read back with `fklua modsettings read` before the engine saw it, because a writer that quietly dropped the byte would have produced the same short reading as an engine that truncated it: it does carry a NUL, and the hexdump for `AB<NUL>CD` is `03 00 00 05 41 42 00 43 44`, type string, any-type flag, empty flag, length 5, then the bytes. The instrument was run FIRST on values with no NUL in them, so a short reading could not be an artefact of the probe.

**THE SIX ARMS**, exit codes read directly and never through a pipe:

| Arm | stored value | run exit | what the GUEST saw | mod-settings.dat after |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| control, no NUL | `ABXCD`, 5 bytes | 0 | `len=5 hex=4142584344` | identical, `cmp` exit 0 |
| A, success, short NUL | `AB<NUL>CD`, 5 | 0 | `len=2 hex=4142` | rewritten TRUNCATED to `AB`, `cmp` exit 1 at char 62 |
| B, refusal, short NUL | `AB<NUL>CD`, 5 | 1 | `len=2 hex=4142` | untouched, sha256 equal to the input, still carrying the NUL |
| C, refusal, NUL built INSIDE the message by `string.char(0)` | irrelevant | 1 | Lua message 37 bytes | n/a |
| D, success, long NUL | `2 iron-plate<NUL>, 3 copper-plate`, 29 | 0 | `len=29`, full hex | byte for byte identical, `cmp` exit 0 |
| E, refusal, long NUL | the same 29 bytes | 1 | `len=29`, Lua message 73 bytes | untouched |

Arm A is the design record's row, and its truncation is SILENT: no warning, no `Failed to load mod mod setting` line, nothing in the log naming the setting, and `fklua modsettings read` of the file the engine rewrote prints `"nulprobe-text": "AB"` with the length prefix moved from `05` to `02`. Arm C is the message question the round-1 paragraph called unmeasured: the mod built a 37-byte message with `string.char(0)` in the middle of it and the engine printed `Failed to load mod "nulprobe": __nulprobe__/data.lua:7: NULPROBE cut: BEFORE` and nothing more, so it reads a load-failure message as a C string. THE 37 IS THE MOD'S MESSAGE AND NOT THAT LINE: 20 bytes of it survived, the engine wrapped them in 56 bytes of its own frame, and the printed line is 76 (`printf '%s' 'Failed to load mod "nulprobe": __nulprobe__/data.lua:7: NULPROBE cut: BEFORE' | wc -c`). Arm E reproduces the assessment's finding 10 exactly: 73 bytes of Lua message cut after `entry 1 ("2 iron-plate`, no closing quote and no reason, with the mod-settings.dat untouched afterwards so the refusal repeats for ever. B and E together are why a refusal over a NUL would be a lock-out and not a diagnosis, which is finding 2's rule arriving from a second direction.

**THE BOUNDARY, SWEPT BOTH WAYS.** 47 settings in one run, each holding `"A" * (L-2) + NUL + "Z"` for L = 2 to 48, all round-trip verified through `fklua modsettings read` before the engine ran:

```
NULPROBE nulprobe-L21 want=21 got=19
NULPROBE nulprobe-L22 want=22 got=20
NULPROBE nulprobe-L23 want=23 got=23
NULPROBE nulprobe-L24 want=24 got=24
```

L02 through L22 give `got = L - 2`, cut at the NUL; L23 through L48 give `got = L`. The engine's rewrite agrees with the guest per key: written length equals rewritten length for every L from 23 to 48 and for none from 2 to 22. AND THE AXIS IS TOTAL LENGTH, NOT NUL POSITION, which is the finding the second sweep buys: pinning the NUL at index 3 and varying only the total (`"AB" + NUL + "C" * (L-3)`, L = 4 to 30) gives `got=2` for every P04 through P22 and `got=L` for every P23 through P30, the same 22/23 cliff with the byte in the same place. That is the signature of a short-string buffer being read as a C string rather than of anything about NUL handling: on 64-bit libc++ a `std::string` holds up to 22 bytes inline, and the inline case is the one that loses the tail. THAT FIGURE IS RECALLED, NOT DERIVED HERE, and it is the only number in this subsection with no command behind it: it is offered as the shape the measurements fit, and nothing in the finding rests on it.

**WHAT IT MEANS FOR THIS LIBRARY'S OWN MESSAGES**, and the accounting has to be PER PATH rather than per side of the cliff, because finding 2 moved the player's own text off the load-failure path and the three texts that can carry a stored NUL now leave by three different doors. THE PLAYER'S TEXT IS A LOG LINE. A list the language refuses and a stored value that is not text are both FALLBACKS now, routed through `noteFallback` into `res.logs` (`go/customize.go:1113` and `:1123`), and `PlanData` turns logs into `OpLog` only after every check has passed, so on a refused load they never reach the host at all. What Factorio's `log()` does with a NUL is NOT MEASURED, in either direction, and this round did not ask it. THE AUTHOR'S DECLARED LIST IS A LOAD-FAILURE MESSAGE, AND IT IS ESCAPED. `validateTextSettings` re-renders and re-parses every declared default at plan time (`go/customize.go:556-558`), both planners run it in front of the walk (`go/data.go:119`, `go/settings.go:48`; Rust `data.rs:163`, `settings.rs:49`), and its refusal leaves through `Raise` / `fkdata::raise`. THAT is the path `quotable()` genuinely buys, and arm C is why it is a property rather than a convenience: `quotable()` and its Rust twin write every invisible-set member as its `U+XXXX` token before the message is composed, both halves fold `U+0000` into that set (Go `r >= 0 && r <= 0x1f`, Rust `0x0000..=0x001f`), and the whitespace set that is asked first does not contain it, so a NUL cannot take the word-separating path instead; `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` pins three shapes, a NUL at the end of an entry as the first range's boundary member (line 456), a lone NUL (583) and one inside a name (586), and both suites run them byte for byte. THE STORED DROPDOWN VALUE IS A LOAD-FAILURE MESSAGE AND IS NOT ESCAPED, which is this round's counterexample to the sentence it was writing: `readDropdown` (`go/data.go:1575`) and `read_dropdown` (`rust/src/data.rs:2316-2319`) interpolate the value raw into `holds "..."`, and the chain reaches the engine (`r.refuse` -> `res.refusal` -> `go/data.go:58` -> `go/guest.go:89` `fkdata.Raise`; `self.refuse` -> `plan_data` -> `rust/src/emit.rs:105` `fkdata::raise`). It is an open item below, with the measured reason it was not closed here. NOTHING IN THE LIBRARY CHANGED FOR THIS: the measurement confirms the rule the language already had, and names the one producer that never had it.

**AND WHAT IT COSTS THE PLAYER BELOW THE CLIFF**, which is the finding this measurement actually turned up and is not about this library's messages at all. Below 23 bytes the engine DELETES everything from the NUL onward: the guest is handed only what precedes the byte, which parses without a word whenever that shorter text is still a legal list, and nothing warns and no line in the log names the setting (arm A); then the engine writes the truncation back into `mod-settings.dat`, so the deletion is PERMANENT. THIS LIBRARY'S OWN INVISIBLE-CHARACTER POLICY IS "NOTHING IS DELETED FROM THE TEXT" (the design record's row), and the round that set it recorded the reason as "a deletion answered a text nobody typed". The engine demonstrably does that deletion, one layer below where the policy can reach, and the record used to call both sides of the cliff safe. AND THE PREMISE THAT MADE IT LOOK RARE WAS WRONG: this file and the design record both said 23 bytes is where a list a player writes sits, and this repository's own `testdata/ingame/flipped.json` stores `1 steel-plate, 10 water` at exactly 23, ON the cliff, and `2 iron-stick, 1 steel-plate` at 27, while `iron-plate` alone is a legal list at 10 (all three `printf '%s' '...' | wc -c`). Short lists are ordinary, so the deletion's range is ordinary. It is an open item below, and it may not be closable here at all.

**THE CONFIRMING RUN, and it agrees.** The headline arm was re-taken by a second pass, independently staged, so the record does not rest on one run of one instrument: a freshly written probe mod under a fresh private user directory, the binary re-asked its version first, arm D's value and no other.

```sh
SCRATCH=$(mktemp -d)                                    # the whole pass lives here and nowhere else
MODS=$SCRATCH/mods
CFG=$SCRATCH/userdir/config/config.ini                  # [path] read-data=__PATH__system-read-data__,
mkdir -p "$MODS/nulconfirm" "$SCRATCH/userdir/config"   # write-data=$SCRATCH/userdir, so nothing touches
                                                        # the real user directory
# $MODS/mod-list.json enables base, elevated-rails, quality, space-age and
# nulconfirm, which is the mod set every row in this file names.

# $MODS/nulconfirm/settings.lua declares ONE startup string-setting,
# nulconfirm-list, default_value = "AAA", allow_blank = true. Its data.lua is
# four lines and the last one is the whole reading:
#
#   local v = settings.startup["nulconfirm-list"].value
#   local hex = {}
#   for i = 1, #v do hex[i] = string.format("%02x", v:byte(i)) end
#   log("NULCONFIRM len=" .. #v .. " hex=" .. table.concat(hex))
#
# long.json is the document `fklua modsettings write --from` takes, which is
# the same shape `fklua modsettings read` prints:
#
#   {"version": [2, 0, 77, 0],
#    "startup": {"nulconfirm-list": "2 iron-plate\u0000, 3 copper-plate"},
#    "runtime-global": {}, "runtime-per-user": {}}

"$FACTORIO" --version                                   # Version: 2.0.77 (build 84539, mac-arm64, steam)
fklua modsettings write --from long.json --out long.dat # the JSON escapes the byte as \u0000
xxd long.dat                                            # ... 03 00 00 1d 32 20 69 72 6f 6e 2d 70 6c 61 74 65 00 2c 20 33 ...
fklua modsettings read long.dat                         # the NUL is in the file BEFORE the engine sees it
cp long.dat "$MODS/mod-settings.dat"
"$FACTORIO" -c "$CFG" --mod-directory "$MODS" --dump-data
cmp long.dat "$MODS/mod-settings.dat"
```

Every exit code 0, including the engine run and the `cmp` after it. The guest logged `NULCONFIRM len=29 hex=322069726f6e2d706c617465002c203320636f707065722d706c617465`, all 29 bytes with the `00` at offset 12, and `fklua modsettings read` of the file the engine rewrote returns the value it was given. A control at the same 29 bytes with an `X` where the NUL is (`len=29 hex=322069726f6e2d706c617465582c203320636f707065722d706c617465`, `58` where the `00` is, `cmp` exit 0) verified the instrument before the NUL arm ran, exactly as the first pass did. The write-data directory was private to the pass and the real user directory was never named.

**WHAT MOVED.** Prose in two files, one comment in each half, and one comment line in the corpus. `agents/customizer-design.md` loses the inference and its probe row now says the measured answer and carries the command that produced it; this file's finding-10 paragraph says which note is true of which range and why the two runs differed, its round-1 open item closes naming what closed it, the correction against `3a9de88` points at this subsection instead of at an open question, and the customizer round's one-line summary of the same fact gains the range it holds over. The comment above `quotable()` in `go/ingredientlist.go` and `rust/src/ingredient_list.rs` carried the same inference and the same "INFERRED, and asserted nowhere" about the message; both now carry the measurement, and both say that "here" is the file in Go and the module in Rust, because one producer outside it does not escape. In `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` the comment above the NUL cases said a NUL "would truncate the message inside the engine", which is now true of the wrong path: those particular refusals are log lines, so that comment now says what is measured and what is not. NO CASE, `in:`, `ok:` OR `refuse:` LINE MOVED, the file stands at 242 `in:` lines as before, no golden moved, and no behaviour changed: every other edit is inside a comment block, so there is nothing to red-prove.

**Gates.** Every gate in CLAUDE.md's Gates block that this round is asked to run, exit codes read directly and never through a pipe, all 0: `gofmt -l .` (no output), `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./...`, `go/examples/notext` `go vet .`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo test`, `RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --workspace`. `scripts/run-mirror.sh` and `scripts/run-ingame.sh` are not this round's to run, and nothing they read moved. NO COUNT MOVED, which is the point: the Go suite stands at 221 `func Test` and the Rust lib target at 203 `#[test]` beside the 2 in the public-surface witness, and `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` at 242 `in:` lines, every one of them the figure the previous subsection recorded, because the only thing that changed anywhere is comment and prose. The repository-wide dash sweep (`perl -CSD -ne 'print if /[\x{2013}\x{2014}]/'` over `git ls-files`) returns the same three lines it returns at the previous head, all of them `agents/docs-style.md` quoting the characters it bans.

### The report to the pilot was true and the pilot's suite still went red, so migration.md owes a consumer the difference

**BOTH SENTENCES THE ROUND-1 REPORT SENT THE PILOT ARE TRUE, AND THE PILOT'S GATES STILL WENT RED.** "Nothing in BBB is required for the library changes to take effect" and "BBB's declarations compile unchanged" both hold: the pilot's `make mod` is exit 0 on the untouched tree against the shipped library. What the report did not say is that COMPILING IS NOT THE GATE A CONSUMER HAS. `make check` came out at **exit 2 with five failing tests**, all in one package, and `make datastage-check` at **exit 2** as well. Every figure in this paragraph is the pilot's, out of `agents/fkrecipes-migration.md` in the BetterBeltBalancer checkout; none of it was re-taken here, and this repository ran no gate of that mod's.

The five, and which library behaviour each one had pinned:

| the pilot's test | what moved under it |
|---|---|
| `TestEverySettingPrototypeIsTheOneThatShipped` | the composed description |
| `TestTheCustomResearchDefaultsAreTheFallbackUnit` | the composed description, through a different predicate |
| `TestEveryOptionFallsAllTheWayToIronPlate` | the ladder merge; renamed to `TestEveryOptionCollapsesOntoIronPlateAndMergesTheAmounts` |
| `TestACustomTextTheGameCannotAnswerIsRefused` | the player-text fallback; renamed to `...FallsBackAndSaysSo` |
| `TestAPackTextTheGameCannotAnswerIsRefused` | the player-text fallback; renamed to `...FallsBackAndSaysSo` |

**TWO OF THE FIVE ARE NAMED FOR THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT THE LIBRARY DOES, AND A THIRD FOR HALF OF IT**, which is the shape of the cost a consumer actually pays: a suite pins MESSAGES and BEHAVIOUR, and a name is an assertion too. The two are the pilot's own reading, that the two `...IsRefused` names assert the opposite of what the library now does. The third is weaker and the difference is worth keeping: every option does still fall all the way to `iron-plate`, so `TestEveryOptionFallsAllTheWayToIronPlate` was never false, only silent about the merge that now happens on the way, and its rename ADDS the arithmetic rather than reversing the claim. The engine half says the same thing from the other side: both of the pilot's engine arms moved their `mod_settings_sha256` IDENTICALLY (`df4d6c3fc7bf854d` to `aa75497e55ce4333`) while both `data_raw_sha256` are unmoved to the digit, which is the composed description seen through a dump rather than through a transcription.

**WHAT LANDED IS ONE SECTION IN `docs/migration.md`, "Upgrading a mod that has already adopted the library".** It leads with what compiles not being what passes, lists FIVE BEHAVIOURS as one matchable line each (the player-text fallback, the ladder merge, the invisible-character set, the reserved-word fold, the composed description with its two hash directions), adds this round's own newer line (a resolved list naming the recipe's own product), and closes with what a consumer should DO: the suite is the gate, read the log stream and not only the prototypes, and re-take every hash over a settings dump. THOSE FIVE BEHAVIOURS ARE NOT THE FIVE TESTS ABOVE and do not map onto them one for one: three of the five behaviours produced the pilot's five tests and the other two broke nothing of that mod's, which is the ordinary case for a consumer whose plan does not reach a behaviour. It CROSS-REFERENCES rather than repeats: all five behaviours are already documented for an author in `docs/usage.md` and `docs/ingredient-list.md`, and the section links those instead of restating them. It names no consumer mod, carries no round codes and no commit hashes, and states current behaviour rather than a change history, because `agents/docs-style.md` governs that file. The document's opening paragraph gains one sentence pointing at it.

### The tooltip's ceiling was never the line count, and the widest line is the consumer's own

**THE RECORD FRAMED THE OPEN QUESTION AS WHETHER A CLIENT CAN RENDER 13 LINES, AND THE FRAMING WAS WRONG.** The pilot re-measured its own worst composed tooltip and the numbers say the axis is width and not height. `bbb-recipe-cost` is **13 lines**, exact rather than approximate because the composition holds twelve `"\n"`, and it spends **7 of 20 localised parameters** at **depth 3 of 20**: both ceilings that exist are measured, both are far away, and NEITHER OF THEM IS A LINE COUNT. Growth costs one top-level parameter per preset and no depth at all, so that mod would need 13 more recipe presets before its top level filled, and even THAT is a fill rather than a wall: `localisedGroup` / `localised_group` nests the overflow into one more table, so the 21st preset costs a level of depth instead of a refused load. The whole tooltip is **1,163 characters** over those 13 lines, and the **widest line is 557 characters and is LINE 1, the consumer's own `[mod-setting-description]` entry** rather than anything this library composes; the twelve lines the composition puts below it run **32 to 66 characters each**, and SIX OF THOSE TWELVE ARE THE CONSUMER'S OWN PRESET LABELS: a preset is a label line carrying the consumer's `[string-mod-setting]` entry and then the library's indented `type:` rendering, so six of the twelve short lines are this library's and six are that mod's. Its twin `bbb-tech-cost` is 4 lines with a **622-character** first line, the tallest single wrap that mod can produce. Every figure here is the pilot's, out of `agents/fkrecipes-migration.md` in the BetterBeltBalancer checkout, taken from the engine's own settings dump with that mod's locale substituted, and no client was run here. THE FIGURES THE WRAP QUESTION RESTS ON WERE RE-TAKEN HERE, off that mod's own locale file at 894220b, because a figure this file leans on should not be a transcription: `grep '^bbb-recipe-cost' mod-data/locale/en/better-belt-balancer.cfg | perl -CSD -ne 'chomp; /^([^=]+)=(.*)$/ and printf "%s %d\n", $1, length($2)'` prints the key twice, 20 for the `[string-mod-setting]` label and **557** for the `[mod-setting-description]` entry that is line 1, then the six preset labels at 50, 38, 58, 65, 35 and 52, which is **35 to 65**. THE BAND IS THE LIBRARY'S AND THE CONSUMER'S LINES SIT INSIDE IT, which is the opposite of what a first reading of "32 to 66" suggests: the six `type:` lines are `"  type: "` in front of each preset's rendering, which the pilot transcribes rung by rung in its own `plan_test.go`, and they measure 57, 38, 62, 66, 32 and 41, so both ends of the band are this library's own lines. The three figures multiply out to the pilot's total as well: 557 for line 1, 594 for the twelve, and the twelve `"\n"` themselves, which is **1,163** and is that number re-derived here rather than transcribed. The same command over `'^bbb-tech-cost='` gives **622**. The `custom` value's label is 52 characters and appears in no tooltip at all, because the composition writes a line per PRESET and none for the custom arm. The parameter and depth counts were checked against this library's own composition rather than re-taken from the pilot: `settingDescriptions` builds a dropdown's description as one group of the consumer's `[mod-setting-description]` reference plus one `presetLine` per choice, so six presets is 7 top-level parameters, and a `presetLine` is a table holding a `localeRef` table, which is depth 3.

**SO ONE ITEM IS REFRAMED AND A DIFFERENT ONE OPENS, and REFRAMED is the verb throughout rather than ANSWERED.** No client has still seen 13 lines and this changes nothing about that; what the measurement above retires is the claim that the height is the thing at risk, because the height was never what the shape rests on. What opens in its place is **how a long composed line WRAPS**, and whether the roughly 37-character truncation of a CLOSED DROPDOWN'S LABEL hides the half a player can actually type. It is stated as a question a client run answers with two hovers, which are the pilot's own: `bbb-recipe-cost`, where all six `type:` lines must be present and the last of them, `  type: 1 express-splitter, 2 steel-plate`, visible and neither clipped nor scrolled off; and `bbb-tech-cost`, whose 622-character first line is the single tallest wrap available.

**THE 37 CHARACTERS GET MORE LOAD-BEARING RATHER THAN LESS**, which is the reason nothing that cites them moved. The truncation is cited in TWELVE places, counted rather than estimated (`go/customize.go`, `rust/src/settings.rs`, `go/customize_test.go`, `rust/src/tests/customize.rs`, `scripts/run-mirror.sh`, `scripts/run-ingame.sh`, `docs/usage.md`, `agents/customizer-design.md` and this file in four, one of which this change added), and every one of them stays true as written; the reframed sentences were checked against them and agree, because the label the widget truncates is the CONSUMER'S string and the 557-character line 1 is that same consumer's string one layer up. The seven-line measurement stays too, as HISTORY: it is the record of what one screen showed under the one-line preset shape, and what it stopped being is the live claim.

**WHAT MOVED.** Prose in two files and one comment in each half, and NOT ONE LINE OF CODE. `agents/customizer-design.md`'s claims paragraph carries the re-measurement and the two hovers, and its earlier clause says the seven is a record of one screen rather than a ceiling; its client-run section keeps the seven and says which shape it belongs to. This file's finding-5/6/9 paragraph, the pilot's to-do item and round 1's open bullet each say what the ceilings are and what is open in place of the height. `go/customize.go`'s `textDescription` comment and `rust/src/settings.rs`'s `text_description` twin keep the seven-line measurement, say which shape it was taken on (a DROPDOWN's description, one line per preset, which is not the shape that function composes at all), and say that the ceilings are the parameter count `maxLocalisedParams` (Rust `MAX_LOCALISED_PARAMS`) holds and the nesting depth its own comment records, and that neither is a line count. The two comments remain TWINS, word-diffed: the only differences are the constant's spelling and the backticks the Rust half already had around `default`. No `testdata/` file moved, no golden moved, no count moved, and there is nothing here to red-prove because nothing here executes.

### The round's own summary line was false where its table was right, and the four fixtures were re-taken to settle it

**WHAT WAS FALSE, AND IT WAS THIS FILE'S OWN.** Round 1's open list summarised the refused alternative as "it shrinks the wasm and grows the packaged Lua a player downloads, in all four guests". The table it summarises says otherwise in half its rows: Option A shrinks the wasm on the two `notext` fixtures (716,976 to 715,680 and 149,774 to 149,501, so -1,296 and -273) and GROWS it on the two guests that DO declare text settings (1,087,275 to 1,088,130 and 244,345 to 244,473, so +855 and +128); only the Lua growth is true of all four (+657, +82, +2,013, +1,315).
