Every one of those six deltas is that table's own two columns subtracted, so the summary never needed a rerun to be caught, only reading. The bullet carries the six now and says what it used to say, and the subsection's own "the wasm SHRINKS by 1,296 and 273 bytes" gained the word THERE and the other direction beside it.

**THE FOUR FIXTURES WERE RE-TAKEN AT THIS HEAD**, with the block printed above and ONE change to it, which is a remedy a later maintainer needs: the host's Go has moved to go1.27.1 since 2026-09-09 and TinyGo 0.41.1 refuses it outright (`requires go version 1.19 through 1.26, got go1.27`, the build's own stderr, exit 1), so the block runs under `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.6`, which is the version the original pass used and which the Go command downloads on demand. Same 113-character scratch path, cargo 1.97.1, TinyGo 0.41.1, jq 1.8.2, 2026-09-13:

| guest | wasm | `fk_data_module.lua` | lines | against the four-fixture table at `1f8e363` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `go/examples/notext` | 720,902 | 2,719,629 | 68,277 | module and lines EXACT; wasm +1,164 |
| `rust/examples/notext` | 150,358 | 2,302,697 | 55,946 | EXACT, all three |
| `go/examples/datastage` | 1,091,504 | 3,866,422 | 101,444 | module and lines EXACT; wasm +1,465 |
| `rust/examples/datastage` | 244,929 | 3,340,019 | 83,446 | wasm size and lines EXACT; module -9 |

**BOTH DEVIATIONS WERE CHASED TO A CAUSE RATHER THAN ACCEPTED.** The two Go wasm figures are the DWARF path again: a `GOTOOLCHAIN`-downloaded toolchain roots under the module cache rather than under the Homebrew cellar, TinyGo writes that root into the wasm, and the PACKAGED MODULE is byte-identical in both Go rows anyway, which is the artefact a player downloads and the one the table exists for. The Rust one is not environmental and is the more interesting of the two: this head's `datastage` wasm is the SAME SIZE as `1f8e363`'s and differs from it in 24 bytes (`cmp -l`), and `1f8e363` rebuilt at a SECOND 113-character path is byte-identical to the original pass's, so the difference is the SOURCE and not the path. The two commits between are comment-only, and the differing bytes say which comments: two 16-bit values read 1277 and 1279 there and 1289 and 1291 here, +12 apiece, and `git diff --numstat 1f8e363..HEAD -- rust/src/ingredient_list.rs` is `39 27`, net +12 lines. A COMMENT-ONLY RUST COMMIT MOVES THE PACKAGED MODULE'S BYTES, through the source line numbers the compiler embeds, and 24 differing wasm bytes come out as a 9-byte NET difference in the module, which is the Lua encoder's escaping and not a conversion rate. The Go half does not move that way, measured: both Go modules are identical across the same two commits. Nothing a consumer OBSERVES moves either way, because no golden in this repository is taken over a module's bytes: the mirror pins a transcript and the engine gate pins dump hashes.

**THIS COMMIT'S OWN TREE WAS MEASURED TOO, because the paragraph above predicts that a comment-only change CAN move a Rust guest's module.** It did not move this one. The four fixtures built from the working tree this commit leaves behind (tracked files only, the same 113-character path, the same commands) give the four wasm sizes, module sizes and line counts of the table above to the byte, and all four packaged `fk_data_module.lua` are byte-identical to the HEAD build (`cmp`, exit 0, four times). Whatever source locations the two Rust guests embed, none of them is one this commit's comments moved, so the table is current for the tree it leaves behind rather than stale on arrival.

**WHAT ELSE WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN TAKEN ON TRUST.** The tooltip subsection above now carries its own re-take of the pilot's 557, 622 and the six preset labels, and the parameter and depth counts read off this library's own composition. THREE OF THOSE CHECKS CAME OUT OF THE ADVERSARIAL REVIEW OF THIS COMMIT'S OWN DIFF and each changed a sentence: the pilot's reading is that TWO of its five test names assert the opposite of the library and a third is merely incomplete, where this file had said three; the 32-to-66 band is the LIBRARY'S six lines with the consumer's six inside it, where a first draft of the re-take said the consumer's were the short ones; and "about a dozen" citations of the 37 characters is twelve, one of them added by this round. The "cited in about a dozen places" claim was counted rather than estimated and says TWELVE now: `go/customize.go`, `rust/src/settings.rs`, `go/customize_test.go`, `rust/src/tests/customize.rs`, `scripts/run-mirror.sh`, `scripts/run-ingame.sh`, `docs/usage.md`, `agents/customizer-design.md` and this file in four places, one of which this round's own reframing added. Every one of them is about the LABEL in the closed widget, which is what the reframing leaves alone.

**GATES, on the tree this commit leaves behind.** Every gate in CLAUDE.md's Gates block, exit codes read directly and never through a pipe, all 0: `gofmt -l .` (no output), `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./...`, `go/examples/notext` `go vet .`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo test` (203 in the lib target and 2 in the public-surface witness), `RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --workspace`, `scripts/run-mirror.sh` (`run-mirror: OK`, both jump rows the ones recorded above) and `scripts/run-ingame.sh` (`run-ingame: OK` on 2.0.77, both dump rows matching `testdata/ingame/dump-sha256.txt`, the two languages agreeing on both, and the engine's own rewrite carrying every flipped value back). NO GOLDEN MOVED and nothing under `testdata/` is dirty, which is the only outcome a commit that changes no behaviour may have. THE TWO WASM GATES NEEDED ONE THING THE BLOCK DOES NOT SAY, and it is the same remedy the fixture re-take needed: the host's Go is go1.27.1, TinyGo 0.41.1 refuses it, and both scripts were run under `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.6`. Without it the mirror stops at the Go guest's build with the version in the message, which is a loud failure rather than a silent skip, so no rule of this repository is bent by leaving the scripts alone.

### What this round leaves open on this side

- **The two lengths are not tied together.** `PlanData` / `plan_data` indexes `res.recipes[i]` against `l.recipes` and nothing makes the two the same length. A SHORTFALL panics, loudly and at a known site. An OVERSHOOT, an arm calling the hand-over twice for one recipe, would be SILENT: every later recipe would be emitted with the previous one's ingredient list and the last list would be dropped on the floor. It is pre-existing and unreachable today, because every arm hands over exactly once and `continue`s. This round created the one place it could have been made unrepresentable, `addRecipe` / `add_recipe` being the single door, and did not take it: a hand-over that took the recipe's index, or a resolution that carried the two lists as one, would make an overshoot a compile error or a refusal rather than a silent shift. Deliberately not fixed here.
- **The Rust source property has one hole, named above**: `res = Resolution { recipes: lists, ..Default::default() }` leaves it green, where the Go twin's composite-literal rule catches the equivalent. Closing it needs a needle with real occurrences today, so it is a false-positive trade rather than an oversight.
- **The `notext` size numbers in this file were stale** from the self-product commit onward, for the reason given in its own subsection. CLOSED by the adjective commit above, which re-took all four fixtures at 1f8e363 from a `git archive` at a matched path length, twice: the two fixtures grew 9,432 and 8,973 bytes of packaged Lua and the two guests that declare text settings grew 8,770 and 9,248, with the SAME +228 and +174 lines per language across both shapes, which is what one unconditional string compiled into every guest looks like. THE FOUR FIXTURE FIGURES ARE CURRENT AS OF THAT COMMIT, and that is the whole of the claim: nothing else in this file was re-taken with them. The round-1 jump row is a 0077f3c pair and now says so beside the current one, the follow-up section's table is a c7a806e pair and now says so, and every other figure here keeps the head it names.
- **Option A's patch is in a scratch tree and nowhere else.** Moving the three description prose constants behind the seam was implemented, gated and measured, and refused on cost; only its numbers are in this file. Anyone who wants the code back rebuilds it, and the table above says what it would buy and what it would cost before they start.
- **One load-failure message still interpolates a stored value UNESCAPED**, which is the counterexample to the escaping property the NUL subsection above states, found while writing it. `readDropdown` (`go/data.go:1575`) and `read_dropdown` (`rust/src/data.rs:2316-2319`) build `<setting> holds "<value>", which is not one of its values` by putting the stored value in raw, and the chain reaches the engine: Go `r.refuse` -> `res.refusal` -> `go/data.go:58` -> `go/guest.go:89` `fkdata.Raise(err.Error())`, Rust `self.refuse` -> `plan_data` -> `rust/src/emit.rs:105` `fkdata::raise`. NOT FIXED, and the reason is MEASURED RATHER THAN AESTHETIC: `quotable` lives in `ingredientlist.go` / `ingredient_list.rs`, behind the language seam, and calling it from the data planner would name the ingredient language from a file outside that seam and link the parser and the renderer into every consumer that declares no text setting. `go/source_test.go`'s `TestOnlyTheLanguageFilesNameTheLanguage` guards EVERY top-level function the language file declares and refuses a name written anywhere else, so the call would need a new row in an allowance list whose stated property is that it can only shrink. Rust needs a step more: `quotable` is private, so it would first have to become `pub(crate)`, which is exactly the widening `nothing_reachable_in_the_language_module_is_unguarded` exists to catch (whatever faces outward of that module is a name the seam table guards, and everything else stays private), and it would then need a `SeamGuard` row naming `data.rs`. It is the same trade the merge lines already took, where a fluid line names the ladder's first rung rather than call `formatListAmount`. THE PATH IN IS NARROW: the engine resets an out-of-list stored dropdown value to the default before any stage runs (measured), so the only way to reach the sentence is a hand-edited `mod-settings.dat`, which is exactly what `fklua modsettings write` produces. THE COST WHEN IT IS REACHED, given the subsection above: the message is cut at the byte, on a load that REFUSES and therefore does not rewrite the file (arm B), so the same half sentence comes back on every load until somebody edits the file. Closing it wants a NUL-only escaper that lives outside the seam, or a refusal that names the setting instead of quoting the value.
- **Below the cliff the engine silently deletes part of the player's text and persists it, and nothing here can see it happen.** Measured above: a stored value of 22 bytes or fewer loses everything from its NUL onward before the guest is handed it, with no warning and no line naming the setting, and a load that completes writes the truncation back into `mod-settings.dat`. This library's own headline on invisible characters is that NOTHING IS DELETED FROM THE TEXT, and the round that set it refused a strip because "a deletion answered a text nobody typed"; the engine does the deletion anyway, one layer down. THE REASON IT MAY NOT BE CLOSABLE AT ALL: a value the engine cut is indistinguishable from one the player typed, an ordinary shorter list with no marker of any kind, so there is no state a check inside the guest could compare against and no refusal that would not fire on honest text. What a later round could still do is smaller than a fix: say it in the composed description of every text setting, so a player who pastes and loses characters has at least been told; and report it upstream, since the shape of it, a cliff exactly at the inline capacity of a 64-bit libc++ `std::string`, reads as a buffer handed to a C-string API rather than as a decision.
- **NOTHING IN THIS REPOSITORY'S GATES SEES WHAT A CONSUMER'S SUITE SEES**, which is the general shape behind the pilot's five red tests. THREE of this round's four behavioural changes produced those five, and the fourth, the invisible-character and reserved-word policy, broke nothing of that mod's, which is worth saying beside the shape: a consumer pays for the changes its own plan reaches. Every gate here ended green on each of the changes that broke them, re-recording its own golden wherever the emission moved, and correctly so: the mirror and the in-game goldens pin what THIS repository emits, and a consumer's own transcriptions of a setting prototype, its own hashes and its own test NAMES are a surface no gate here can reach. What answers it now is a document and nothing mechanical: `docs/migration.md`'s upgrade section carries the list by hand, so the next behavioural change owes it a line and no gate fails if that line is missing. Whether a machine-checkable version of that list is worth having (a pinned transcript of the composed shapes, say, that a consumer could diff against) is a question for a later round.
- **THE WRAP AND THE LABEL TRUNCATION ARE UNANSWERED, and they are what replaced the height question.** No client is reachable from this repository, so the two hovers stay owed: `bbb-recipe-cost`'s six `type:` lines all present with the last unclipped, and `bbb-tech-cost`'s 622-character first line, which is the tallest single wrap the pilot can produce. The library cannot shorten either of the two strings the question is about: line 1 of every composed tooltip is the consumer's own `[mod-setting-description]` entry, and the truncated label is the consumer's `[string-mod-setting]` entry. IT DOES NOT FOLLOW THAT THE ANSWER IS ADVICE TO AUTHORS, and an earlier draft of this bullet said it did. By this round's own figures the library owns 6 of those 13 lines and 308 of the 1,163 characters (the six `type:` renderings and the twelve newlines), so shortening or dropping what it composes is available to it; which of the two answers a client run would call for is unmeasured, and this bullet's job is to say the question is open rather than to pre-decide it.

## Fix round 2

The trigger is the consumer's SECOND migration assessment, BetterBeltBalancer's `agents/migration-assessment-2.md` at that repository's commit `1caaf08`, 2026-09-13. Round 1 answered what the pilot found while porting; this one answers what it found by measuring a release against the release before it, which is a question no gate in this repository had ever asked. Each decision lands as its own commit and appends its own subsection here.

### Decision A: the text is the switch

**WHAT CHANGED.** The state "the player's own list is in force" moved out of a dropdown value and into the text setting itself. A recipe's `IngredientsFrom` applies whenever its stored text is not the reserved word `default` (case-folded and trimmed, as the language already folds it), and the `IngredientsBy` dropdown beside it decides whenever it is. A technology's `CostFrom` is the same rule per FIELD: each of the pack text, the count and the seconds that is not at its declared default overrides the `CostBy` tier, and each that is comes from that tier. `IngredientsFrom` may now be declared beside `IngredientsBy`, and `CostFrom` beside `CostBy`; `Ingredients` and `Unit` stay exclusive with both.

**WHY, MEASURED.** Finding 3 of the consumer's assessment, on Factorio 2.0.77: a release that declares a dropdown RESETS a stored value its `allowed_values` does not list and PERSISTS the reset, with no line in the log, while every setting a release does NOT declare survives verbatim. A `custom` dropdown value is therefore destroyed by one launch of an older release, on any rollback, in a modpack pinned to an older version, or on a second machine on the last public release, and destroyed silently: the typed text survives, because it belongs to a setting the old release does not declare, so the field still shows the player's recipe while the dropdown has gone back to a preset. Keeping the state in the text instead makes adopting the customizer on a dropdown a mod already ships an IDENTITY: the option list does not change, no stored choice is reset or changes meaning, a rollback loses nothing and a return restores everything. That is docs/migration.md's new headline for the step.

**THE SURFACE REMOVED.** Go: `IngredientChoices.Custom`, `IngredientChoices.CustomValue`, `CostChoices.Custom`, `CostChoices.CustomValue`, `CustomCost.Position`, `defaultCustomValue`, both `customValue()` methods, `coversIngredientValue`, `coversCostValue`, `countValue`, `withoutValue`, `customArmValues`, `customPrereqs`, `noteIgnoredText`, `noteIgnoredNumber`. Rust: the same list under its own names, plus `presets_of`, `custom_arm_for`, `CustomArm`, `Presets`'s old shape, `ingredients_arm`, `cost_arm` (their surviving refusals folded back into `validate_bindings`), and `is_edited` with its row in the language seam table, which had no caller left. `CustomCost.Seconds` changed type from `DoubleSettingRef` to `IntSettingRef`, which also makes the old "one double backs a recipe's crafting time and a research time at once" case UNREPRESENTABLE: no handle fits both slots any more, and the test that held it says so.

**THE SURFACE ADDED.** A `relativeOrder` / `relative_order` helper per half, comparing the two emitted `order` strings the settings stage already computes and answering `above` or `below`, so a composed sentence about "the option chosen above" is true whatever order the author declared. `costTier` / `CostTier` carries the tier's own unit, its dropdown's emitted name and the chosen value into the custom-cost resolver, which now answers whether the custom cost applies at all rather than always building one. `tierNumber`, `tierPackEntries`, `setUnitField`, `withoutUnitField` and `hasUnitField` (Rust: `tier_number`, `tier_pack_list`, `set_unit_field`, `without_unit_field`, `has_unit_field`) read and overwrite the tier's unit in place, so a `count_formula`, a `max_level` and any field this library has never heard of survive a player who moved one slider. `deferrableCountFault` / `deferrableSecondsFault` are the two fault rules with 0 let through, which is what 0 means beside a tier.

**THE EXACT NEW TEXTS.** Two clauses on the lines that record what was read, and they are two sentences because they say different things: the ingredient override is total and the cost override is per field.

```
fkrecipes: <recipe> takes its ingredients from <setting>: <canonical>; the <dropdown> choice <chosen> is set aside
fkrecipes: <tech> takes its research cost from <setting>: count <n>, time <s>, packs <canonical>; the <dropdown> choice <chosen> supplies what the settings leave at default
fkrecipes: <tech>: <countSetting> replaces the count_formula the <chosen> cost carries
```

Three new refusals, and one existing one reworded because the setting is an int now:

```
fkrecipes: the setting <name> backs a research count beside a research dropdown, so its declared default and its minimum must both be 0 (0 means the dropdown decides)
fkrecipes: the setting <name> backs a research time beside a research dropdown, so its declared default and its minimum must both be 0 (0 means the dropdown decides)
fkrecipes: the setting <name> backs a research number but declares no maximum of at least 1; a number the player types needs a ceiling it can reach
fkrecipes: the setting <name> backs a research time but declares no minimum of at least 1 (the engine refuses a unit time of 0)
```

Two refusals kept their shape and lost the words `Custom arm`, because there is no arm any more: `the setting <full> takes a text setting from more than one recipe; one dropdown composes one description` and its technology twin. One locale finding is new:

```
the setting <full> has no [mod-setting-description] entry, and a research number needs one to say what the number is for; the library composes the range onto it
```

And four composed lines, which are the only place the settings screen can say which of two fields is deciding, because it has no conditional visibility at all (measured on 2.0.77 and recorded in agents/customizer-design.md's table):

```
\nWhile this says default the option chosen <above|below> applies; anything else applies instead of it.
\nWhile this says default this mod's own list applies.
\nThe setting <above|below> applies instead while it does not say default.
\nA whole number from 0 to <max>. While it is 0 the option chosen <above|below> decides.
\nA whole number from <min> to <max>.
```

A text setting's description is five parameters now rather than four, and the two research numbers gain a description where they had none. The numbers in the range line come out of the language's own amount formatter, which the corpus pins byte for byte in both halves, rather than out of either language's float formatting.

**THE DELETIONS.** `... is edited, but ... is not on custom, so the text is ignored` and its `number` twin are gone with their producers and their call sites. Nothing replaces them: every non-default value is live now, so there is nothing left for such a line to be about.

**THE RED PROOFS.** Each break was made in the source, the designed failure observed, and the break reverted.

- *The text winning over the dropdown.* Broke the precedence in `go/data.go` so the dropdown wins even with a text in force (`if false && usedText`). `TestTextBesideADropdownTakesTheListOver` went red on the missing line and on the recipe coming out of the `oil` preset instead of the typed list, and `TestARecipeWhoseListNamesItsOwnProductSaysSo` went red with it.
- *A text saying `default` letting the dropdown decide.* Broke the dropdown branch so a recipe with a text setting never consults the dropdown at all. `TestTextOnTheWordLetsTheDropdownDecide`, `TestTextOnTheWordWithATrailingCommaLetsTheDropdownDecide` and `TestAnUnreadableTextLetsTheDropdownDecide` all went red with the setting's own declared list (2 steel-plate) where the chosen preset (1 copper-plate) belongs.
- *A partial custom cost.* Broke the merge in both halves so a field left at its default is taken from the setting rather than from the tier. Go: `TestATimeAloneOverridesTheTier` and `TestAPackTextAloneOverridesTheTier` went red with `count 0` where `count 200` belongs. Rust: `a_time_alone_overrides_the_tier` and `a_pack_text_alone_overrides_the_tier` went red on the same two numbers.
- *The `is set aside` clause.* Broke the condition in `ingredientsFromLine` so the clause is appended with no dropdown. `TestTextWithNoDropdownCarriesNoSetAsideClause`, `TestTextSettingOnAnEditedText` and `TestTextSettingNamesThePlansOwnItem` went red reading `; the  choice  is set aside`, which is also what says the clause is built from the dropdown rather than from decoration.
- *The new count and seconds validation.* Broke the beside-a-dropdown bound check and the maximum rule. Six subtests of `TestCustomizerPlanRefusals` went red, each at BOTH planners: the two maximum cases and the three beside-a-dropdown cases accepted plans they must refuse.
- *The research-number locale rule, in both halves.* Broke the obligation in `go/locale.go` and in `rust/src/locale.rs`. Go went red on `TestCheckLocaleRequiresAResearchNumberDescription` and `TestCheckLocaleFindings`; Rust went red on `check_locale_requires_a_research_number_description`, which this round added because the Rust half had no witness for the new rule and the shared golden does not reach it (the example's own `.cfg` supplies every description).

**WHAT MOVED IN EACH GOLDEN, AND WHY.**

- `testdata/mirror/transcript.golden` was re-recorded. The three dropdowns lost their `custom` value from `allowed_values`; every dropdown with a text setting beside it gained the switch line as a last parameter; every text setting's description gained its own switch line as a fifth parameter; the four research numbers became int settings with descriptions of their own; and the stand-in's settings table was rewritten to the new coverage, so the data half of the transcript moved with it.
- `testdata/mirror/standin.lua`'s flipped table is the COMPLEMENT of the in-game gate's, field by field, which is what makes the two gates cover both sides of every switch: `quench-medium` on `oil` with its text TYPED (the text wins, the set-aside clause appears), `chain-links` on `long` with its text ABSENT (the dropdown decides, no line), `tips-research-tier` on `military` with `tips-count` alone moved (a PARTIAL custom cost: the count is the player's, the time and the packs the tier's), `chain-*` unchanged in shape, and `rivet-ingredients` still carrying the text the language refuses.
- `testdata/ingame/flipped.json` was rewritten, `flipped.golden.dat` re-recorded from it and both rows of `dump-sha256.txt` re-recorded. The old file set two dropdowns to `custom`, which no longer exists and which the engine would silently reset. The new one leaves `quench-ingredients` alone behind the `oil` preset, types into `chain-ingredients` over the `long` preset with a fluid and a fractional amount, overrides the research cost whole (count 40, time 20, the pack text still the `militar-science-pack` typo), keeps `rivet-ingredients` as the typed list of a recipe with no dropdown, and adds `forging-time` at 7.5 so the double encoding is still exercised now that `tips-seconds` is an int. Both hashes moved: the data hash because the row emits different prototypes, and the settings hash on BOTH rows because the setting prototypes themselves changed (three dropdowns lost a value, two settings changed type, every composed description grew a line).
- `testdata/locale/example.cfg` lost its three `[string-mod-setting]` `-custom` entries, which name values that no longer exist. `testdata/locale/findings.golden` did NOT move, and that is the point of dropping them: the plan stopped declaring those three values and the file stopped naming them in the same commit, so neither a missing-value finding nor an orphan-entry finding appears, and the fixture's four deliberate faults are the whole report exactly as before. The fixture already carried `[mod-setting-description]` entries for all four research numbers, so the new REQUIRED rule is satisfied there without adding any.
- `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` did not move, and could not have: no message the language builds changed in this commit.

**THE SEAM AND THE SIZE.** The link-time seam holds: both `notext` fixtures still carry none of the language's own messages (`invisible character`, `is longer than`, `stands alone`, `no item or fluid is named` all grep zero in the packaged `fk_data_module.lua`, and one each in both example guests), which is the property the whole indirection exists for, and the new range line reaches the amount formatter only through a setting a `CustomCost` names, so a plan with no packs setting cannot reach it. The COMPOSED PROSE every guest carries grew from 234 bytes of string data to 540, because the three switch lines and the two range lines ship behind a load-time check like the four before them. The two fixtures got SMALLER anyway: 2,719,629 to 2,640,076 bytes for the Go one and 2,302,697 to 2,215,300 for the Rust one, because the custom-arm machinery every guest used to link went with the surface. Those pairs are not the same measurement, and the README says so: the earlier figures were taken against an `fklua` at FkLua b88965d on 2026-09-09 and the later ones against 01d640a on 2026-09-13, so both this library and that one moved between them.

**WHAT THE ROUND DECIDED AGAINST, AND WHAT IT LEFT OPEN.**

- *The mirror lost its ingredient-ladder drop line, and the loss is deliberate.* Every dropdown in the stand-in is now either overridden by the text beside it or left on a preset whose plan has no absent rung, so no ingredient ladder is walked there. The in-game gate's default row walks both quenching plans and asserts the fallback and the drop; what the mirror holds instead is the SWITCH, which no engine run can show on both sides at once. The script says so where the greps used to be.
- *The locale checker's `custom arm` wording is fixed.* `the dropdown setting <full> has no [mod-setting-description] entry, which the custom arm composes its preset list onto` named a thing this library no longer has, and now reads `... entry, and the library composes its preset list onto that entry`, byte-identically in both halves. `testdata/locale/findings.golden` did NOT move: the example's own `.cfg` supplies that entry, so the fixture never produces this finding, and the witnesses that do are the unit tests in both halves.
- *A tier whose unit carries no `count` at all.* When the count setting is at its default and the tier prices itself by `count_formula` alone, the cost line now prints `count by formula` rather than the setting's declared default, which beside a dropdown is a 0 nothing in the emitted unit is using. Both terms of the condition are load-bearing: the formula test is what keeps the phrase true, because a unit with neither a count nor a formula is one the engine refuses outright and is reachable only from a fixture World, where the honest answer is the number. Neither gate reaches this path, because the only formula-priced tier either of them chooses is chosen by a row that also supplies a count; the unit tests reach it in both halves.
- *The tier's own packs are never held to the packless rule.* A `CostBy` tier whose unit names no science pack this game has produces a research the player finishes instantly, and the library says nothing, exactly as it said nothing before this commit: the packs belong to whoever declared that technology. What IS new is that a typed pack list CLEARS the mark a `CostBy` fallback set, because the player's own list is what the technology is priced in and refusing over packs nothing emits would be a refusal the player's text had already removed.

#### Decision C's tooltip line, folded into decision A's commit

**WHY IT IS HERE AND NOT IN ITS OWN COMMIT.** An adversarial review of the round's five decisions landed after decision A's work was done, and its one gating verdict is that A must not land alone. The reason is structural. A's rule ("a field that is not the reserved word is live") is a RULE and not a STATE: the settings screen has no conditional visibility at all, so it can say which field wins and can never say which one is winning. A player who moves the dropdown while a non-default text sits under it therefore gets nothing, and before this line the only signal was a `fkrecipes: ` log line. THE LOG IS NOT A DISCLOSURE, by the threat model's own second paragraph: a player reads the settings screen, the tooltip of the thing in front of them, and the changelog. So the disclosure ships in the same commit as the behaviour it is about, and head never carries the awkward state.

**THE SHAPE.** `appendLocalised` / `append_localised` is the single writer of `localised_description` on every prototype this library emits, and it takes a third argument now. Author description present and no note: `{"", "<description>"}` byte for byte as before, so a golden taken before this line existed does not move for a load nothing fell back on. Description and note: `{"", "<description>", "\n<note>"}`. Note alone: `{"", "<note>"}`. Neither: nothing emitted. An ITEM never carries one and `itemProto` passes the empty string, because the fallback is about what a recipe makes or what a technology costs.

**THE TEXTS**, as this round wrote them; the outcome clause of the first was reworded by fix round 3's decision 2 and now reads `so the game loaded as though that setting had been left alone`.

```
The stored value of <setting> could not be used, so this mod's own choice applies instead. The reason is in the log.
Changing a recipe empties an assembling machine's input slots of anything the new list does not use.
```

The first is the whole note on a TECHNOLOGY. A RECIPE carries both, joined by a space on the one trailing line, and the second also ends the `fkrecipes: ERROR: ` line of a recipe's INGREDIENT text and of nothing else: the pack text and the two research numbers keep their line byte for byte, because repricing a research destroys nothing. The sentence is measured rather than cautionary, out of finding 8 of the consumer's assessment: up to eighty items destroyed outright rather than spilled on the ground, with no line anywhere. It is one constant with two readers in each half, so the tooltip and the log line cannot drift apart, and `playerFallback` takes a TAIL parameter rather than branching on the reason string, so no sentence here is chosen by reading another sentence.

**ENGLISH LITERALS, NEVER A LOCALE KEY, AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT DECIDED IT.** On 2.0.77 an undefined locale key anywhere in a prototype's `localised_description` drops the recipe's ENTIRE description on the client: no `Unknown key` marker, no empty line, the title and the ingredients and the crafting time all still drawn, exit 0, and the engine's own `data-raw-dump.json` holding the description verbatim. So the dump every gate in this repository reads cannot see the defect at all. Wrapping the key as `{"?", {key}, "literal"}` inside the join rescues it, and an undefined key under `?` counts as a FAILED alternative rather than as a successful `Unknown key` render, measured on the same client run with the failure mode beside it as a control. That form is the answer for a SETTING's composed description; it is not the answer here, because the library has no localisation channel for prototype prose at all (`appendLocalised` wraps a consumer's own `Description` as a literal too) and inventing a key would make every consumer owe an entry whose absence deletes the sentence it was meant to carry. Every sentence this library composes onto a setting is already an English literal, and this follows that precedent rather than setting a new one.

**THE PLUMBING, AND THE SHAPE THAT WAS REJECTED.** `res.fellBack` / `fell_back` records which SETTINGS fell back, in walk order and once each, and that is all it could ever say: it does not know which recipe or technology the setting was bound to. Two parallel slices sized from the declaration counts hold the notes (`recipeNotes`, `techNotes`), and the walk threads a `noteTarget` / `NoteTarget` into every reader that can fall back. A CURSOR ON THE RESOLUTION WAS THE OBVIOUS ALTERNATIVE AND IT IS THE WRONG SHAPE: a cursor left stale by one arm writes a note silently onto the previous declaration, while a parameter makes a caller that forgot it a compile error. Deriving the index from `len(res.recipes)` is wrong for a second reason: a recipe's list is handed over at the END of its arm and every read that can fall back happens before it. `noteFallback` records the note BEFORE the per-setting dedupe, deliberately: one bad field on the settings screen is one log line however many declarations read it, but a crafting-time setting two recipes read is TWO tooltips, and a player hovering the second is owed the same sentence as the first. Within one prototype the FIRST note in walk order wins, so the string is the same every run.

**THE RED PROOFS, each break made in the source, the designed failure observed, and the break reverted.**

- *The note beside an author's own description.* Broke `appendLocalised` in Go so the three-parameter arm emits the description alone. `TestAFallbackNoteJoinsTheAuthorsOwnDescription` went red on the recipe AND on the technology, each reading `["", "<author sentence>"]` where the three-element form belongs. The Rust twin of the same break went red on `a_fallback_note_joins_the_authors_own_description` with the same two lines.
- *An item carrying a note.* Passed a note into `itemProto`. The same test went red on line 3 with the item's description grown a second parameter, which is the witness that "an item never carries one" is a rule and not an accident of no item ever having had a fallback.
- *The recipe-only tail on the ERROR line.* Broke `textFallbackFor` in Go so every text fallback takes the recipe tail. Four tests went red, and the informative ones are the PACK-text ones: `TestPackTextNamingAPlanItemIsToldItIsAnItem` and `TestARefusedPackTextBesideATierTakesTheTiersPacks` each read the assembling-machine sentence on a line about a research cost. The Rust twin went red on five, including its public-surface witness.
- *The note's walk order.* Broke `noteOn` so the LAST note wins instead of the first. `TestPlayerFieldsFallBackWhileTheAuthorChannelStillRefuses` went red naming `steelworks-axe-ingredients` where `steelworks-forging-time` belongs, which is the one test in either half where two settings fall back on one recipe.
- *The second recipe of a shared crafting time.* Moved the note behind the per-setting dedupe. `TestOneBadCraftingTimeSettingTwoRecipesLogsOneLine` went red with the HAMMER recipe carrying no note while the axe recipe carried one, which is exactly the defect the two-rule split exists to prevent.
- *The gates, both of them, against the library rather than against their own greps.* Dropped the note from `techProto` in both halves and ran the engine gate: `FAIL: the technology whose pack text was set aside says so in its own tooltip (the dump says false)` and `FAIL: the flipped dumps do not match the golden for Factorio 2.0.77`, which is also the proof that the flipped data hash moved BECAUSE of this line. Dropped it from `recipeProto` in both halves and ran the mirror: `FAIL: the recipe whose text was set aside carries no note in its own description` and the transcript golden mismatch beside it.

**WHAT THE GATES PIN NOW.** `scripts/run-mirror.sh` greps the whole composed `localised_description` of `fkrecipes-example-steel-rivet` verbatim, and greps that `fkrecipes-example-salvaged-steel-rivet` carries no note; both restricted to the `TRANSCRIPT extend#` lines, because the stand-in's `FINAL` dump is one line holding every prototype at once and a grep over the file would pass on the note landing anywhere at all. `scripts/run-ingame.sh` pins `fkrecipes-example-hardened-tips`'s whole three-element description out of the flipped dump and pins `fkrecipes-example-steel-chain`'s two-element one, the recipe in that row whose typed list was taken and so fell back on nothing.

**WHAT IS OWED, AND NO GATE HERE CAN TAKE IT.** The dump carries the string whether or not it draws, which is the whole lesson of the undefined-key measurement above, so nothing in this repository can see this line RENDER. The client render of the composed trailing line at its longest, where it wraps in a real crafting tooltip and where the technology's wraps in the tech tree, is OWED and belongs to the same client round as the wrap questions already open for the settings tooltips. It will be taken before the consumer adopts this.

#### The localised ceilings, corrected

The design record said "20 parameters and 19 nested tables". The parameter figure is right and its SCOPE was wrong, and the nesting figure was wrong outright. Re-measured on 2.0.77 twice independently this cycle, headless, one `--dump-data` run per row:

- **20 PARAMETERS PER TABLE**, not per string. The 21st refuses the load, exit 1: `Too many parameters for localised string: 21 > 20 (limit).` A literal parameter and a table parameter count alike. A recipe prototype carries the same ceiling with its own kind in the text: `Error while loading recipe prototype "probe-sweep" (recipe): Too many parameters for localised string: 21 > 20 (limit).`
- **20 LEVELS OF NESTING DEPTH.** The 21st refuses: `Too deep recursion for localised string: 21 > 20 (limit).` The root table is level 1, every parameter sits one level below the table holding it, a plain-string parameter occupies a level of its own, and the key at element 0 does not. The earlier "19 nested tables" came of reading the engine's own counter as a table count.
- **NO GLOBAL TABLE BUDGET AT ALL.** A description holding 421 tables at depth 3 loads with exit 0.

The constant's VALUE (20) and `localisedGroup`'s overflow rule (keep 19, nest the rest into slot 20) were both already correct and neither moves; only the prose around them was wrong, and it is corrected in both halves' comments and in `agents/customizer-design.md`. What changes in reading is the headroom: nesting spends DEPTH, which is the budget with 20 levels in it, so a dropdown that outgrows its top level is a fill rather than a wall.

#### count_formula, measured

The working tree already dropped `count_formula` and wrote the player's count when the count setting is in force. That is now measured to be the only workable choice, headless on 2.0.77:

- a unit carrying BOTH `count` and `count_formula` is REFUSED, exit 1: `Error while loading technology prototype "tp-both" (technology): Ambiguous definition: count and count_formula are both defined.` So leaving the formula beside the player's count does not exist as an option;
- a unit carrying `count_formula` and no `count` loads, which is the shape base's infinite technologies are in (`physical-projectile-damage-7` carries `"count_formula": "2^(L-7)*1000"` and no count);
- a unit carrying `count` and no `count_formula` under `max_level = "infinite"` loads and reads `research_unit_count = 500` at level 1, level 2 and level 5. **THAT IS THE COST OF THE DROP: the technology still levels and every level costs the same, the scaling is gone from the prototype, and the engine says nothing.** It is disclosed by the log line naming the setting and, now, by decision C's trailing line on the technology itself;
- dropping the formula without writing a count is refused by name (`Key "count_formula" not found in property tree at ROOT.technology.X.unit`), and `count = 0` beside no formula is refused with `count must be positive if a count_formula is not defined.`

The drop stays and there is no refusal at Emit: the trigger is a number the player typed, and this round's whole rule is that a player's typed value never stops a load. One cosmetic defect the implementer had flagged is fixed with it: when the count setting DEFERS (0) beside a formula-priced tier, the cost line now reads `count by formula` instead of `count 0`, which was a number nothing in the emitted unit was using.

**A PRE-EXISTING HAZARD THIS COMMIT DOES NOT INTRODUCE AND NOW DOCUMENTS.** A `count_formula` is written in terms of the level `L` and `CostOf` copies a unit verbatim, so a formula written for a source starting at level 7 evaluates at level 1 on a technology with no `max_level`: `count_formula = "2^(L-7)*1000"` on a one-level technology reads `research_unit_count = 15`, with no refusal and no warning. That reaches a `CostBy` tier whose ladder lands on such a source too. It is a caution in `docs/usage.md`'s cost section now rather than a code change.

#### The type change, measured

`CustomCost.Seconds` moves from a `double-setting` to an `int-setting` under the SAME name, and `docs/migration.md` had no row for a type change and an upgrade section that claimed the declaration surface is stable across an upgrade. Both are fixed. Measured headless on 2.0.77, an `int-setting` with minimum 0, maximum 600 and a declared default of 100, under a name whose stored value is a double:

| stored | engine line | exit | value the mod saw | the file after |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `45.0` | none | 0 | `45` | rewritten, now signed-int encoded |
| `7.5` | none | 0 | `7` | rewritten as `7` |
| `-0.5`, below the new minimum | none | 0 | `0` | rewritten as `0` |
| `1200.5`, above the new maximum | none | 0 | `100`, the DECLARED DEFAULT rather than a clamp | rewritten as `100` |

The mechanism: the double is truncated TOWARD ZERO (`-5.5` to `-5`, `599.5` to `599`), then range checked; in range it is kept, out of range the declared default applies. Nothing is logged in any path, `--dump-data` exits 0 in all of them, and the first successful load rewrites the file so the old value is unrecoverable. **THE LIBRARY CANNOT WARN ABOUT THIS AND NEITHER CAN A CONSUMER**: by the data stage it sees `7`, with nothing marking it as having been `7.5`, and the engine's own type-mismatch check fires only on a genuine property-tree KIND mismatch (a string under a number), not on a double under an int, and even then the load succeeds on the declared default.

#### The consumer break, and the migration paragraphs owed with it

`docs/migration.md` now names the break by declaration: `IngredientChoices.Custom`, `IngredientChoices.CustomValue`, `CostChoices.Custom`, `CostChoices.CustomValue`, `CustomCost.Position`, and `CustomCost.Seconds` needing an `IntSettingRef` where it took a `DoubleSettingRef`; in Rust the three struct literals fail with `E0560: struct has no field named 'custom'`. Beside it, two paragraphs the round owed:

- **Withdrawing a dropdown value costs what adding one costs, run backwards.** The engine's rule is about the running release's `allowed_values` and nothing else, so removing a value a release already shipped resets a player who stored it, silently and permanently, exactly as adding one resets a player who rolls back. The library cannot tell an addition from a withdrawal, because a declaration says which values exist now and never which ones existed before. For THIS library's own change it costs nothing, because no published consumer release ever offered `custom` in a dropdown and finding 3's rollback destruction has no subject left; for a consumer who had already shipped one it is a real loss and belongs in a release note.
- **The orphan locale entry.** With the `custom` value gone the checker no longer requires `string-mod-setting.<dropdown>-custom`, so a consumer's existing entry becomes an orphan finding on the next `CheckLocaleWith` run. Said out loud, so an upgrading consumer deletes the line rather than debugging the report.

#### The README size figures

The `notext` figures in the working tree were re-measured against FkLua `01d640a` while the committed pair was taken at `b88965d`, and the paragraph presented the two as a before-and-after with a disclaimer attached. The disclaimer is gone and so is the older pair: the README now carries ONE pair, names the `fklua` head and the date it was taken at, and says that a packaged size is a figure for its `fklua` head as much as for this library, so two of them are comparable only when both name the same head. The figures at `01d640a` are the Go guest's 2,640,076 bytes and the Rust guest's 2,215,300.

### The adversarial review of decision A, and the eight things it moved

A code review over the uncommitted tree found one false sentence where a player looks, two helpers with no test that could tell them from a constant, and six pieces of prose the round had left behind. Every fix below is in the same commit as the work it amends.

**THE DESTROYED-INPUTS SENTENCE WAS SCOPED BY PROTOTYPE KIND, AND THE KIND IS NOT THE PREDICATE.** `noteOn` chose the tail off `!tgt.tech`, so a recipe whose CRAFTING TIME fell back carried, in its own crafting tooltip, `Changing a recipe empties an assembling machine's input slots of anything the new list does not use.` It does not: a crafting-time fallback moves `energy_required` and emits a byte-identical ingredient list. That is a false sentence in the one place this round exists to make honest, and it contradicted the ERROR line beside it, which `textFallbackFor` had already scoped to a recipe's INGREDIENT TEXT alone. The fix is the narrow rule: `noteFallback` and `noteOn` take a `destroysInputs` / `destroys_inputs` argument, the two text sites pass the predicate and the crafting-time and research-number sites pass `false` outright, and the predicate has one name (`movesIngredients` / `moves_ingredients`) that `textFallbackFor` reads too, so the tooltip and the log cannot disagree about which fallbacks destroy anything. `fallbackNote`'s second parameter is that answer rather than a prototype kind now.

**NEITHER GATE COULD HAVE CAUGHT IT, WHICH IS WHY IT SURVIVED.** The mirror's only fallen-back prototype is a recipe whose INGREDIENT TEXT was refused, which keeps the sentence; the in-game flipped row's only one is a technology, which never had it. No row in either gate reaches a crafting-time fallback, so this correction moved NO golden: `testdata/mirror/transcript.golden` and both rows of `testdata/ingame/dump-sha256.txt` are byte for byte what they were. The witnesses are the unit tests in both halves, which had been PINNING the defect (`noteIn(..., true)` on three crafting-time transcripts per half) and now pin the rule.

**THE TWO ORDER HELPERS WERE INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM CONSTANTS.** Every fixture in the repository declared the dropdown before the text setting, so `relativeOrder` / `relative_order` answered `above` on the text side and `below` on the dropdown side in all of them; the strings `chosen below` and `The setting above` appeared nowhere, and replacing both helpers with hard-coded words left every test and both gates green. One test per half now declares a generated text setting FIRST and a `LegacyDropdownSettingNeedingLocale` carrying the consumer's own order `z` after it, which sorts the dropdown under the text setting, and asserts the two sentences with their words swapped.

**A GO TWIN FOR THE FALLBACK-TIER PATH.** `a_setting_overrides_the_fallback_tier` had no Go half and neither gate walks it: a `CostBy` ladder whose chosen sources carry no unit falls back to the author's declared fallback cost, and the settings then write over THAT tier field by field. `TestASettingOverridesTheFallbackTier` holds the same three lines.

**THE COMPOSED DESCRIPTION IS FIVE PARAMETERS AND SIX PLACES STILL SAID FOUR OR THREE.** Corrected: `docs/ingredient-list.md` and `docs/usage.md` each said the library composes three lines and enumerated three (both now name the switch line as the fourth), `docs/migration.md` said two library sentences under the default line (three), `go/locale.go`'s required-description comment said an absent entry loses all four (five), `go/locale.go` and `rust/src/locale.rs` both said the composition is fixed at four parameters (five), `agents/customizer-design.md` said it spends 4 of 20 in two places (5 of 20, and the 4 is noted as the figure before the switch line landed), `agents/implementation-notes.md` said `depth 2 of 19` where the corrected ceiling is 20 levels, and `rust/src/tests/transcript.rs` called the tail two lines (three).

**THE DRIFT GUARD IGNORED THE LINE THIS ROUND ADDED.** `guardedTextDescription` / `guarded_text_description` was already passing the switch line into the composition it hands the guard, but `composedTextLinesMissing` / `composed_text_lines_missing` still checked only the format line and the fallback line, so `textDescription` dropping its switch-line argument would have shipped silently. The guard has a third row now, reported as `the library composes no line about which field decides while the text says default onto a text setting's description; ...` in the composition's own order. The switch line is the one line of the three that is not a constant, so `guardedTextDescription` returns it beside the composition rather than the rule recomputing it: what the guard answers is whether `textDescription` put the line it was handed into the table it returned.

**DEAD FIXTURE SURFACE, RE-POINTED RATHER THAN DELETED.** `withSettingButAbsent` had lost its only callers with `noteIgnoredNumber` and `noteIgnoredText`, and its comment still named both. The `!ok` term it guards is alive in `resolveTextList` and `readNumber`, so it is read by a test again: `TestASettingAnsweringAValueBesideNotOkIsAbsent` hands a text setting and a crafting time each a perfectly edit-shaped value beside `ok=false` and asserts both take their declared defaults with the ordinary unreadable line and NO note. IT IS A GO-ONLY HAZARD AND HAS NO RUST TWIN, deliberately: Go's `World` answers `(Value, bool)` and can express the pair, Rust's answers `Option<Value>` and cannot.

**`researchNumberMaximum` SAID "DECLARES NO MAXIMUM" OF A DECLARATION THAT CARRIES ONE.** It fires on `!HasMax || Max < 1`, so `Between(0, 0)` beside a research dropdown tripped a sentence that was false about it. Both halves now say `backs a research number but declares no maximum of at least 1; a number the player types needs a ceiling it can reach`, byte for byte, and each half's validation table gained the `Between(0, 0)` case that the old wording lied about. It is written beside a dropdown because that is where a minimum of 0 is legal at all; with no dropdown the minimum rule answers first.

**THE `fklua` HEAD, AND WHAT IS OWED.** `CLAUDE.md` claimed `rust/Cargo.lock`'s `b88965d` is "the same head the harness builds fklua from". It is not: the lock pins the fkdata DEPENDENCY this crate compiles against, while both scripts build the `fklua` BINARY out of whatever the `FKLUA_CHECKOUT` sibling holds, which moved to `01d640a` under this round. Neither the lock nor the checkout was touched here; `CLAUDE.md` now says the two can drift and currently do. **SYNCING THIS REPOSITORY ONTO THE NEWER FkLua HEAD IS OWED AS ITS OWN ROUND**, and it is a round rather than a `cargo update` because every packaged-size figure in `README.md` is a figure for the head it was taken at, so the sync re-takes them.

**THE CHEAP ONES.** `agents/customizer-design.md` still named `customPrereqs`, which this diff deletes; `rust/src/settings.rs` still said `custom_arm_for` where the Go twin already said `settingDescriptions`; `go/settings.go` said "every kind but a text setting and a dropdown with a Custom arm" where it is three kinds and there is no arm; `docs/usage.md` stated the `count by formula` rule twice in adjacent paragraphs and keeps one; `scripts/run-mirror.sh` said the DEFAULT in-game row walks both quenching ladders where it walks the water plan and the flipped row walks the oil plan; `testdata/mirror/standin.lua` had "the two lines that says it"; and one doc-comment line in `rust/src/tests/source.rs` was long enough to have been hand-joined and `cargo fmt` does not reflow prose.

**THE RED PROOFS, each break made in the source, the designed failure observed, and the break reverted.**

- *The destroyed-inputs scoping.* Put `noteOn` back on `fallbackNote(setting, !tgt.tech)`. Go went red on three tests, `TestPlayerFieldsFallBackWhileTheAuthorChannelStillRefuses`, `TestBoundCraftingTimeFallsBack` (all three subtests) and `TestOneBadCraftingTimeSettingTwoRecipesLogsOneLine`, each reading the assembling-machine sentence on a recipe whose `ingredients=` is identical on both sides of the diff. The Rust twin of the same break went red on the same three.
- *The two order helpers.* Replaced each half's body with a bare `above`. `TestTheSwitchLinesFollowTheEmittedOrder` went red reading `the option chosen above` where `below` belongs, and `the_switch_lines_follow_the_emitted_order` with the same line.
- *`count by formula`.* Set `byFormula` / `by_formula` to `false`. `TestATimeBesideACountFormulaLeavesItAlone` and `a_time_beside_a_count_formula_leaves_it_alone` each went red reading `count 0` where `count by formula` belongs, which is the proof this round's cosmetic fix owed and had not recorded.
- *The guard's third row.* Dropped the switch-line parameter out of `textDescription` / `text_description`. Go went red on four tests, the guard's own and three `CheckLocale` ones, each reporting `the library composes no line about which field decides while the text says default ...` from a healthy locale file; Rust went red on the same rule.

**WHAT THIS AMENDMENT LEAVES OPEN, IN THE ROUND'S OWN VOICE.**

- *One note per prototype means the note can name the wrong field.* The first fallback in walk order wins, and a crafting time is read before an ingredient text, so a recipe whose crafting time fell back AND whose ingredient text was refused carries the crafting-time note alone: the tooltip names a field the player can go and fix that is not the reason their ingredients are wrong, and the line about the text is log-only. `TestPlayerFieldsFallBackWhileTheAuthorChannelStillRefuses` is exactly that plan and pins exactly that behaviour. The design is left as it is rather than grown a second note, because two notes on one tooltip is a shape neither the client render nor the parameter budget has been measured for; it is recorded here so the next round decides it deliberately.
- *Two cosmetic gaps in the cost log line, reachable from a fixture World only.* A tier unit carrying no `time` key leaves the line printing `time 0`, with no `by formula` equivalent to print instead, because a time has no formula form for the phrase to name. A tier carrying neither `count` nor `count_formula` prints the count setting's DECLARED DEFAULT, which beside a dropdown is a 0 nothing in the emitted unit uses. Neither shape reaches an engine: a unit with no count and no formula is refused outright (`count must be positive if a count_formula is not defined.`), and a unit with no time is refused the same way, so both are fixture-only and the honest answer there is the number the resolution actually held.
- *Neither gate walks a crafting-time fallback.* Both gates' only fallen-back prototypes are an ingredient text and a pack text, so the scoping rule corrected above is held by unit tests alone. Adding a crafting-time row would move both goldens and is a round's decision rather than an amendment's.

**GATES.** Every gate in `CLAUDE.md`'s Gates block, exit codes read directly and never through a pipe, all 0: `gofmt -l .` (no output), `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./...`, `go/examples/notext` `go vet .`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo test`, `RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --workspace`, `scripts/run-mirror.sh` and `scripts/run-ingame.sh`. NO GOLDEN MOVED: the mirror transcript is byte for byte what it was, both rows of `testdata/ingame/dump-sha256.txt` are unchanged (`e824cd83...` default, `8cf5985e...` flipped, `b4491942...` settings on both), `testdata/ingame/flipped.golden.dat` compares clean at 689 bytes, `testdata/locale/findings.golden` did not move because the guard's new row fires only on a library defect, and `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` did not move because no message the language builds changed. This amendment added three `func Test` in Go (the order helper, the fallback tier's Go twin and the value-beside-not-ok one) and one `#[test]` in Rust (the order helper), plus one case in each half's research-number validation table and two more assertions inside the drift guard's existing test; the Go suite stands at 223 `func Test` and the Rust lib target at 209 `#[test]` beside the 2 in the public-surface witness.

### Decision B: a mod set never bricks the game either, and refusal is for what the author can see

**THE RULE AS IMPLEMENTED.** A check that asks the `World` anything is ENVIRONMENTAL and degrades loudly; a check that reads only the DECLARATION is an author bug and refuses by name. It keys on WHAT THE CHECK ASKS and not on where an author would have noticed it, because "visible in development" is a property of the author's fixture and this library cannot see a fixture: a fixture World answering `ToolExists` true for a pack the shipped mod set demotes is green in development and red in play. The exceptions are ENUMERATED rather than argued, and they are four: degrading would overwrite another mod's prototype, degrading would hand the engine something it refuses anyway, degrading would invent a value the author never declared, and (the threat model's rather than the rule's) the game cannot reach the site at all.

**THE CLAIM IS DELIBERATELY NOT A TOTAL ONE.** "Nothing an environment does can stop the load" is cycle 1's "a player is never refused" one level up, and the adversarial review said so before a line was written. What this round ships is the two-sided rule plus a named list of survivors, and the list is longer than the brief's table: the review's four rows plus the two named-prototype probes in `validate` (`place_result` and `ResultNamed`), which are environmental, do stay refusals, and fit the enumerated exception "there is nothing declared to degrade to". They are in the classification table with that reason.

**WHAT STAYS A REFUSAL, BY NAME AND WITH ITS EXCEPTION.** (THREE ROWS OF THIS TABLE LEFT IT IN FIX ROUND 3, 2026-09-14: `checkCycles` except for a ring this plan owns no edge in, the copied unit in neither engine form, and `checkResolvedPacks`, which is deleted. The table is left as this round wrote it because the record is dated; the list as it stands is in that round's own section and in `CLAUDE.md`.)

| Site | Exception |
|---|---|
| the three `already exists in data.raw` sentences in `validate` (item, recipe, technology) | degrading silently clobbers a stranger's prototype |
| `validate`'s `names a place_result <x> that does not exist` and `produces <x>, which does not exist` | a bare string with no declared ladder behind it: nothing to degrade to |
| `checkCycles`' `a prerequisite cycle: ...` | emitting a cycle hands the engine a worse message; the legitimate degrade is dropping the splice, a design this round does not make |
| the six `CostOf(...)` sentences | a `CostOf` source is a bare string with no declared ladder behind it |
| the NEW `<tech>: the unit of <source> holds a table this library cannot copy faithfully` | a pack-list entry in neither engine form: passing a name this library never read through to the engine earns a refusal naming neither the technology nor the property |
| `checkResolvedPacks` (the packless refusal) | the floor under every pack degradation: nothing author-declared is left to price the research in |
| `readDropdown`'s `holds "<v>", which is not one of its values` | not reachable through the game (the engine resets an unoffered stored value before any stage runs, measured), so only a hand-edited file arrives there, which the threat model puts out of scope |
| every declared-default post-condition (`refuseCostNumbers`, `checkResolvedCraftTimes`, every `validate*`) | not environmental at all: it reads only the declaration |

**THE TWO MEASUREMENTS THAT MOVED IT.**

*The engine's own two refusals for an unusable research pack*, headless on 2.0.77 build 84539, one `--dump-data` run per row, a probe mod declaring one technology whose `unit.ingredients` names the row's value:

```
tprobe.sh iron-plate '{"iron-plate", 1}'
  Error while running setup for technology prototype "tprobe-t" (technology): Invalid research unit (iron-plate). Research unit(s) can only be tool type items at the moment.
tprobe.sh water '{"water", 1}'
  Error in assignID: item with name 'water' does not exist.
```

Two questions and not one: a pack that is an ITEM but not a TOOL fails one way, and a name the game does not have at all fails EARLIER and more coarsely, naming neither the technology nor the property. `ToolExists` is the one probe that answers both, so the copied unit's every ingredient goes through it.

*A packless research unit IN PLAY*, which FkLua's own `agents/datastage.md` and this repository's design record both carried as an open item ("`research-unit-empty` measures that a unit with no packs LOADS; whether such a research ever completes for a player is a runtime question and the probe has no runtime"). Taken with a scenario mod whose `control.lua` calls `force.add_research` on a technology of `{count = 10, time = 15, ingredients = {}}` and prints the force's state every 600 ticks, driven headless:

```
factorio -c <config> --mod-directory <mods> --benchmark <save>.zip --benchmark-ticks 10000 --benchmark-runs 1
```

It LOADS with exit 0 and no engine line; `research_unit_energy` reads 900 and `research_unit_count` 10; `add_research` returns true; the queue holds it; `research_progress` advances every tick in a lab holding no science pack at all (the lab's `automation-science-pack` count is 0 at every sample) and the technology is `researched=true` between tick 9000 and tick 9600, which is `count * time` ticks. It consumes nothing. **SO A PACKLESS TECHNOLOGY IS NOT A STUCK TECHNOLOGY, IT IS A FREE ONE**, and emitting one silently would be a balance change the player did not choose. That is why the packless refusal stays, and it is recorded in `agents/customizer-design.md` against the open item it closes.

**WHAT DEGRADES NOW.**

- *A copied research unit's packs.* `filterCopiedPacks` / `filter_copied_packs` runs over the verbatim unit a `CostOf` copies and over the one a `CostBy` tier's chosen source supplies, decoding `ingredients` in BOTH engine forms (the short tuple `{"automation-science-pack", 1}` and the long `{name = ..., amount = ...}`), reading only the NAME and keeping the ENTRY, so an amount this library does not model and a field no version of it has heard of survive the filter untouched. Each pack the game does not have is dropped with `fkrecipes: <tech>: <name> is not a science pack this game has, so it is left out of the <source> cost`. `CostOf` moved out of `techUnit` / `tech_unit` and into `resolve` for this: a question about the game belongs where every other question about the game is asked and where a log line can still be written.
- *A copied cost that keeps no pack.* Where there is an author-declared cost behind it, which is a `CostBy` tier's `Fallback`, that is what the technology is priced in, resolved through the same ladder so its own absent rungs drop the same way, with `fkrecipes: ERROR: <tech>: the <source> cost names no science pack this game has, so this mod's own declared cost applies instead`. The PREREQUISITE AND THE LEVEL CAP STAY, because the tier still chose that rung and only the price moved. Where there is none, `CostOf` being the case, the refusal stays.
- *A merged amount over a ceiling.* `mergeIngredient` / `merge_ingredient` and `mergePack` / `merge_pack` cap at 65535 (items) and 1e301 (fluids) with a line in the merge line's own voice ending `, so it is capped there`, rather than refusing. The DECLARED ceilings (`validateIngredients`, `validateUnit` and their twins) stay refusals.

**THE EXACT NEW TEXTS.** (The second and the sixth of these are GONE at fix round 3: the copied unit in neither engine form degrades with a longer line, and the packless refusal is deleted outright.)

```
fkrecipes: <tech>: <name> is not a science pack this game has, so it is left out of the <source> cost
fkrecipes: <tech>: the unit of <source> holds a table this library cannot copy faithfully
fkrecipes: ERROR: <tech>: the <source> cost names no science pack this game has, so this mod's own declared cost applies instead
fkrecipes: <subject>: <name> is in the list twice after the fallbacks, and <a> plus <b> is above the item ceiling of 65535, so it is capped there
fkrecipes: <subject>: <name> is in the list twice after the fallbacks, and the added amount is above the fluid ceiling of 1e301, so it is capped there; the ladder from <rung> resolved onto it
fkrecipes: the technology <tech> has no science pack the game has; research takes at least one, and none of <names> is a science pack here
```

And four trailing lines in an emitted prototype's own `localised_description`, by commit A's mechanism and with their own composers:

```
This game has no <name>, so this research was priced without it. The reason is in the log.
This game has none of the science packs the <source> cost names, so that cost was not used to price this research. The reason is in the log.
Two ingredients resolved onto <name> and the total was above what one slot holds, so it was capped at 65535. The reason is in the log.
Two ingredients resolved onto <name> and the total was above the largest amount the game can hold, so it was capped at 1e301. The reason is in the log.
```

**THE FOURTH OF THOSE IS NOT IN THE BRIEF AND IS REPORTED AS A DEVIATION.** The decision gave one clamp sentence, with "what one slot holds" and the literal 65535 in it. Both halves of that phrase are FALSE of a fluid: a fluid amount is not a stack and its ceiling is 1e301. Writing the item sentence onto a fluid clamp would be a false sentence in the one place this round exists to make honest, which is the same defect commit A's amendment removed from the destroyed-inputs tail. So the fluid clamp has a twin in the same voice. The item sentence is the brief's, byte for byte.

**A RECIPE CLAMP CARRIES THE DESTRUCTION SENTENCE AND A PACK CLAMP DOES NOT**, through the `destroysInputs` / `destroys_inputs` argument commit A's amendment installed: a clamped ingredient changes the ingredient LIST, and repricing a research empties no assembling machine.

**THE LADDER GETS NO NOTE, DELIBERATELY**, and the reason is in the design record and in `docs/usage.md`: a resolve-or-drop ingredient ladder is the library's advertised contract and the SETTINGS SCREEN is where that contract is disclosed, whereas a clamped amount and a dropped science pack are arithmetic and presence a player cannot check anywhere. **[AMENDED by fix round 3's decision 5, 2026-09-14: this paragraph quoted "the dropdown's own composed description already discloses it (where one names something your mods do not have, the nearest thing they do have is used instead)", and the library composed no such sentence. That was the PILOT CONSUMER'S own locale entry, quoted verbatim in the build report as if it were the library's output, which is the exact defect finding 22 was about. The library composes it now, in three vocabularies, through `dropdownLadderLine` / `DROPDOWN_LADDER_LINE` and `textLadderLine` / `text_ladder_line`. And the exclusion is narrower than this sentence says: a ladder that empties the recipe COMPLETELY carries `ingredientlessNote` / `ingredientless_note` on the prototype, because a recipe with nothing to craft it from is a balance change rather than a shorter list.]**

**`withFallbackNote`'S ROUTE IS GONE AND ITS FACT IS NOT**, and the composer is now `fallbackFact` / `fallback_fact` at the same four post-resolution exits (the carried refusal, the craft-time post-condition, the packless refusal, the cycle walk; the Rust side is one `map_err` over three checks plus the carried refusal). The old sentence named Settings > Mod settings > Startup, and finding 14 re-measured on the client that the `Error loading mods` dialog cannot reach that screen: it offers Disable listed mods, Disable all mods, Manage mods, Restart, Exit and a Reset mod settings checkbox; `Manage mods` has no Mod settings button and its `Back` returns to the same dialog; `Restart` relaunches into an identical dialog over a file whose sha256 has not moved; and the one escape resets every startup preference and disables the mod. That measurement condemns the ROUTE. It does not touch the FACT beside it, which is still the only thing a refused load can say about a value the player typed, because the accumulated log ops never reach the host at all; and the composed description of every text setting PROMISES that fact in its own tooltip ("the reason is in the log, or in the load error if the load stops anyway"), so deleting the composer outright made a settings-screen sentence false. What a refusal adds is therefore `. The stored value of <setting> could not be used, so the mod's own declaration applied.` and nothing more: no screen, no route, no advice. (That outcome clause is as THIS round wrote it; fix round 3's decision 2 found it false beside a preset dropdown and it now reads `and was set aside, so what applied is what that field gives when it is left alone.`) `res.fellBack` / `fell_back` has TWO readers and always did, which an earlier draft of this section got backwards: it is the per-setting dedupe that keeps ONE log line per field however many declarations read it (`go/data.go`'s `noteFallback` says so), and its first element is the setting `fallbackFact` names. Decision C's note is written UNCONDITIONALLY, before that dedupe, and reads the field not at all: two recipes bound to one crafting-time setting are two tooltips.

**A DIVERGENCE THE DECISION DID NOT ANTICIPATE, AND THE CHOICE MADE.** Another mod's science pack can be named with bytes that are not UTF-8. In Rust those arrive as `Value::Bytes` and `tool_exists` takes a `&str`, so that half CANNOT ask the World about such a name; in Go they arrive in an ordinary string and that half could. Refusing them would have regressed `a_copied_unit_carries_a_pack_name_that_is_not_text`, which pins that a copied unit crosses byte-exact. So a pack name this library cannot put to the World is KEPT UNASKED in both halves, and Go declines to ask (`askableName` over `utf8.ValidString`) so that the two answer alike. `copiedPackName` / `copied_pack_name` carries three answers in two values: absent is an entry in NEITHER engine form (refused), the EMPTY name is an entry in a form whose name is not text (kept), and anything else is probed. No prototype is named by the empty string, so the sentinel names nothing real. This is the one place the two halves could have differed silently, and it costs `unicode/utf8` in `go/data.go`.

**THE SOURCE-PROPERTY TEST (the review's B2).** `TestEveryRefusalSiteIsClassified` in `go/source_test.go` and `every_refusal_site_is_classified` in `rust/src/tests/source.rs`, each built on the mechanism that half's existing source properties already use: Go parses the package with `go/ast` and walks every `errors.New`, `fmt.Errorf` and `resolution.refuse` call; Rust scans each module with the same comments-and-literals state machine the other three properties use and matches `Err(format!(`, `Err(String::from(`, `Err(declared_craft_time_problem(` and `.refuse(`, brace-matching each classified item's body. Every refusal site must sit inside a classified item; a `declaration` row's item may not name a `World` (checked from the source rather than asserted); an `environmental` row carries one of the four enumerated exceptions and nothing else; a `mixed` row lists its environmental sites by a distinctive fragment of the sentence, each of which must name EXACTLY ONE site; and every item that can see the game is pinned at its site count, so a refusal added beside the classified ones goes red until somebody classifies it.

The Rust table carries one row the Go one does not, and the asymmetry is real rather than an omission: this crate's ingredient language answers with `Result<_, String>` where the `String` is a PROBLEM that becomes a player's fallback line and never stops a load, while the Go half returns a plain string in the same places and builds no `errors.New` at all, so the Go census never sees the module. `ingredient_list.rs` has a whole-module row saying so, counted. Two more asymmetries the tables record: Go's `PlanData` holds four sites where Rust's `plan_data` holds two (Go guards a nil `World`, which `&dyn World` makes unrepresentable, and Go's carried-refusal re-raise is an `errors.New` while Rust's is a `clone`), and Go keeps the two declared-cost-number refusals in a `resolution` method that names no `World` while Rust inlines them into `resolve_custom_cost`, which does, so that row is `mixed` with no environmental sites.

**THE RED PROOFS, each break made in the source, the designed failure observed, and the break reverted.**

- *The classification test, twice per half.* (1) Added an unclassified `probeRefusal` / `probe_refusal` taking a `World` and refusing. Go went red with `data.go:1380:1: probeRefusal builds 1 refusal(s) and no row classifies it; add one to refusalClasses ...`; Rust with `data.rs:2159: builds a refusal inside no classified item; add a row to REFUSAL_CLASSES ...`. (2) Added a refusal INSIDE `validate`, which both tables already classify. Go went red with `validate in data.go builds 48 refusals and the row pins 47; classify the one that moved`; Rust with `fn validate( in data.rs builds 48 refusals and the row pins 47`.
- *The copied-unit filter, AT THE GATE and not only in a unit test.* Replaced `w.ToolExists(name)` with `true` in `filterCopiedPacks` and ran `scripts/run-mirror.sh`: the stand-in refused the packaged Go guest's load with the engine's own measured sentence, `Invalid research unit (military-science-pack). Research unit(s) can only be tool type items at the moment.` That is finding 13 reproduced end to end, and it is why the stand-in demotes a pack (below).
- *The merge clamps.* The four rewritten tests (`TestMergedItemAmountAboveTheCeilingIsClamped`, `TestMergedFluidAmountAboveTheCeilingIsClamped`, `TestPackLaddersThatLandOnOnePackMerge` and their Rust twins) were written against the clamped transcripts first and each went red on the refusal the old code raised, reading `the plan was accepted with N ops` inverted or a refusal where a transcript belongs.
- *Every new behavioural test* was written before the arm it covers was wired: the six copied-unit tests per half and the three tier tests per half each went red on the unfiltered unit, on an accepted plan where a refusal belongs, or on the missing ERROR line, and then green.

**WHAT MOVED IN EACH GOLDEN, AND WHY.**

- `testdata/mirror/standin.lua` DEMOTES A SCIENCE PACK, which is the coverage this commit owed the mirror. Four lines after the base `data:extend`, `military-science-pack` is moved from `data.raw.tool` into `data.raw.item` with its `durability` dropped: the prototype stays, as an item, and stops being a tool. It happens AFTER the base rows on purpose, because that is the real shape: base declared `military-4` while the pack was still a tool and that row is legal, the demotion is a later mod's doing, and this library's data stage runs after both. The stand-in's own `check_technology` already enforced the engine's measured tool rule, so the guests' filter is what keeps the run green and dropping it is the gate-level red proof above.
- `testdata/mirror/transcript.golden` re-recorded, six lines: the new drop line; the cost line losing `, 1 military-science-pack`; `hardened-tips`'s emitted unit losing that pack and gaining the trailing note in its `localised_description`; and the stand-in's own `RAW item:` and `RAW tool:` rows and its `FINAL` dump moving with the demotion. Nothing else moved.
- `scripts/run-mirror.sh` gained three checks and changed two: the drop line verbatim, the technology's whole three-parameter `localised_description` verbatim (restricted to the `extend#` lines, for the reason the existing note greps are), and the unit and cost-line greps narrowed to two packs.
- `testdata/ingame/dump-sha256.txt` DID NOT MOVE, and that is the right answer rather than a gap: on a stock 2.0.77 install every pack the example's copied units name is a real tool, so nothing this commit added degrades there. Both rows are byte for byte what they were (`e824cd83...` default, `8cf5985e...` flipped, `b4491942...` settings on both), and `testdata/ingame/flipped.golden.dat` compares clean at 689 bytes.
- `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` did not move: no message the LANGUAGE builds changed here. `testdata/locale/findings.golden` did not move either.

**THE LADDER WITH NO LEGAL RUNG WAS ALREADY RIGHT AND NOTHING CHANGED.** The brief asked for a confirmation. An ingredient ladder none of whose rungs exists drops the ingredient with `fkrecipes: <recipe>: none of <a>, <b> is present, so the ingredient is dropped`, and a recipe left with nothing emits an empty ingredient list; both halves pin it (`go/fluid_test.go`'s `none of water is present` case, which asserts the drop line and the `ingredients=[]` prototype beside it, and the Rust twins in `rust/src/tests/data.rs`).
