The language's own rendering of an empty list is the word `none`, which the corpus pins. No code changed for this row.

**GATES, exit codes read directly, all 0:** `gofmt -l .` (no output), `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./...`, `go/examples/notext` `go vet .`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo test`, `RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --workspace`, `scripts/run-mirror.sh` and `scripts/run-ingame.sh`.

**WHAT THIS DECISION LEAVES OPEN.**

- *One note per prototype still means the note can name the wrong thing.* The first note in walk order wins, and a degradation and a player's fallback compete for the same slot. A technology whose pack text fell back AND whose copied tier lost a pack carries whichever came first in the walk. The shape is commit A's and is left as it is for the reason recorded there: two notes on one tooltip is a render nobody has measured.
- *One note per prototype also means a note the walk must RETRACT.* The case that forced it is below, under the review of this decision: the tier arm writes its packless note before the custom cost is read, and a typed pack list makes that note false. The retraction clears the slot rather than handing it back to a note `noteOn` shut out earlier, because the notes that could have been shut out there were composed about the same price the retraction is deleting.
- *Neither gate walks a clamp or a packless fallback.* The mirror walks a dropped pack and the in-game gate walks neither; the clamp, the tier fallback and the packless refusal are held by unit tests in both halves. Adding a clamp row to either gate would mean an example recipe declaring 40000 of something, which is content rather than a fixture, and that is a decision rather than an amendment.
- *`CostOf` still has no ladder.* The review's (c) suggested giving `CostOf` the fallback shape `CostBy.Fallback` and the pack lists already have, so an absent or unusable source degrades onto a declared alternative rather than refusing. That is additive surface and mirrors a shape the library already carries twice; this round did not take it, and it is the one thing that would empty the `CostOf` row out of the exception table.

### The adversarial review of decision B, and the nine things it moved

A read-only adversarial review of the working tree found three must-fixes, six should-fixes and four notes. All of them were taken. The rule, the exception table and the two measurements are unchanged; what moved is what the code and the documents SAY about them, plus three real defects.

**THE COMPOSER CAME BACK AS A FACT, WHICH IS A DEPARTURE FROM THE BRIEF'S LETTER AND IS THE HEADLINE.** Deleting `withFallbackNote` outright left the composed description of every text setting promising something that no longer happened: "the reason is in the log, or in the load error if the load stops anyway", in a setting's own tooltip, which is one of the three places a player looks. The objection finding 14 raised was to the unreachable SCREEN ROUTE and not to the fact the sentence carried beside it. So `fallbackFact` / `fallback_fact` decorates the same four post-resolution exits with `. The stored value of <setting> could not be used, so the mod's own declaration applied.` (reworded by fix round 3's decision 2, as above) and nothing more, the composed description is true again UNCHANGED (`textFallbackLine` and `TEXT_FALLBACK_LINE` are byte for byte what they were at `21f5d89`, checked with `cmp`), and the two source comments that had been edited to assert the opposite now name the composer that makes them true. `res.fellBack` / `fell_back` gets its second reader back, which three documents had credited it with while it had only one.

**A NOTE THAT SAID THE MOD'S COST APPLIED WHILE THE PLAYER'S PACKS DID.** A technology declaring both `CostBy` and `CostFrom` (the example guest's `hardened-tips` shape), on a mod set where the tier's source loses every pack, wrote `packlessSourceLine` and `packlessSourceNote` BEFORE the custom cost was read; a player who had typed a pack list then got a tooltip saying the mod's own declared cost applied while the packs, and possibly the count and the seconds, were theirs. The walk already knew the case, because it cleared `res.packless` in exactly that branch. The fix is the narrow one: `costTier` / `CostTier::Chosen` carries the tier's `source`, and the typed-list branch retracts the ERROR line and the note it can now name. `retractLog` / `retract_log` removes the first exactly-matching line; `retractNote` / `retract_note` clears the slot only when it holds exactly that sentence. The drop lines stay, because they are true.

**THE REFUSAL NOW NAMES THE FIRST THING THAT WENT MISSING.** Both halves carried a test comment saying the packless refusal names every rung the walk asked about, copied pack first. It did not: `markPackless` / `mark_packless` was fed the fallback ladder's `tried` alone. The tier's `filtered.dropped` names are prepended in the tier's packless branch, so the sentence reads `none of automation-science-pack, military-science-pack, space-science-pack is a science pack here` where it used to drop the first name. Each name appears ONCE, in first-seen order, and the rule is applied in exactly ONE place per half: `markPackless` / `mark_packless`, the single writer of the only state the sentence is composed from. It cannot live in any producer, because four callers feed that state and none of them can see another's list: the copied unit's dropped packs, the declared ladder's rungs, and the merge of the two all pass through unfiltered and are deduped on the way in.

**THE CLASSIFICATION TEST DID NOT CATCH A MISLABEL, WHICH THE REVIEWER PROVED BY RELABELLING `checkResolvedPacks`.** The only thing keying a `declaration` row to reality was "may not name a `World`", and a check that takes a resolution rather than a `World` passes that whatever it is labelled. Three holes are closed in both halves. A SECOND, independently written list (`refusalPolicy` / `REFUSAL_POLICY`) pins every row's class, its exception and its site exceptions in order, and it is grouped BY CLASS, so a relabel has to carry the row out of one group and into another where a reviewer sees it; `TestTheTwoClassificationListsAgree` / `the_two_classification_lists_agree` holds the two to each other in both directions and on length. An environmental row's exception is now checked for being the RIGHT one and not only for membership in the four. And `CLASS_MIXED` with an empty `sites` list is refused outright, along with a mixed row whose site count is not strictly less than its refusal count; the one row that legitimately needed it, Rust's `resolve_custom_cost`, gets a class of its own (`CLASS_DECLARATION_IN_WORLD`) which is enforced BOTH ways: it must name a `World`, or it is plain `CLASS_DECLARATION` and is held to that check. Go gains `classNotARefusal` for `fallbackFact`, which builds an `errors.New` that carries no sentence of its own; Rust never matched it, because its needles are the `Err` constructors.

**THE ONE DELIBERATE DIVERGENCE-AVOIDANCE IS NOW TESTED IN BOTH HALVES.** `askableName` exists so the Go half declines to ask `ToolExists` about a pack name the Rust half cannot ask about, and only Rust pinned it. `TestACopiedUnitKeepsAPackNameThatIsNotText` is the Go twin: it asserts the entry survives with its bytes and that NOT ONE line is written. Deleting `askableName` makes the Go half drop the pack, lose every pack in the copy and refuse the plan, while Rust emits it.

**THE FOUR CHEAP NOTES.** `copiedPackName` and `copied_pack_name` disagreed about a map entry whose `name` key is not a string: Go walked past it and could match a later duplicate key, Rust returned on the first. Go now returns on the first. An `ingredients` key that is PRESENT and is not an array was passed through unfiltered in both halves, which is the one shape "a form you cannot decode is not a licence to pass it through" did not cover; it is now refused with the same `copiedUnitRefusal` sentence an undecodable ENTRY earns, with a case per half. Go marked a technology packless under `!tier.has && len(entries) == 0`, which structurally includes the typed-list path while Rust marked it only in the `(None, None)` arm; the Go arm now names the path (`parsed.isDefault && len(entries) == 0`), so the halves agree structurally instead of agreeing because the language refuses an empty typed pack list. And the fluid clamp's emitted amount renders as `1.0000000000000001e301` while the note and the log line spell the ceiling `1e301`. They are the SAME double: `maxFluidAmount` / `MAX_FLUID_AMOUNT` is the literal `1e301`, and both halves' transcript renderers print a non-integral double with 17 significant digits, which is what that value's nearest double needs. A reader comparing a tooltip or a log line to a transcript, a golden or an engine dump should expect the two spellings of one number and not go looking for an arithmetic error.

**THE TWO FIELD DOCS THIS ROUND HAD MADE FALSE.** `resolvedTech.hasUnit` said it was false only for `CostOf`, whose unit was copied verbatim at emit; `CostOf` now resolves and filters a unit, so `hasUnit` is true for all four cost shapes and false only where no cost was named. `ResolvedTech::unit` said both it and `max_level` were `None` for every shape but `CostBy`; `unit` is now `Some` for all four and `max_level` is still the CostBy answer alone, because a `cost_of` reads its source's cap at emit.

**THE TWO ENUMERATIONS THAT CLAIMED COMPLETENESS.** `docs/usage.md`'s "what stays a refusal is enumerated rather than open-ended" omitted `validate`'s two named-prototype probes, so a modpack removing the entity a `PlaceResult` names stopped the load while the list said it would not; both are named now, with the reason they are on it. `CLAUDE.md`'s "By name, those are ..." omitted `PlanData`'s two host-wiring sites, which its own table classifies as environmental.

**README.md WAS NOT IN THE DIFF AND CARRIED THREE STATEMENTS THIS ROUND MADE FALSE**: the deleted screen-route promise, `CostOf` taking the source's whole `unit` unchanged, and "a science pack that does not exist" in the refusal list. All three are corrected, and the degrade rule and the tooltip note get a bullet of their own. Its size figures are dated measurements against a named `fklua` head and were not re-taken.

**A LUA CEILING THE JUMP ROW DOES NOT TRACK, found by the mirror gate refusing to load the module.** Wiring `fallbackFact` back into `PlanData` as four decorated statements made the packaged Go guest fail to COMPILE under lua52f: `fk_data_module.lua:71842: function or expression too complex near 'v23'`, inside `F[148]`, which the module's own comment names `(*github.com/Techrocket9/fkrecipes/go.Lib).PlanData`. That function had 166 `v` locals plus its temporaries, and an indirect call passing 37 arguments; Lua 5.2 allows 200 locals and 250 registers per function, and the jump row the gate prints tracks a DIFFERENT limit (the 18-bit jump offset, which the relay works around). `resolve` inlines into `PlanData`, so every local the walk gains lands there. The fix is to keep the whole post-resolution gate in one `//go:noinline` function, `afterResolution`, and call it once: the carried refusal, the three checks and the decoration all move out of `PlanData`. It also bought headroom rather than spending it, measured by the gate's own jump row: 1,017,018 bytes across 14 relay stations before, 967,223 across 11 after. The lesson for a later round is that `PlanData` is the function to keep thin, and that the symptom is a Lua line number with no Go symbol in it. THE RUST HALF HAS NO TWIN OF `afterResolution` AND NEEDS NONE: its `plan_data` lowers to 61 percent of the jump limit with no relay at all, and the constraint is TinyGo's inlining into one wasm function rather than anything about the design. The two halves still emit the same bytes in the same order, which is what the mirror compares.

**THE RED PROOFS, each break made in the source, the designed failure observed, and the break reverted.**

| What was broken | What went red |
|---|---|
| `fallbackFact` / `fallback_fact` returns the message undecorated | Go `TestPlayerFieldsFallBackWhileTheAuthorChannelStillRefuses` and three more; Rust `player_fields_fall_back_while_the_author_channel_still_refuses` and three more. Go's message: `got: ... is a science pack here` / `want: ... is a science pack here. The stored value of steelworks-forging-time could not be used, so the mod's own declaration applied.` |
| the retraction in the typed-list branch is skipped | Go `TestATypedPackListTakesBackTheTiersPacklessSentence` and the Rust twin, on the ERROR line reappearing and on `localised_description=["", "This game has none of the science packs the steel-processing cost names, so this mod's own declared cost applies. ..."]` beside a unit priced in the player's own `logistic-science-pack` |
| `markPackless` / `mark_packless` fed the fallback's `tried` alone | Go `TestATierWhoseFallbackIsAlsoUnpayableStillRefuses` and the Rust twin: `automation-science-pack` missing from the head of the list |
| `checkResolvedPacks` / `check_resolved_packs` relabelled `classDeclaration` with its exception dropped, which is the reviewer's own exploit (fix round 3 deletes that function; the same exploit is available on any row that takes a resolution rather than a `World`) | Go `TestTheTwoClassificationListsAgree` and Rust `the_two_classification_lists_agree`, naming both the class disagreement and the exception disagreement |
| `askableName` deleted | Go `TestACopiedUnitKeepsAPackNameThatIsNotText`: `plan refused: fkrecipes: the technology hardened-tips has no science pack the game has; ...` (at fix round 3 the same break loses the pack instead of refusing, and the test's assertion moved with it) |
| the non-array `ingredients` arm restored to `continue` | Go and Rust `a_copied_unit_whose_pack_list_is_in_neither_form_is_refused`: the plan was accepted |

**GATES AFTER THE REVIEW, exit codes read directly, all 0:** `gofmt -l .` (no output), `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./...`, `go/examples/notext` `go vet .`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo test`, `RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --workspace`, `scripts/run-mirror.sh` and `scripts/run-ingame.sh`. NO GOLDEN MOVED. The mirror's stand-in does not reach a technology that declares both `CostBy` and `CostFrom` over a packless tier, and the in-game guests do not either, so the retraction and the longer packless sentence are held by unit tests in both halves and by nothing else; the two shapes are recorded as an open item rather than added as example content.

### Decision D: a composed description survives a locale key the game does not define

**WHAT CHANGED.** Every locale key this library composes goes out in the engine's alternatives form, `{"?", {"section.key"}, "raw"}`, and never as the bare `{"section.key"}` it used to emit. There is one writer per half now, `localeRef` (`go/customize.go`) and `locale_ref` (`rust/src/settings.rs`); the Rust half inlined the key at five sites before this and calls the helper at all five now, so a change to a key's shape is one edit in each half. The three key shapes and their raw fallbacks: `mod-setting-description.<full>` falls back to the emitted setting name, `string-mod-setting.<full>-<value>` to the dropdown value itself, `technology-name.<source>` to the technology's internal name. A cost preset whose label key is missing now reads `\n<value>: cost of <source>` instead of costing the whole tooltip.

**WHY, MEASURED.** Finding 15 of the consumer's second assessment, graded MISLED, reproduced on Factorio 2.0.77 build 84539 this cycle by two independent arms: a headless `localised_print` from `on_init` under `--create`, and a client probe standing on the Mod Settings screen. Both agree, and the facts are cited rather than re-derived here:

- `{"?", {"a-section.a-key-nobody-defines"}, "raw fallback"}` renders `raw fallback`.
- `{"a-section.a-key-nobody-defines"}` with no `?` renders `Unknown key: "a-section.a-key-nobody-defines"` through `localised_print`, and on a SETTING it deletes the info icon and the whole tooltip; on a RECIPE it deletes the entire description block, the library's own literal sentences with it. Exit 0, no engine warning, no `fkrecipes: ` line, and the settings dump says the description is present. No headless gate could have seen it.
- On the client, beside finding 15's own control in ONE screen: every `?` row renders and the plain join does not. **An undefined key counts as a FAILED alternative**, which is the crux the form rests on.
- `?` takes any number of alternatives and returns the first that resolves. A nested `?` is itself an alternative and resolves inside out. A raw fallback is literal text, `__1__` included; an empty one renders empty, not a marker.
- **A plain string alternative always resolves**, so a raw string anywhere but last short circuits everything after it; and when every alternative fails the result is the LAST alternative's `Unknown key: "..."` marker. Both reasons put the raw fallback last.
- **Cost:** the wrapper occupies exactly ONE parameter slot of its parent, the same as the bare key table it replaces, and costs exactly ONE level of depth. Against the ceilings corrected one commit earlier (20 parameters per table, 20 levels of depth, no global table count) the theoretical preset ceiling drops from 342 to 323, and a 323-preset description carrying 647 wrappers both loads and resolves in full. (Decision E's wrap line takes one more top-level parameter slot on an INGREDIENT dropdown, so that kind's ceiling is 322 from decision E onward; a cost dropdown's stays at the 323 measured here.) A recipe prototype carries the same parameter ceiling with its own refusal text, `Error while loading recipe prototype "probe-sweep" (recipe): Too many parameters for localised string: 21 > 20 (limit).`

**THE LOCALE CHECKER: REQUIRED INSIDE THE PREFIX, ADVISORY OUTSIDE IT.** The round's brief asked for every referenced consumer key to be REQUIRED; the design review narrowed it and the narrowing is binding, for a reason this checker already knows. Factorio's locale namespace is flat and shared, so defining `technology-name.logistics-2` in a consumer's `.cfg` sets the displayed name of BASE's technology for every mod in the game, and the checker has a flat-namespace collision scan for exactly that hazard. Requiring an out-of-prefix key would be requiring what the same checker flags, and with the wrapper in place a missing one is not a defect at all.

So `mod-setting-description.<full>` and `string-mod-setting.<full>-<value>` stay REQUIRED, with no change to those findings, and `technology-name.<source>` becomes an ADVISORY, returned by `CheckLocaleAdvisories` / `check_locale_advisories` and by NOTHING ELSE (the review below moved it out of the checker's return and is where that decision is recorded). One per referenced out-of-prefix key in composition order, reported whether or not the consumer's file defines it, byte-identical in both halves:

```
note: the dropdown setting <full> composes the game's own key <key>, which this plan does not own; where the game does not define it the tooltip shows <raw> instead, and defining it here would rename it for every mod
```

**AN ADVISORY IS NOT A FINDING, AND A SEPARATE ACCESSOR IS WHAT MAKES THAT TRUE.** This commit first appended the advisories after the required findings inside `CheckLocale`, under a `localeAdvisoryCap` of their own; the review below took them out of that return entirely, for a reason the ordering could not fix. They keep the cap and its closing line (`(and N more advisories)`, singular arm included), which now simply belongs to a report of its own. A dropdown naming one key twice says the same sentence twice, so it is said once: the walk dedupes on the produced sentence, which is exactly per (dropdown, key).

**WHAT THE ADVISORY DELIBERATELY DOES NOT DO.** It does not ask for an entry, it does not go away when the file supplies one, and it reads nothing from the author's file at all. Both halves have a witness for the last of those: a `.cfg` carrying `[technology-name]` entries for both composed keys produces exactly the same two lines.

**THE RED PROOFS.** Each break was made in the source, the designed failure observed, and the break reverted.

- *Every composed reference is wrapped.* Broke `localeRef` / `locale_ref` to return the bare key table. Go's `TestEveryComposedLocaleReferenceIsWrapped` and Rust's `every_composed_locale_reference_is_wrapped` went red naming every reference: `the composed reference ["mod-setting-description.steelworks-quench-medium"] is a bare key rather than the alternatives form`, and the same for a `string-mod-setting` and a `technology-name` key.
- *The raw fallback is LAST.* Swapped the two alternatives in both halves, which is the defect the measurement names: a raw string short circuits, so the key would never be consulted. Both tests went red three times over on the same wrapper: `a raw fallback sits before the last alternative, which short circuits every alternative after it`, `a wrapper does not end in a raw fallback, so a game defining none of its keys renders the last one's Unknown key marker`, and the bare-key line, because with the key in the last slot it is no longer at the wrapper's slot 1.
- *The advisory exists.* Made `checkLocale` / `check_locale_inner` drop the advisories. Go went red on `TestCheckLocaleMatchesTheGolden`, `TestCheckLocaleAcceptsACompleteFile` and `TestCheckLocaleAdvisesOnAKeyTheGameOwns`; Rust on `check_locale_matches_the_golden` and `check_locale_advises_on_a_key_the_game_owns`.
- *The advisory comes LAST.* Prepended the advisories to the findings instead. Both halves' golden tests went red with the two notes at the top, and the Go witness added `finding 0 is an advisory sitting among the required findings` and `defining the game's keys moved the advisories`.
- *An in-prefix key stayed REQUIRED.* Deleted the per-value `[string-mod-setting]` requirement in both halves. Both golden tests went red with `the dropdown setting fkrecipes-example-quench-medium has no [string-mod-setting] entry for its value oil` gone from the report and the two advisories still there, which is the distinction stated as a diff.
- *The gate's own new assertion.* `run-ingame.sh`'s "every composed locale key rides in the alternatives form" row was red-proven against the recorded dump rather than against the engine, because the input is the dump: `jq 'walk(...)'` unwrapping one `technology-name` reference takes the two counts to 20 and 18, and reordering every wrapper takes them to 20 and 0. Both make the row false.

**THE GATES SEE THE SHAPE NOW.** `scripts/run-mirror.sh`: the three greps that pinned a composed description were rewritten to the wrapped bytes; one grep pins a whole wrapped reference on its own (`{1="?",2={1="mod-setting-description.fkrecipes-example-rivet-ingredients"},3="fkrecipes-example-rivet-ingredients"}`); and a loop refuses the three bare shapes by name (`{1="",2={1="mod-setting-description.`, `3={1="string-mod-setting.`, `5={1="technology-name.`), each of which is present only in the bare form and absent from the wrapped one. `scripts/run-ingame.sh`: the row that pinned the cost dropdown naming its technology now names all three slots of the wrapper, because the key table still sits INSIDE the wrapper and an assert that only looked for it would have gone on passing after a regression; the ingredient-preset row walks one level further in; and a new row counts every section-prefixed string in every `fkrecipes-example-` description against the ones sitting at a wrapper's slot 1 under a wrapper ending in a string, which catches both the bare key and the reordered wrapper.

**WHAT MOVED IN EACH GOLDEN, AND WHY.**

- `testdata/mirror/transcript.golden` was re-recorded: thirteen lines, every composed description in the transcript, because every key in one grew from a one-element table to a three-element one. Nothing else in it moved.
- `testdata/ingame/dump-sha256.txt`: the SETTINGS hash moved on BOTH rows, from `b4491942...` to `6d43d3a3...`, because the setting prototypes changed. **Neither DATA hash moved**, which was checked before the golden was re-recorded and is the expected result: the default row is still `e824cd83...` and the flipped row still `8cf5985e...`, since no recipe or technology prototype carries a composed key. `testdata/ingame/flipped.golden.dat` did not move and was compared byte for byte before any engine ran, as it is on every run.
- `testdata/locale/findings.golden` DID NOT MOVE IN THE END. This commit first added the two advisories to it; the review below took them back out along with the change that put them there, so the file at the head of this commit is byte for byte the file it was before. `testdata/locale/example.cfg`'s header says where the advisories live instead and why no edit to that file makes them appear or go away.
- `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` did not move and could not have: no message the language builds changed here.

**WHAT IS STILL OWED.** Whether the client's settings tooltip renders the library's own wrapped composition AT ITS REAL SIZE is unmeasured. The client probe measured PROBE shapes (an 18-parameter description with a `?` at depth 2 and an inner join at depth 3 rendered in full, and a 323-preset description loads and resolves), not the example guest's own emitted descriptions: nothing has hovered `fkrecipes-example-tips-research-tier` on a client since the wrapper landed. The `notext` size figures are NOT owed any more: they are re-taken in the review pass below, which also corrects the mechanism this paragraph first guessed at.

**GATES, exit codes read directly, all 0:** `gofmt -l .` (no output), `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./...`, `go/examples/notext` `go vet .`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo test` (222 lib tests and 2 integration tests, up from 220), `RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --workspace`, `scripts/run-mirror.sh` (OK; the Go jump row reads 967,022 bytes of widest pre-relay span over 11 stations, down from the 967,223 over 11 the previous commit recorded, and the Rust one 397,040 with no relay; block room unmoved at 314,648 for Go and 323,877 for Rust), `scripts/run-ingame.sh` (OK on Factorio 2.0.77 build 84539, re-asked with `"$FACTORIO" --version`, `FACTORIO_USERDIR` at its `/tmp/fkrecipes` default, 13 to 14 seconds per language for three engine runs). The repository-wide dash sweep returns only `agents/docs-style.md` quoting the characters it bans.

#### The review of decision D

A read-only adversarial review of the working tree found two must-fixes, five should-fixes and four notes. Every one was taken; three of them found something the review had only suspected.

**THE ADVISORY LEFT THE CHECKER'S RETURN ALTOGETHER (must-fix 1), which is narrower than the review's own suggestion and better.** `docs/migration.md` step 4 tells a consumer to run `CheckLocaleWith` from their own suite and that "it should be clean", and the pilot follows that literally, with `for _, finding := range Plan().CheckLocaleWith(...) { t.Error(finding) }`. An advisory in that return is therefore a PERMANENT RED TEST over a key the consumer is explicitly told not to define, which is the MISLED shape this round exists to close, reintroduced one layer up. Ordering the advisories last does not fix it, and neither does a second cap. The decisive observation is that an advisory does not depend on the .cfg at all: it is reported whether or not the file defines the key, so it is a property of the PLAN and never of the locale file.

So `CheckLocale` and `CheckLocaleWith` are back to their contract, an empty return means a clean file, and the advisories are `CheckLocaleAdvisories(modName string) []string` / `check_locale_advisories(&self, mod_name: &str) -> Vec<String>`. Same sentences, same order, same dedupe, same cap with the same closing line; no cfg parameter, because there is no file to read. Nothing is re-exported in `rust/src/lib.rs`: both `check_locale` and `check_locale_with` are inherent methods on `Lib`, which `lib.rs` already exports, so the new one is reachable the moment it exists. `testdata/locale/findings.golden` is byte for byte what it was before this commit, and the two sentences are pinned as an IN-TEST EXPECTATION in each half (`advisoryNotes`, `ADVISORY_NOTES`) rather than in a third copy on disk: the halves already have to agree with each other byte for byte, and a golden would only add a place to forget. `testdata/locale/example.cfg`'s header says where they went.

**THE NORMATIVE DESIGN SECTION STILL SPECIFIED THE BARE KEY (must-fix 2).** `agents/customizer-design.md`'s "Semantics both halves must share" gave all three composed references as bare key tables, which is the shape this commit exists to eliminate; the "Orderings and shapes" bullets below it had been updated and this had not. A normative section and a dated record are different things, so this one is rewritten to the wrapped form outright and points at decision D.

**THREE MEASURED DEPTH FIGURES WERE INVALIDATED BY THIS COMMIT AND ONE COMMENT WAS FALSE (should-fixes 3 and 4).** Re-measured out of the engine's own settings dump on 2.0.77, both languages' dumps answering identically:

```sh
jq 'def depth: if type=="array" then ([ .[] | depth ] + [0] | max) + 1 else 0 end;
    ."string-setting"[$k].localised_description | {params: (length-1), deep: depth}' \
  --arg k fkrecipes-example-tips-research-tier tmp/ingame/normalised-settings-go-1.json
```

| shape | parameters at the top | tables deep | parameters per preset line |
|---|---|---|---|
| a text setting's description | 5 | 3 | n/a |
| an ingredient dropdown's | 4 | 4 | 3 |
| a cost dropdown's | 4 | 4 | 4 |

`agents/customizer-design.md`'s client-round paragraph said 5 and 2, 3 and 3, and 3 and 3, and closed "the preset shapes are unchanged in both counts"; the sibling bullet under Orderings had been corrected by this commit and the paragraph had not, so the file contradicted itself on a measured number. Corrected in place, with what it used to say named, because it is the paragraph a later reader would act on. `go/customize_test.go` said "a one-preset description is three table levels deep either way", which is now four, and Go asserted NOTHING about depth at all: the Rust twin's `deepest_table` was updated by this commit and had no Go counterpart, so the wrapper's entire measured cost, one level and nothing else, was pinned in one half. `deepestTable` is the Go twin, and the flat cost description is asserted at four.

**THE CAP AND THE DEDUPE HAD NO TEST (should-fixes 5 and 6).** Both are now held per half, on the new accessor: `TestCheckLocaleAdvisoriesAreCapped` / `check_locale_advisories_are_capped` walks 150, 101 and 100 distinctly-sourced choices under one dropdown, which is all it takes to reach the cap, and pins the singular arm at the boundary; `TestCheckLocaleAdvisoriesSayARepeatedKeyOnce` / `check_locale_advisories_say_a_repeated_key_once` puts one source under two tiers and asserts the deduped pair in composition order, the repeated key at its FIRST position.

**THE TWO GUARDS ON THE ADVISORY WALK ARE FIXED AND TESTED, AND THE FIX ALONE WAS NOT ENOUGH (should-fix 7).** The review preferred consolidation over tests: have the advisory walk consult the predicate the required-description rule already encodes, rather than spell the technology arm a third time. That is done, and it is the better structure, so it stays: `costDropdownComposesPresetLines` / `cost_dropdown_composes_preset_lines` is the ONE spelling, read by the composition (`settingDescriptions`, `presets_beside_text`), by the checker's required-description rule and by the advisory walk. But the reasoning behind preferring it, that the guards are then exercised by the required-description findings, is FALSE AND WAS MEASURED SO: with the three spellings reduced to one, dropping `validPacksSetting`, dropping `validDropdownSetting` and dropping the `namedCostSources` clause each left `go test ./...` green. No fixture in either suite declares a technology whose cost dropdown carries a handle the plan never issued. So the tests were added as well, one per half, over a handle from ANOTHER `Lib`, asserting both that no advisory is produced and that no description is required for a preset list nobody composes.

**THE DATA-STAGE HALF OF THE WRAPPER TEST WAS VACUOUS IN BOTH HALVES (note 3 of the review), which is more than the note claimed.** It asserted only that the composed-key counts are zero, so it would stay green if the data stage stopped composing descriptions altogether; the review asked for a witness that the walk saw at least one `localised_description`. Adding that witness turned BOTH halves red on the spot. Neither fixture composed a single data-stage description: Go's `steelworksSettings()` under `customWorld()` and Rust's `every_composed_shape()` under `base_world()` each declare no `Description` and degrade nothing, so every assertion in that half was over an empty walk. The two halves also used DIFFERENT fixtures for one property, which is the review's fourth note; both are answered at once by aligning them. `everyComposedShape` is now the Go twin of `every_composed_shape`, declaration for declaration, against the same two worlds, and both carry a literal `Description` on the recipe and on the technology, which is exactly the shape decision C fixed and exactly what the zero counts are about.

**`op_values` IS EXHAUSTIVE NOW.** Its `_ => Vec::new()` wildcard would have silently skipped a fourth `Op` variant carrying a `Value`, in a walk whose whole claim is that it sees everything both stages emit. `Op::Log(_)` is named instead, so a new variant is a build error.

**THE `notext` SIZE FIGURES MOVED, AND THIS FILE'S STATED MECHANISM WAS WRONG FOR THREE OF THE FOUR COLUMNS.** Decision D said `locale_ref` "adds a `?` literal and a third element to every reference, which ships in every guest", which predicts growth everywhere. Re-taken with this round's own recipe (the block under "THE FOUR FIXTURES, RE-TAKEN AT THIS HEAD" above, with `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.6`, which the host's go1.27.1 now needs for TinyGo 0.41.1), `67b5090` against this working tree, both trees unpacked at equal-length 113-character paths so the source path TinyGo and rustc embed cannot read as a delta, `fklua` built from FkLua at `01d640a`, TinyGo 0.41.1, cargo 1.97.1, 2026-09-13:

| `notext` guest | wasm | `fk_data_module.lua` | lines |
|---|---|---|---|
| go | 752,339 to 749,241 (-3,098) | 2,800,538 to 2,805,419 (+4,881) | 70,246 to 70,507 (+261) |
| rust | 156,652 to 156,570 (-82) | 2,403,750 to 2,396,816 (-6,934) | 58,423 to 58,307 (-116) |

**THE REAL MECHANISM, confirmed rather than argued.** A `notext` guest COMPOSES NOTHING at run time: both dropdown arms of `settingDescriptions` require a text setting beside the dropdown (an `IngredientsSetting` on the recipe arm, a `PacksSetting` inside `CustomCost` on the technology arm), and a `notext` plan declares neither, so no wrapper is ever built and no description is ever emitted. `localeRef` is LINKED anyway, at both heads, because the gate is a RUNTIME branch over a value in the plan and whole-program elimination cannot read one: `strings -a` over the packaged Go wasm answers `github.com/Techrocket9/fkrecipes/go.localeRef` at `67b5090` and at this head alike, beside `settingDescriptions` and `textDescription`. That is exactly the mechanism this file already records for the three description prose constants, one section up. What moves the four columns is therefore CODEGEN AND NOT STRING DATA, and the two columns move independently because `fk_data_module.lua` is TRANSPILED Lua rather than an encoding of the wasm bytes: the Go wasm carries debug sections the transpiler never reads, which is why 749,241 bytes of Go wasm becomes 2,805,419 bytes of Lua while 156,570 bytes of Rust wasm becomes 2,396,816. `README.md` carries the two `fk_data_module.lua` figures and names the `fklua` head, the toolchains and the date; it does not carry the wasm column, which is path-length sensitive and comparable only within one pass.

**WHAT MOVED IN THE GOLDENS IN THIS PASS: NOTHING.** `testdata/locale/findings.golden` went back to its pre-advisory content, which is byte for byte the committed file, so it is not in the diff at all. `testdata/mirror/transcript.golden` and `testdata/ingame/dump-sha256.txt` are unchanged from where decision D left them, which is what both gates assert and what their file mtimes confirm: no composed description changed here, only where the advisory sentences are returned from. `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` and `testdata/ingame/flipped.golden.dat` did not move either.

**THE RED PROOFS, each break made in the source, the designed failure observed, and the break reverted.**

| What was broken | What went red |
|---|---|
| the advisories appended to `checkLocale` / `check_locale_inner`'s return again | Go `TestCheckLocaleMatchesTheGolden`, `TestCheckLocaleAcceptsACompleteFile` and `TestCheckLocaleAdvisories` (`CheckLocale over an empty file carries an advisory at 35: note: the dropdown setting ...`); Rust `check_locale_matches_the_golden`, `check_locale_advisories` and `check_locale_advisories_do_not_depend_on_the_file` |
| `composedGameKeyAdvisories` / `composed_game_key_advisories` returns nothing | Go `TestCheckLocaleAdvisories`, `TestCheckLocaleAdvisoriesDoNotDependOnTheFile` and `TestCheckLocaleAdvisoriesSayARepeatedKeyOnce`; Rust all four of the advisory tests |
| the dedupe replaced by a plain append | Go and Rust `..._say_a_repeated_key_once` alone, on the three-tier ladder whose first and third tiers name one source |
| the advisory cap skipped | Go `TestCheckLocaleAdvisoriesAreCapped`: `got 150 advisories, want the cap plus one closing line`; Rust `check_locale_advisories_are_capped` |
| the cap's singular arm deleted | the same two, at the boundary: `the closing line at the boundary is "(and 1 more advisories)"` |
| `validPacksSetting` dropped from `costDropdownComposesPresetLines` | Go and Rust `a_cost_dropdown_with_a_handle_from_another_plan_composes_nothing`, the packs arm, on BOTH assertions: an advisory appeared and `the dropdown setting mymod-tier has no [mod-setting-description] entry, and the library composes its preset list onto that entry` was required |
| `validDropdownSetting` dropped from the same predicate | the same test, the dropdown arm, the same two assertions |
| `appendLocalised` / `append_localised` stops emitting `localised_description` | Go and Rust `every_composed_locale_reference_is_wrapped`: `the data stage composed no localised_description, so the counts below prove nothing` |

**GATES AFTER THE REVIEW, exit codes read directly and never through a pipe, all 0:** `gofmt -l .` (no output), `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./...`, `go/examples/notext` `go vet .`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo test` (226 lib tests and 2 integration tests, up from 222), `RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --workspace`, `scripts/run-mirror.sh` (OK; the jump rows are unmoved, Go 967,022 bytes of widest pre-relay span over 11 stations and 314,648 of block room, Rust 397,040 with no relay and 323,877 of block room), `scripts/run-ingame.sh` (OK on Factorio 2.0.77, `FACTORIO_USERDIR` at its `/tmp/fkrecipes` default, 13 and 14 seconds per language for three engine runs, both rows matching the committed golden). The Go suite stands at 241 `func Test` and the Rust lib target at 226 `#[test]`. The repository-wide dash sweep returns only `agents/docs-style.md` quoting the characters it bans.

### Decision E: what the field accepts is written beside it

**WHAT CHANGED.** Two sentences, both composed, and neither of them a change to what this library emits into a recipe or a technology.

- `"\nA list too long for one line continues on the next; the continuation is part of the same list."` (`listWrapLine` / `LIST_WRAP_LINE`) goes onto every TEXT setting's description, directly under the `\ndefault: <rendered>` line, and onto an INGREDIENT dropdown's description, after the last preset line and before decision A's switch line.
- `" The word none empties the list, so the recipe costs nothing to craft."` (`ingredientNoneClause` / `INGREDIENT_NONE_CLAUSE`) extends the format line on an INGREDIENT text setting and on no other.

**ONE WAY OF COUNTING THE LINES, EVERYWHERE.** Three documents counted them three ways and each was internally consistent, which still left a reader reconciling "five lines", "four library sentences under the default line" and "five library lines" across `docs/usage.md`, `docs/migration.md` and this file. The count is now one sentence in all four places, this one included: A TEXT SETTING'S DESCRIPTION IS FIVE LINES, A DEFAULT LINE AND FOUR LIBRARY SENTENCES UNDER IT, and the four are named rather than numbered, as the wrap sentence, the format sentence, the switch sentence and the fallback sentence. Naming them is what killed an off-by-one that had already shipped: `docs/usage.md` listed five lines and then said "the second of those also names the word `none`", where `none` rides on the THIRD of that list and on the SECOND of `docs/migration.md`'s four. An ordinal into a list that is counted two ways is a defect waiting for the next edit.

`textDescription` / `text_description` and `textFormatLine` / `text_format_line` take one more argument for the second of those, a bool saying whether this is an ingredient list, and `guardedTextDescription` / `guarded_text_description` hands it back beside the switch line for the same reason the switch line is handed back: it is an input of the composition that the checker's rule cannot recompute from the table it is given.

**THE SENTENCE SAYS "THE CONTINUATION" AND NOT "BOTH LINES", AND THAT IS A SPEC DEFECT CORRECTED RATHER THAN A REWORDING.** The brief handed this commit the literal `"\nA list too long for one line continues on the next; both lines are one list."` and it shipped that text; the round's own review found the arithmetic wrong. TWO IS NOT A BOUND ON ANYTHING HERE. The wrap threshold is near 57 to 60 characters, measured on the client; the list the default line renders is the AUTHOR'S OWN DECLARED LIST, which has no bound short of the language's 2000-character parse ceiling, so three and more visual lines are reachable; and on an ingredient dropdown the line above the rendered list is the consumer's own LOCALISED LABEL, which can wrap on its own and is not a list at all. A player who trusted "both lines are one list" over a list that wrapped twice would copy two of three lines and drop the tail, which is the exact failure the sentence exists to prevent. The text is now `"\nA list too long for one line continues on the next; the continuation is part of the same list."`, byte-identical in both halves, and it names the RELATIONSHIP a continuation has to what it continues instead of counting lines no composition can count. It is 95 bytes where the first text was 77.

**WHERE THE WRAP LINE GOES, WHICH IS NARROWER THAN "BOTH COMPOSED TOOLTIPS".** The brief for this commit read the disclosure as belonging to a dropdown's description and to a text setting's description. In the code it belongs wherever a TYPEABLE LIST is rendered, which is three of the four composed shapes and not all four: both kinds of text setting (an ingredient list and a pack list both render a list into the `default:` line), and an INGREDIENT dropdown, whose preset lines are `  type: <rendered list>`. A COST dropdown does NOT carry it. Its preset is a localised label followed by a localised technology name, one vocabulary throughout with nothing in it to copy, which is the same reason that preset stayed on one line when the ingredient preset became two (the client round above). A sentence there would be about a hazard that preset does not have, and both gates assert its absence there by name.

**WHY, MEASURED.** Finding 5 of the consumer's second assessment, graded AWKWARD, measured on the client: a preset line too long for the tooltip is wrapped by the engine, and THE CONTINUATION STARTS AT THE LEFT MARGIN rather than under the line it belongs to. It therefore reads as a line of its own, and a player who copies what looks like a whole line loses the last ingredient. The wrap is the engine's and no composition can change it. Under the threat model's rule for an engine behaviour (scope K) it grades CLEAN once disclosed where a player looks, and the settings tooltip is where a player looks.

Finding 18, graded MISLED: the word `none` empties an ingredient list (`go/ingredientlist.go`, `rust/src/ingredient_list.rs`), the emitted recipe carries `ingredients: {}`, the engine's derived recycling recipe goes with it, and no description, changelog or README named the word. It is a legitimate thing for a player to want, so it is kept and said out loud. It is NOT said on a packs setting, whose own parser refuses it with `research takes at least one science pack`: a library that told a player to type a word it turns down would have replaced one MISLED with another.

**THE ONE-LINE PRESET SHAPE WAS REJECTED, AND THE ARITHMETIC IS WHY.** The assessment's remedy for finding 5 was to put the label and the typeable list back on one line as `<label>: <typeable list>`. That is the exact shape the client round removed, and its stated rationale ("shorter lines wrap less") is backwards. On the assessment's own example the proposed line is 71 characters where today's indented `  type: ...` line is 66, against a wrap threshold measured near 57 to 60. A longer line wraps at least as often, and it has MORE comma boundaries for a wrap to land on. That matters because a truncated copy is only SILENTLY wrong when the cut lands on a comma boundary: cut anywhere else it ends in an amount with no name, which the language refuses loudly. So the two-line shape stays and the wrap is disclosed instead.

**WHAT IT COSTS AT THE CEILING, RE-CHECKED.** One parameter slot of the top table, on the shapes that carry it, and no level of depth: the sentence is a plain string. Measured out of the engine's own settings dump at this head, both languages' dumps answering identically (`jq 'def depth: if type=="array" then ([ .[] | depth ] + [0] | max) + 1 else 0 end; ."string-setting"[$k].localised_description | {params: (length-1), deep: depth}' --arg k <setting> tmp/ingame/normalised-settings-go-1.json`):

| shape | parameters at the top | tables deep | at the decision-D head |
|---|---|---|---|
| a text setting's description, either kind | 6 | 3 | 5 and 3 |
| an ingredient dropdown's | 5 | 4 | 4 and 4 |
| a cost dropdown's | 4 | 4 | 4 and 4 |

Against the measured ceilings (20 parameters PER TABLE, 20 LEVELS of depth, no global table count) the only shape that can reach a ceiling is a dropdown with many presets, and there the cost is exactly one preset: an INGREDIENT dropdown is flat up to 17 presets where it was 18, and nests past that exactly as it did before, since `localisedGroup` / `localised_group` keeps 19 and hands the rest to a nested group in slot 20. A COST dropdown is unchanged at 18. A text setting's description spends 6 of 20 and is nowhere near anything. Both halves now pin the ingredient dropdown's fill point, including which parameters the level hands on first: at eighteen presets the nested group holds exactly the wrap line and the switch line, and at nineteen it holds a preset line as well. **[AMENDED by fix round 3's decision 5, 2026-09-14: the ladder line spends a second slot, so an ingredient dropdown is flat up to 16 presets and the seventeenth nests; the two tests are `TestIngredientDropdownDescriptionNestsPastSixteenPresets` / `an_ingredient_description_nests_past_sixteen_presets` under those names now, and the nested group at seventeen holds the ladder line and the switch line.]**

**THE DRIFT GUARD HAS A FOURTH ROW, AND THE FORMAT LINE IS NOW PER KIND.** `composedTextLinesMissing` / `composed_text_lines_missing` reports `the library composes no line about a list that continues on the next line onto a text setting's description; ...` first, in the composition's own order, and asks for `textFormatLine(ingredients)` rather than a constant. A rule handed the wrong kind sees a line it does not recognise and reports the format line missing, which is what the new per-kind test asserts in both halves; a guard that recomputed the kind instead of being handed it would have agreed with itself and seen nothing.

**THE RED PROOFS, each break made in the source, the designed failure observed, and the break reverted.**

| What was broken | What went red |
|---|---|
| `listWrapLine` / `LIST_WRAP_LINE` dropped out of `textDescription` / `text_description` | Go 19 tests: five prototype goldens, the order test, the guard's own (`the real composition reported [the library composes no line about a list that continues on the next line ...]`) and twelve `CheckLocale` ones, because the guard's finding goes first in every report. Rust 12, the same shape: seven locale tests, three prototype goldens and two order tests |
| the wrap line dropped from the INGREDIENT dropdown's composition | Go `TestPlanSettingsComposesADropdownDescription`, `TestTheSwitchLinesFollowTheEmittedOrder` and `TestIngredientDropdownDescriptionNestsPastSixteenPresets`, named `...PastSeventeenPresets` when this row was written (`sixteen presets did not stay flat`); Rust the three twins |
| the `none` clause never appended, so an ingredient text loses it | Go 8: the five prototype goldens, the order test, the guard's own and `TestTheComposedTextLinesAreTheStatedOnes`. Rust 7, the same shape |
| the `none` clause appended to EVERY text setting, so a packs text gains it | Go 4: `TestPlanSettingsEmitsAPacksSettingAndAnEmptyList`, `TestOrderAfterPlacesGeneratedSettingsUnderALegacyOrder`, the guard's own and `TestTheComposedTextLinesAreTheStatedOnes` (`a packs setting's format line names the word none: ...`). Rust 4, the same shape. THE TWO BREAKS ARE EACH OTHER'S CONTROL: dropping the clause reddens the ingredient goldens and adding it everywhere reddens the packs ones, so no single edit satisfies both |
| the wrap line ALSO pushed onto a COST dropdown | Go `TestPlanSettingsComposesACostDropdownDescription` and `TestCostDropdownDescriptionNestsPastNineteenPresets`; Rust `plan_settings_composes_a_cost_dropdown_with_a_custom_arm` and `a_cost_preset_with_no_source_reads_as_the_fallback` |
| `guardedTextDescription` / `guarded_text_description` told the wrong kind | Go `TestTheDriftGuardInspectsTheFirstTextSettingOnly` (`the guard was told the first text setting is not an ingredient list`); Rust `the_drift_guard_inspects_the_first_text_setting_only`. WHAT ELSE GOES RED DEPENDS ON WHICH HALF OF THE PAIR IS BROKEN, and the row's first draft reported one of the two breaks as if it were the only one. Invert the kind INSIDE the accessor, so the composition is built and reported with the same wrong flag, and nothing else goes red at all: the two agree and `CheckLocale` stays clean. DESYNC them instead, handing `composedTextLinesMissing` / `composed_text_lines_missing` the opposite flag to the one the composition was built with, and twelve Go `CheckLocale` tests and six Rust ones go red on the format-line finding. Both breaks were taken and reverted at this head. So this test is a CONTRACT PIN on the accessor's output rather than the sole detector of a wrong kind, and it is what covers the first break, which nothing else sees |
| `scripts/run-mirror.sh`, the wrap row, proven against the real run | with the dropdown's wrap line dropped: `FAIL: an ingredient dropdown does not disclose that a list may continue on the next line`, exit 1 |
| `scripts/run-mirror.sh`, the packs rows, proven the same way | with the clause on every text setting: `FAIL: the packs setting's composed description is not in the transcript`, `FAIL: the packs setting fkrecipes-example-chain-packs names a word the library refuses there` and the same for `fkrecipes-example-tips-packs`, exit 1 |
| `scripts/run-ingame.sh`, all five new rows, proven AGAINST THE RECORDED DUMP rather than against the engine, because the dump is the input | each row returns `true` over `tmp/ingame/normalised-settings-go-1.json` and `false` over a `jq`-doctored copy of it: the clause spliced onto `fkrecipes-example-chain-packs`, the wrap line deleted from `fkrecipes-example-rivet-ingredients`, the wrap line deleted from `fkrecipes-example-quench-medium`, the wrap line appended to `fkrecipes-example-tips-research-tier`, and the clause stripped from the ingredient format line |

### The adversarial review of decision E, and the eighteen things it moved

A read-only adversarial review of the working tree found two must-fixes, nine should-fixes and seven notes. All were taken. ONE OF THEM CHANGED AN EMITTED SENTENCE and the rest are prose, a table row and a list item, which is the shape a closing commit's review should have: the round's code was right and what the round SAID about it was not, in eighteen places.

**THE ONE THAT MOVED BYTES: the wrap sentence was arithmetically wrong, and it came from the brief.** Recorded above under decision E. `"... both lines are one list."` becomes `"... the continuation is part of the same list."`, 77 bytes to 95, byte-identical in both halves.

**THE TWO MUST-FIXES.**

1. `docs/ingredient-list.md` still described the PRE-ROUND composition, four lines with no wrap line and no mention of `none`, in the one document a player-facing setting's own page points at. `docs/usage.md` and `docs/migration.md` were rewritten in this commit and that paragraph was not. Rewritten to the five lines the composition emits, with the `none` clause named on an ingredient setting and explicitly not on a packs one.
2. **The adoption list would have had the consumer ship six false tooltips.** Item 8 said no `[mod-setting-name]` or `[mod-setting-description]` entry becomes an ORPHAN and stopped there, which is true about KEYS and reads as a statement about the file; item 5 has the consumer withdraw the `custom` value and delete the one test that pinned the word. At BBB `1caaf08`, ten entries of `mod-data/locale/en/better-belt-balancer.cfg` go FALSE the moment the value is withdrawn, and NOTHING IS RED ANYWHERE: the checker reads keys and not strings, `--dump-data` carries the key and not the string, and BBB's own suite checks one entry's first sentence. A consumer following the list exactly ships settings tooltips telling the player to pick an option that does not exist, which is the MISLED shape this whole round exists to close, arriving through this library's own migration instructions. Worse, the second assessment moved finding 6 to CLEAN partly on the strength of one of those sentences. New adoption item 9 names all ten entries by line and says what each must become; `docs/migration.md`'s withdrawal paragraph gains the same instruction for every consumer, because every consumer who shipped the value has the hole and not only the pilot.

**THE NINE SHOULD-FIXES.** Three were claims that were not true. Ownership row 4 said "every value the screen shows is in force", which a fallen-back text contradicts on the screen the row is about; row 3's "no design of ours reaches it" is true for the pilot and false for any consumer who already shipped a dropdown value it withdraws, which `6518971`'s own note says; row 16's "A removed this library's path and not the path" is stronger than the code, because `CraftTimeFrom` is still a `DoubleSettingRef`. One piece of the "the six CLEAN were not regressed" evidence was not real at all: the bullet for finding 1 claimed the in-game flipped row asserts a merged ingredient list out of a real engine's dump, and `grep -c 'amounts are added\|in the list twice'` returns 0 over both gate scripts. Every other bullet in that list was re-checked against the scripts the same way and every one of them holds. The other five are stale prose the same commit left behind, swept for rather than taken from the list: the drift guard's opening sentence said three lines over four paragraphs that say four, in both halves; `textDescription`'s doc opening said three things where `FIVE PARAMETERS` and `THE FOUR LINES` beside it had been updated, in both halves; `localised_group`'s "nineteen presets or fewer is flat" was wrong twice over (it is 17 for an ingredient dropdown and 18 for a cost one) and had no Go twin at all, so both halves now carry the same corrected paragraph; `agents/customizer-design.md` gave the top-level parameter list without the wrap line or the switch line in it and said two sentences and two lines where there are four; the Go twin of a Rust comment corrected in this commit still said three; `rust/examples/datastage/src/lib.rs` named the withdrawn dropdown value; and decision D's `342 to 323` preset ceiling is now stated per kind, 323 on a cost dropdown as measured and 322 on an ingredient one by the wrap line's one parameter slot. The sweep also found four comments in the Rust suite still describing a dropdown's `Custom` arm, which has no API behind it, and one open item the closing list had lost.

**THE SEVEN NOTES.** The `none` disclosure was silent on the second-order effect the assessment measured by name, so `docs/ingredient-list.md`'s `none` row says that an empty list has no recycling recipe either; the tooltip clause is left short. Adoption item 10 gains `plan_test.go:117-119` and `:170`, which transcribe the two allowed-value lists as literals ending in `"custom"`, so `TestEverySettingPrototypeIsTheOneThatShipped` moves with item 5 and not only the compiler. Ownership row 8 gains a BBB share for the changelog sentence the assessment's remedy (h) asks for. Row 15's qualification is restored and row 16's phrasing fixed as above. The drift guard's red proof was reframed: the row claimed "Only a test of the guard's INPUT can see it", and BOTH BREAKS WERE TAKEN AND REVERTED at this head to settle it, with the result that a CONSISTENT break (the kind inverted inside the accessor, so the composition is built and reported with the same wrong flag) reddens that one test and nothing else, while a DESYNC (the rule handed the opposite flag to the one the composition was built with) reddens twelve Go `CheckLocale` tests and six Rust ones. So it is a contract pin that covers the first break, not the sole detector of a wrong kind. And three documents counted the composed description's lines three ways: there is now one count in all four places, five lines, a default line and four library sentences under it, and the four are NAMED rather than numbered, which is what killed an off-by-one `docs/usage.md` had already shipped.

**WHAT THE LONGER SENTENCE COSTS, MEASURED AS A CLEAN A/B.** 18 bytes of literal (77 to 95) against the two `notext` fixtures, both halves built and packaged at the SAME path in both arms, so no path-length correction is needed and none was applied: the pre-review text was restored in `go/customize.go` and `rust/src/settings.rs` alone, both guests built and packaged, the text put back, and both built and packaged again. `fklua` from FkLua at `01d640a` (the binary `scripts/run-mirror.sh` had just built), TinyGo 0.41.1, cargo 1.97.1, `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.6`, 2026-09-13.

|---|---|---|---|
| go | 750,500 to 750,520 (+20) | 2,809,608 to 2,811,252 (+1,644) | 70,615 to 70,639 (+24) |
| rust | 157,040 to 157,056 (+16) | 2,402,399 to 2,402,411 (+12) | 58,453 to 58,453 (unmoved) |

The BEFORE row reproduces the two module figures `README.md` carried before this review EXACTLY, bytes and lines, in both languages, which is what says this pass is comparable to the one that recorded them even though its paths are a different length: the MODULE column is not path sensitive and the wasm column is, and the wasm figures above are therefore comparable only with each other and not with decision E's table. `README.md` now carries the AFTER pair and `705 bytes of string data` where it carried 687, which is the same 18 bytes counted in the other place it appears. THE GO MODULE MOVES EIGHTY-TWO TIMES WHAT THE GO WASM DOES, and that is the packager rather than the compiler: 18 bytes of string shift the rodata blob TinyGo emits and the Lua the packager writes for it re-lines around the shift. `strings -a` finds `A list too long for one line` once in each packaged `notext` wasm, before and after, so the sentence still ships in a guest that declares no text setting, exactly as decision E recorded.

**THE RED PROOFS.**

| What was broken | What went red |
|---|---|
| `listWrapLine` / `LIST_WRAP_LINE` changed in the source alone, before any pin was updated | Go 7: `TestPlanSettingsEmitsATextSetting`, `TestPlanSettingsEmitsAPacksSettingAndAnEmptyList`, `TestPlanSettingsEmitsALegacyTextSetting`, `TestPlanSettingsComposesADropdownDescription`, `TestTheSwitchLinesFollowTheEmittedOrder`, `TestTheComposedTextLinesAreTheStatedOnes`, `TestOrderAfterPlacesGeneratedSettingsUnderALegacyOrder`. Rust 8, the same shape plus `an_empty_declared_ingredient_list_reads_as_none` and `an_ingredient_description_nests_past_sixteen_presets`, named `...seventeen...` when this row was written. THIS IS THE PROOF THAT THE PINS ARE PINS: the sentence is a constant in one place per half and nineteen assertions across the two suites see it move |
| the drift guard's kind inverted INSIDE `guardedTextDescription` / the Rust accessor | Go `TestTheDriftGuardInspectsTheFirstTextSettingOnly` alone; Rust `the_drift_guard_inspects_the_first_text_setting_only` alone |
| the same kind DESYNCED, `composedTextLinesMissing` / `composed_text_lines_missing` handed the opposite flag | Go 12 `CheckLocale` tests including `TestCheckLocaleMatchesTheGolden` and `TestCheckLocaleAcceptsACompleteFile`; Rust 6 including `check_locale_matches_the_golden`. Both breaks reverted |

### The round's ownership table

A row per finding this round touched, its grade in the consumer's second migration assessment, and who owns the outcome AFTER this round. THE OWNER IS WHOEVER CAN CHANGE THE OUTCOME BY CHANGING THEIR OWN SOURCE, and a finding may have two owners with the split stated. This table is the chain's stopping rule: the chain stops when an assessment reports nothing above CLEAN that is in scope and owned by FkRecipes, BBB or FkLua.

| # | was | owner after | why |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | BLOCKED | FkRecipes, closed; engine, residual; the consumer for a withdrawal it ships | the `custom` value is withdrawn and this library adds no value of its own to a dropdown, so an older release has nothing left to reset. The engine's silent reset of a stored value the running release does not offer is the engine's, and it cannot be told a withdrawal from an addition. "NO DESIGN OF OURS REACHES IT" IS TRUE FOR THE PILOT AND NOT IN GENERAL, and the qualification belongs in the row: WITHDRAWING A DROPDOWN VALUE IS A DESIGN OF THIS LIBRARY'S THAT REACHES THE ENGINE'S SILENT RESET, for any consumer who has already shipped that value in a public release, which is 6518971's own note above. It costs BBB nothing because `custom` never shipped in one; for a consumer who did ship one it is a real loss of a stored preference and belongs in a release note |
| 4 | MISLED | FkRecipes, closed; one residual, disclosed on the prototype | the state the screen could not render no longer exists to be rendered: a field that is not at its default overrides, per field for a cost. THE ROW MAY NOT SAY "every value the screen shows is in force", and its first draft did. A text the library cannot USE falls back to the dropdown's CURRENT PRESET while the field goes on showing the player's typo, so there is a value on the screen that is not in force, and the screen is stating the rule rather than the state, which is finding 4's own structure one case narrower. What closes it is decision C rather than construction: the emitted recipe's or technology's own `localised_description` carries a trailing line naming the setting that fell back, so the disclosure sits on the prototype the player hovers and not only in a log they never open, and `textFallbackLine` says the same thing in the setting's own description before the fact |

A cost dropdown does not, because it renders no list to copy, and that is the row where the brief's "both composed tooltips" is narrower in the code than in the brief. Choosing short option labels is the consumer's, and `docs/usage.md` now says so with the measured threshold |

BBB HAS A SHARE AND THE ROW'S FIRST DRAFT OMITTED IT. Its changelog already discloses that changing the recipe empties an assembler, and what it does not disclose is the NEW way to change it that this round creates: a typo in the text field now falls back instead of refusing, so the recipe can move without the player ever touching the dropdown. That is the assessment's own remedy (h), it is one sentence, and it is the consumer's string to write |

The invisible-character half of the finding is closed and is FkRecipes' |

The load no longer stops on the DEFAULT setting because a modpack demoted a science pack |

Decision A made this library's own generated RESEARCH cost settings ints, so the route it used to price research through is gone; a consumer's own double setting and any other mod's still reach the abort. THE ROW MAY NOT SAY "A removed this library's path and not the path", because it did not remove all of this library's: `CraftTimeFrom` is still a `DoubleSettingRef` (`go/lib.go`, `RecipeSpec`), so the library's only route for a player-settable crafting time is still a double, and "Turning the crafting time into a text setting" below says why that stays. **THE SIX THE ASSESSMENT ALREADY GRADED CLEAN (1, 2, 6, 7, 11, 12) WERE NOT REGRESSED, and this is what in the gates says so.**

- **1, the ladder that landed twice on one name.** The merge rule is unchanged and nothing in this round touched the resolver's arithmetic. `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` did not move across the four commits, and the merge and cap lines are pinned in `go/packladder_test.go` and `go/data_test.go` (15 assertions between them) and in `rust/src/tests/data.rs` (15). THE ENGINE CLAUSE THIS BULLET FIRST CARRIED WAS NOT REAL and is struck: NO GATE IN THIS REPOSITORY REACHES A MERGE. `grep -c 'amounts are added\|in the list twice' scripts/run-ingame.sh scripts/run-mirror.sh` returns 0 for both files. What the in-game flipped row actually asserts about a ladder is a DROPPED RUNG, twice (`none of tungsten-carbide, titanium-plate is present, so the ingredient is dropped`, and the preset's own oil ladder), which is a different arm of the same resolver. The merge is host-tested in both halves and unwitnessed by any engine, which is worth knowing rather than worth papering over: a merge that the engine refused anyway would not be caught here.
- **2, no text a player types refuses the load.** The in-game gate's flipped row installs a pack text the language refuses and asserts the run EXITS 0 with the research priced on the mod's own declared packs and EXACTLY ONE `fkrecipes: ERROR: ` line in the engine's log (the count is asserted, not the presence); the mirror does the same for a refused ingredient text. Decision B widened the property rather than narrowing it, and the source-property test in both halves now pins the class of every refusal site.
- **6, the promise is scoped and both sides were measured.** The composed descriptions are pinned byte for byte in both halves, in the mirror transcript and in the in-game dump; this round added to them and re-pinned all three places in the same commit, so a sentence that drifted would fail in three gates rather than none.
- **7, the prerequisite no longer moves.** `scripts/run-ingame.sh`'s `the prerequisite splice reached logistics-2` row, and BOTH data hashes unmoved at this commit, which is the stronger statement: no prototype this library emits changed at all.
- **11, the build's own claim.** BBB-owned. Nothing in this repository can regress it and nothing here touched it.
- **12, every stored value migrates.** `testdata/ingame/flipped.golden.dat` did not move, it is `cmp`'d before any engine runs, and the gate reads the file the ENGINE rewrote back with `fklua modsettings read` and asserts every value the flipped file installed survived. That row passed on every run of this commit.
