### What BetterBeltBalancer must do to adopt this

BBB is Go only and consumes this library by `replace`, so it compiles against whatever the sibling checkout holds. This list is the whole of what the four commits cost it. It is written from BBB's sources at `1caaf08` read read-only; nothing in that repository was changed.

**Declarations that stop compiling.** All four are in one function, `tune.Plan()` in `guest/go/tune/plan.go`.

1. `IngredientChoices.Custom` is gone. `IngredientChoices` is now `{Setting, Choices}`; the text setting moves to `RecipeSpec.IngredientsFrom`, which may now sit BESIDE `IngredientsBy`. BBB's `Custom: recipeIngredients` becomes `IngredientsFrom: recipeIngredients` one level up, in the `RecipeSpec` literal.
2. `CostChoices.Custom` is gone. `CostChoices` is now `{Setting, Choices, Fallback}`; the `CustomCost` moves to `TechSpec.CostFrom`, beside `CostBy`.
3. `CustomCost.Position` is gone entirely. There is no replacement and none is needed: a custom cost is no longer a dropdown value that has to be placed in the tree, so `TechOptions()` is no longer read by the library at all.
4. `CustomCost.Seconds` now takes an `IntSettingRef`. BBB's `techSeconds` is a `DoubleSettingRef` from `lib.DoubleSetting(TechSecondsName, 15, ...)`, and that call becomes `lib.IntSetting`. `DoubleSetting` itself stays in the library and is still the right constructor for a crafting time.

**Plan-time refusals, which are load failures rather than build failures, so they arrive after the compiler is happy.**

5. The choice list must cover the dropdown's `allowed_values` EXACTLY, with nothing subtracted, because this library adds no value of its own to a dropdown. BBB's `RecipeValues()` is `append(RecipeOptions(), RecipeCustom)` and `TechValues()` is `append(TechOptions(), TechCustom)`, seven values against six choices and four against three. Both `custom` values come out of both lists, and the two consts (`RecipeCustom`, `TechCustom` in `recipe.go` and `tech.go`) go with them. Without this the plan refuses with `the recipe bbb-balancer-part offers nothing for the value custom that the setting bbb-recipe-cost allows` and its technology twin.
6. Beside a `CostBy`, a research count or seconds setting must declare default 0 and minimum 0, because 0 is what means "the dropdown decides". BBB declares 20 with minimum 1 for the count and 15 with minimum 1 for the seconds. Both become `Between(0, ...)` with default 0, and the refusal if they are not is `the setting <name> backs a research count beside a research dropdown, so its declared default and its minimum must both be 0 (0 means the dropdown decides)`.

**Stored values that change meaning.** Nothing a player has ever stored is lost. `custom` has never shipped in a public release of BBB and the four generated settings have never shipped under any name, so withdrawing the value costs no player anything; the nine preset values across the two dropdowns all survive by name. What DOES change meaning is the pair of research numbers: today an untouched `better-belt-balancer-tech-count` means 20 units and an untouched `-tech-seconds` means 15 seconds, and after the change an untouched field means "the tier above decides". A player who had deliberately set 20 and 15 keeps those numbers and they now count as an override of the tier, which is the same behaviour by a different route. `better-belt-balancer-tech-seconds` also changes PROTOTYPE TYPE, from `double-setting` to `int-setting`: measured on 2.0.77, a stored non-integral double under a setting redeclared as an int is truncated toward zero and then range checked, with the declared default (not a clamp) applying out of range, and nothing is logged in any path. `docs/migration.md` carries that measurement.

**Suite assertions that move.**

7. `TestTheLocaleFileSatisfiesThePlan` in `guest/go/tune/locale_test.go` iterates `Plan().CheckLocaleWith(...)` and calls `t.Error` on every line. That contract is UNCHANGED and deliberately so: `CheckLocale` and `CheckLocaleWith` still mean "empty is clean". **The new `CheckLocaleAdvisories` / `check_locale_advisories` accessor MUST NOT be wired into that test or any test that fails on any returned line.** It returns a note for every out-of-prefix key the plan composes, whether or not the locale file defines it, it never goes away, and the note tells the consumer NOT to define the key. A suite that failed on it would be a permanently red test over something the library says not to fix.
8. The two orphan locale entries. Withdrawing `custom` makes `bbb-recipe-cost-custom` and `bbb-tech-cost-custom` in `[string-mod-setting]` of `mod-data/locale/en/better-belt-balancer.cfg` match no dropdown value the plan declares, and the checker reports `the [string-mod-setting] entry <key> matches no dropdown value this plan declares` for each, which `TestTheLocaleFileSatisfiesThePlan` turns into two errors. Delete both lines. `TestEveryCustomDropdownDescriptionNamesTheCustomOption` reads those same two entries and takes everything before the first `:` as the word it then requires in the dropdown's own description, so it goes with them. No `[mod-setting-name]` or `[mod-setting-description]` entry becomes an ORPHAN: all four generated settings survive the change, only their binding site moves. That is a statement about KEYS and the item stops one sentence short if it is read as a statement about the file, which is what item 9 is for.
9. The locale prose that goes FALSE with the value, which no gate anywhere can see. The checker reads locale KEYS and never the strings beside them, `--dump-data` carries the key and not the string, and BBB's own `go test ./tune/` checks one entry's first sentence and nothing else, so ten entries in `mod-data/locale/en/better-belt-balancer.cfg` (read at `1caaf08`) go on instructing the player to pick an option the dropdown no longer offers, with nothing red anywhere. SIX DESCRIPTIONS: `bbb-recipe-cost` (`:197`) offers "or Custom to write the recipe yourself in the setting below" and later "Every option but Custom is safe in an overhaul pack"; `bbb-tech-cost` (`:219`) offers "or Custom to write the cost yourself in the three settings below" and later "On Custom it sits after Logistics"; and `better-belt-balancer-recipe-ingredients` (`:218`), `better-belt-balancer-tech-packs` (`:236`), `better-belt-balancer-tech-count` (`:241`) and `better-belt-balancer-tech-seconds` (`:242`) each open by scoping the field to "while the recipe/research setting above is set to Custom". AND FOUR NAMES: `better-belt-balancer-recipe-ingredients` (`:130`) is "Custom balancer part recipe", and `-tech-packs`, `-tech-count` and `-tech-seconds` (`:143`, `:144`, `:145`) are "Custom balancer research: ...". What each must become is one rule applied ten times: THE TEXT FIELD APPLIES WHENEVER IT DOES NOT SAY `default`, AND THE DROPDOWN SUPPLIES THE REST. A dropdown's description drops the option from its list and says instead that the setting below it takes over whenever that setting does not say `default`; a field scoped "while the setting above is set to Custom" says instead that it applies whenever it is not at its default; a field NAMED for the option is named for what it holds ("Balancer part recipe", "Balancer research: science packs"). THIS IS THE MISLED SHAPE THIS ROUND EXISTS TO CLOSE, arriving through this library's own migration instructions, and the round's second assessment moved finding 6 to CLEAN partly on the strength of one of those very sentences, which makes leaving it off the list worse than an omission. `docs/migration.md`'s withdrawal paragraph now says the same thing for every consumer, because every consumer who shipped the value has this hole and not only the pilot.
10. `wantSettings()` in `guest/go/tune/plan_test.go` transcribes all six setting prototypes field by field and `TestEverySettingPrototypeIsTheOneThatShipped` compares them with `reflect.DeepEqual` while rejecting unexpected keys. IT ALSO TRANSCRIBES THE TWO DROPDOWNS' ALLOWED-VALUE LISTS AS LITERALS ENDING IN `"custom"`, at `plan_test.go:117-119` for `bbb-recipe-cost` and `:170` for `bbb-tech-cost`, each under a comment block naming the value as the seventh and the fourth, so item 5's withdrawal moves this test too and not only the compiler. Four of its helpers encode a composition shape that has changed: `localeKey` emits the BARE `{"section.key"}` form where every composed reference now goes out as `{"?", {"section.key"}, "raw"}`; `textFieldLines()` carries two of what are now FOUR library sentences under the default line (the wrap line and the switch line are new, and the format line gains the `none` clause on the ingredients setting and not on the packs setting); `presetLine` and `costPresetLine` need the dropdown switch line after the presets and, on the INGREDIENT dropdown only, the wrap line before it. The two numeric settings are transcribed with no description at all and now carry a composed `numberDescription`, so the unexpected-field check fires on `localised_description` until they are transcribed.
11. `TestTheCustomResearchDefaultsAreTheFallbackUnit` asserts the two numeric defaults are `FallbackUnit()`'s 20 and 15; under item 6 both become 0.
12. The behaviour tests that drive `withStartup(SettingRecipeCost, RecipeCustom)` or `withStartup(SettingTechCost, TechCustom)` in `guest/go/tune/plandata_test.go` are driving a value the dropdown no longer offers. Each becomes a test that sets the TEXT instead and leaves the dropdown on a preset. Two of them pin sentences that no longer exist at all: `TestAnEditedTextUnderAPresetIsIgnoredAndTheLogSaysSo` and `TestAnEditedPackTextUnderATierIsIgnoredAndTheLogSaysSo` pin `... is edited, but ... is not on custom, so the text is ignored`, and nothing is ever edited and ignored now. Two more, `TestThePositionLadderStepsUpAndThenLetsGo` and `TestTheCustomArmTakesItsPlaceFromTheDefaultTier`, are about `CustomCost.Position` and have no API behind them any more.
13. The Python gate, `test/check-datastage.py`: `RECIPE_CUSTOM_ARMS`, `TECH_CUSTOM_ARMS` and `TECH_IGNORED_ARMS` drive the withdrawn value and pin the ignored-field sentences, and `OUR_SETTINGS_ORDERS` pins the six `(name, order)` pairs, which do not move. `test/datastage-goldens.json` carries `mod_settings_sha256` per engine and per mod set: the composed descriptions changed, so every `mod_settings_sha256` row must be re-captured. `data_raw_sha256` should NOT move on the default configuration for the same reason it did not move here, and if it does that is a finding rather than a re-capture.
14. `fixtureWorld` in `guest/go/tune/world_test.go` embeds `fkrecipes.UnimplementedWorld`, which is what keeps a `World` growth a compile error naming the missing method rather than a silent `false`. `World` did not grow in this round, so the embed costs nothing here; it is named because it is the mechanism that will report the next growth.
15. Four comment blocks assert the removed API by name and read as false after the change: the `Custom`-is-the-seventh-value and `Custom`-is-the-fourth-value blocks and the whole `Position` section in `plan.go`, and the closing sentences of `recipe.go` and `tech.go` that name `IngredientChoices.CustomValue` and `CostChoices.CustomValue`.

**And one thing BBB no longer has to do.** Remedy (f) of its own second assessment, "Define `technology-name.logistics-2` and `technology-name.logistics-3` in this mod's own locale file, or stop composing the cost lines from technology names, until the library closes finding 15", is UNNECESSARY and its first branch is now actively wrong. Commit `e4604d4` closed finding 15 in the library: an undefined `technology-name` key degrades to the technology's raw internal name and the tooltip survives whole. Defining those keys in BBB's own `.cfg` would set the displayed name of BASE's technologies for every mod in the game, which is the hazard this library's own flat-namespace collision scan exists to report, and `CheckLocaleAdvisories` says so in one sentence per key.

### What this round decided against

- **The one-line preset shape** (`<label>: <typeable list>`, the assessment's own remedy for finding 5). Rejected on arithmetic: 71 characters against today's 66, on the assessment's own example, against a wrap threshold measured near 57 to 60. A longer line wraps at least as often and has more comma boundaries for a wrap to land on, and a comma boundary is the only cut that produces a silently wrong list rather than a loud refusal. It is also the exact shape the client round removed, for a defect measured on a client. The wrap is disclosed instead.
- **`Lib.Verify` / `Lib::verify`.** Not built, and nothing was built toward it. The threat model puts a later-loading mod rewriting this plan's prototypes out of scope (J), and a verify could not move finding 17's grade in any case: it logs, and THE LOG IS NOT WHERE A PLAYER LOOKS. It could not even see the case it is named for, because mods within `data-final-fixes` run in dependency order and one sorting after the consumer can delete the recipe after the verify ran; and `data.raw` carries no provenance, so a discrepancy line could name the prototype and what changed but never the mod that changed it. Two implementation costs were priced and not paid: in Rust a second data-family call self-refuses by design, so a verify entry point needs either a new `StageKind` variant (a breaking change for any consumer matching on a public enum that is not `#[non_exhaustive]`) or an entry point that does not go through `plan_data`; and reading back what was emitted needs new `World` questions, which grow additively through panicking defaults, so a consumer fixture would compile and then PANIC AT RUN TIME the first time `Verify` asked something it had not implemented.
- **Turning the crafting time into a text setting.** Considered while decision A moved the research numbers to ints and refused: a crafting time is one number with a floor the engine enforces, and a text setting would buy a parser, a renderer and a refusal vocabulary for a field that has no list in it. The double setting stays, and with it the consumer's own exposure to finding 16. `docs/usage.md` now names that exposure in the narrow measured form: the engine's range check turns a stored NaN down for an `int-setting` and accepts it for a `double-setting`, this library prices nothing through a double any more, and a double a consumer declares is the consumer's.
- **Withdrawing `DoubleSetting`.** The design review asked for "no double-typed setting anywhere" and the narrowing is recorded with decision A: the rule is a BINDING rule, not a surface rule. This library no longer generates a double setting for anything it prices, but a consumer's own double is the consumer's to declare, and removing the constructor would not remove the engine's NaN abort, which any other mod's double reaches too.
- **Emitting a packless research unit.** Measured rather than argued, headless on 2.0.77: a unit with no science packs LOADS with exit 0 and no engine line, `add_research` returns true, and the research COMPLETES after count times time ticks in any lab, even an empty one, consuming nothing. So a packless unit is not stuck, it is free, and emitting one silently would be a balance change the player did not choose. The packless degrade lands on the declared cost behind it instead, with its own ERROR line.

### What this round leaves open on this side

**From the previous round's open list.**

- *The two lengths are not tied together* (`res.recipes` against `l.recipes`). CARRIED FORWARD unchanged. This round did not make the overshoot unrepresentable and did not need to.
- *The Rust source property's one hole* (`Resolution { recipes: lists, ..Default::default() }`). CARRIED FORWARD unchanged.
- *The `notext` size numbers were stale.* CLOSED again at this head: all four `notext` figures are re-taken at `e4604d4` against this working tree above, and `README.md` carries the two module figures with the `fklua` head, the toolchains and the date. The claim is the same narrow one: nothing else in this file was re-taken with them.
- *Option A's patch is in a scratch tree and nowhere else.* CARRIED FORWARD unchanged.
- *One load-failure message still interpolates a stored value unescaped* (`readDropdown` / `read_dropdown`). CARRIED FORWARD, and REFRAMED by decision A: withdrawing the `custom` value does not widen the path in, because the engine still resets an out-of-list stored value before any stage runs, so the only route is a hand-edited `mod-settings.dat` and it stays out of scope by H. It remains the one message in this library that a NUL can cut in half, and it is still one line to fix.
- *Below the cliff the engine deletes part of the player's text and persists it.* CARRIED FORWARD as engine-owned, and finding 10's row above records that the assessment's remedy is unimplementable from a guest. The smaller thing that subsection proposed, saying it in the composed description, was NOT taken here and is not obviously right: the composition now carries five lines, a default line and four library sentences under it, and a fifth sentence about a cliff the player cannot see would spend tooltip on a case a hand edit reaches.
- *Nothing in this repository's gates sees what a consumer's suite sees.* CARRIED FORWARD, and this round is the largest evidence for it yet: every gate here is green at every commit of the round while the adoption list above runs to fifteen items, one of which (item 9) is a locale file's prose that no gate in either repository reads at all. `docs/migration.md` is still the only answer and it is still a document rather than a mechanism.
- *The wrap and the label truncation are unanswered.* REFRAMED rather than closed. The LABEL truncation is still unmeasured on a client and still the consumer's string. The WRAP is no longer unanswered as a design question: it is engine-owned, it is disclosed in every composed shape that renders a typeable list, and `docs/usage.md` tells an author why a short option label buys the list room. What stays owed is the client render itself, below.

**What this round itself recorded as owed.**

- **The client render of the composed trailing line at its longest.** Decision C writes one sentence, and sometimes two, into a recipe's or a technology's own `localised_description`. Where that wraps in a real crafting tooltip and where the technology's wraps in the tech tree is unmeasured, and no gate here can see it: the dump carries the string whether or not it draws.
- **The client render of the example guest's own composed descriptions at real size.** The client probe that settled decision D measured PROBE shapes, not `fkrecipes-example-tips-research-tier`'s own emitted description, and nothing has hovered it since the wrapper landed. This commit adds a line to most of those descriptions, so the question is one line larger than it was.
- **One note per prototype, first in walk order.** `noteOn` / `note_on` keeps the FIRST note a prototype earns and drops the rest, which is deterministic and is what makes the emitted string the same every run. A recipe whose ingredient text AND whose crafting time both fell back therefore says one of the two things in its tooltip. The ERROR lines carry both, and the log is not where a player looks, so the case is real and unanswered. Whether the right answer is a second sentence, a merged one, or a rule about which fallback a player most needs to read is undecided.
- **Five shapes no gate in this repository walks, each recorded by the commit that found it and none of them carried into this list until the round's own review asked for them.** (a) A CRAFTING-TIME FALLBACK (`6518971`): both gates' only fallen-back prototypes are an ingredient text and a pack text, so decision C's scoping rule, that only a moved ingredient list earns the destruction sentence, is held by unit tests alone. (b) A MERGE, and a merge CLAMPED at a ceiling (`67b5090`): `grep -c 'amounts are added\|in the list twice' scripts/run-ingame.sh scripts/run-mirror.sh` returns 0 for both, and a clamp row would mean an example recipe declaring 40000 of something, which is content rather than a fixture. (c) A PACKLESS TIER FALLING BACK ONTO THE DECLARED COST (`67b5090`): the mirror walks a dropped pack and the in-game gate walks neither. (d) A TECHNOLOGY DECLARING BOTH `CostBy` AND `CostFrom` OVER A PACKLESS TIER (`67b5090`, recorded in that commit's gate paragraph and nowhere else): neither the mirror's stand-in nor either in-game guest reaches it, so `retractLog` / `retractNote` and the longer packless sentence are held by unit tests in both halves and by nothing else. (e) A `CostBy` LADDER WHOSE CHOSEN SOURCES CARRY NO UNIT, falling back to the declared fallback and then being written over field by field by the settings (`6518971`): `TestASettingOverridesTheFallbackTier` and `a_setting_overrides_the_fallback_tier`, and neither gate walks it either. All five are decisions about EXAMPLE CONTENT rather than about harness code, which is why three commits each recorded one and none took it; what they share is that the unit tests in both halves are the only witness, so a cross-language divergence in any of them would be invisible to the mirror.
- **`CostOf` still has no ladder.** Carried forward from decision B's own open list unchanged. The review's (c) there suggested giving `CostOf` the fallback shape `CostBy.Fallback` and the pack lists already have, so an absent or unusable source degrades onto a declared alternative rather than refusing. It is additive surface, it mirrors a shape the library already carries twice, and it is the one change that would empty the `CostOf` row out of `CLAUDE.md`'s exception table. This round did not take it.
- **Two fixture-only cosmetic gaps in the cost log line.** A tier unit carrying no `time` key prints `time 0`, with no `by formula` equivalent to print instead; a tier carrying neither `count` nor `count_formula` prints the count setting's declared default, which beside a dropdown is a 0 nothing in the emitted unit uses. Neither shape reaches an engine, because the engine refuses both units outright, so both are reachable only from a fixture `World`.
- **The FkLua checkout drift.** `rust/Cargo.lock` pins the fkdata dependency this crate compiles against at `b88965d`, while both gate scripts build the `fklua` BINARY out of whatever `FKLUA_CHECKOUT` holds, which is `01d640a` for every measurement in this round. `CLAUDE.md` says the two can drift and currently do. SYNCING THIS REPOSITORY ONTO THE NEWER HEAD IS OWED AS ITS OWN ROUND, and it is a round rather than a `cargo update` because every packaged-size figure in `README.md` is a figure for the head it was taken at, so the sync re-takes them.

## Fix round 2b

The trigger is the consumer's own fix round 2, BetterBeltBalancer's `agents/fkrecipes-migration.md` at that repository's commit `ea291ac`, which adopted this library at `221ff9c` and found, on a real engine, that the disclosure decision C added to stop a silent degradation had reintroduced the lock-out decision 2 exists to prevent. Three items, and the first is the round.

| item | owner | grade before | what closed it |
|---|---|---|---|
| A recipe-bound fallback note cannot be emitted at all: the composed sentence is over the engine's localised-string ceiling, so a player's typo stops the load with no dump | **FkRecipes**, scope B and F | BLOCKED | the splitter, this section |
| The packless refusal names a shared rung twice | FkRecipes | a wrong sentence, not a refusal | `f2df7ab`, the once-each rule at the single writer |
| `docs/migration.md` does not connect a copied cost dropping an unusable pack to the promise that a fallback is resolved only when used | FkRecipes | a documentation gap a consumer's green test fell through | the third commit of this round |

### The ceiling, re-measured here rather than taken from the report

**200 BYTES PER STRING ELEMENT of a localised string on a DATA-STAGE prototype**, on Factorio 2.0.77 build 84539, twelve runs on a throwaway probe mod that hangs a `localised_description` on prototypes base already ships. The key slot and every literal parameter are policed alike, and the refusal carries a 0-BASED element index: `Error while loading recipe prototype "iron-gear-wheel" (recipe): Localised string key is too large: 201 > 200 (limit). in property tree at ROOT.recipe.iron-gear-wheel.localised_description[1]`, exit 1, no dump. BYTES AND NOT CHARACTERS: 100 e-acutes is 100 characters and 200 bytes and loads, 101 is 202 bytes and refuses reporting `202 > 200`. NO AGGREGATE BUDGET: 995 bytes in six elements and 2985 in sixteen both load clean. `localised_name` is policed identically, and item, recipe and technology all carry it. The table in `agents/customizer-design.md` holds every row with the one shell command that re-takes it.

**AND A SETTING PROTOTYPE IS EXEMPT, WHICH IS THE MEASUREMENT THAT SCOPED THE FIX.** A `string-setting` whose `localised_description` and `localised_name` each held 201, 400, 1000, 2000 and 5000-byte elements exits 0 every time, with no message anywhere and every byte reaching `mod-settings-dump.json`. Without that negative the obvious move would have been to chunk both sides, which would have been wrong twice over: the settings side is where the locale checker lives, and `localisedCarries` / `localised_carries` looks for a whole composed line inside ONE `Str` element, so chunking a settings line would make `CheckLocale` report every composed line as missing and move `testdata/locale/findings.golden` over a rule the engine does not have.

**WHAT WAS OVER AND BY HOW MUCH**, with the name slot empty, measured at THIS commit: `fallbackNote` with the destruction tail is 208 bytes (229 since fix round 3's decision 2 reworded it), `clampedItemNote` with it is 229 and `clampedFluidNote` with it is 246, so all three are over before a name is a variable at all, and the smallest of them leaves MINUS EIGHT bytes for a setting name. The technology channel's three are 107, 84 and 123 and fit, which is why the defect reads as a recipe one; `packlessSourceNote` at 123 goes over at a 78-byte source name (77 lands exactly on 200, which loads), so the technology channel was not safe either, merely not doomed.

### The splitter, and why by construction rather than by shortening

One function per half, `chunkLocalised` / `chunk_localised` in `go/value.go` and `rust/src/value.rs`, and every literal this library writes into a data prototype's `localised_name` or `localised_description` goes through it. It fills elements to at most `localisedChunkBudget` bytes, cutting after the last ASCII space inside the budget, and falls back to a hard cut backed off to a UTF-8 character boundary for a word longer than the budget. The concatenation of the chunks is the input byte for byte, which is what makes this invisible to a player: the engine joins a localised string's parameters with nothing between them.

**TWO CONSTANTS AND NOT ONE, DELIBERATELY.** `localisedElementCeiling` is 200 and belongs to the ENGINE; `localisedChunkBudget` is 180 and belongs to this library. Keeping them apart means an off-by-one in the chunker cannot land exactly on a refusal, the UTF-8 back-off cannot approach the ceiling, and a later edit to one of these sentences has room before anything has to be re-chunked. The ceiling is compared against and never computed with, which is this repository's standing habit with a measured engine number.

**THE NESTING IS THE HELPER THAT ALREADY EXISTED.** `localisedGroup` / `localised_group` answers the measured 20-parameters-per-table ceiling by keeping the first nineteen and handing the rest to a nested group in the twentieth slot, and the data side calls it now. Its doc comment used to say the flat helper was used deliberately, on the reasoning that this composition is at most three parameters at one level; that reasoning is dead, because a consumer's own `Description` is unbounded and so is the chunk count, and the comment says the new reason instead. Capacity: 19*(d-1)+20 parameters at depth d, so 381 chunks at the engine's measured 20-level ceiling, which is 68,580 bytes of one description at 180 bytes a chunk. Past that it is a consumer's declared description that refuses, and it refuses on depth rather than on this rule.

**THE TEST IS A WALK AND NOT A LIST OF CASES.** `TestNoCompositionReachesTheElementCeiling` / `no_composition_reaches_the_element_ceiling` build a plan reaching all fourteen composed descriptions the library can produce, with 200-character names (the engine's own prototype-name ceiling) in every slot that reaches a sentence, then recurse into every emitted `Value` and assert that no `Str` is over 200 bytes and no localised table holds more than 21 elements. The fourteen are counted before the walk, so a fixture that stopped reaching a note fails loudly rather than passing vacuously. The largest composition proven is a 7200-byte author description beside the 409-byte fallback note on a 200-byte recipe name: **7609 bytes in two parameters becomes 45 chunks in a 21-element top-level table nested to depth 3**, every element at or under 180.

RED-PROVEN TWICE IN EACH HALF. Narrowing the asserted ceiling from 200 to 120 turns the walk red naming real elements and their byte counts, which says the walk reaches something. Raising the budget from 180 to 250 turns it red at 200 naming the composition that overflows, which says the splitter is what keeps it under rather than the sentences being short. A third proof is the mirror's, below.

### The two gates that could not see it, and what each gained

**THE STAND-IN NOW REFUSES WHAT THE ENGINE REFUSES.** `testdata/mirror/standin.lua` did nothing whatever about localised strings, which is why a byte-compared cross-language gate recorded an unloadable prototype as correct: the committed golden pinned a 243-byte element on `fkrecipes-example-steel-rivet`. `check_localised` walks `localised_name` and `localised_description` on every prototype that is not one of the four setting types, recursing into nested tables, and refuses over 200 bytes with the engine's own sentence and its own 0-based path. The four setting types are a LOOKUP TABLE and not a match on a trailing `-setting`: a spelling match would exempt a future prototype type with nobody deciding it, while a table stops being right loudly.

**ITS RED PROOF IS THE DEFECT ITSELF.** With the rule landed and `localisedChunkBudget` temporarily raised to 250, the note stays one 243-byte element and `scripts/run-mirror.sh` exits 1 on the first guest with `Error while loading recipe prototype "fkrecipes-example-steel-rivet" (recipe): Localised string key is too large: 243 > 200 (limit). in property tree at ROOT.recipe.fkrecipes-example-steel-rivet.localised_description[1]`. That is the committed golden's own prototype, refused by the gate that used to bless it.

**THE ENGINE GATE WALKS THE RECIPE CHANNEL NOW.** Its only refused text was a science-pack list, which is the TECHNOLOGY channel and loads; the refused ingredient list lived in the mirror, which has no engine in it. `testdata/ingame/flipped.json` puts `2 iron-plat` into `fkrecipes-example-quench-ingredients`, behind the `oil` preset, so the row carries one refused text per channel. The run exits 0, the quench recipe comes out with the OIL preset's own ingredients exactly as it would for a player who typed nothing, the log carries exactly two `fkrecipes: ERROR: ` lines, and the recipe's four description elements are pinned whole out of the dump. Both dumps also get a general assertion that no element of any localised string anywhere in them, base's own prototypes included, is over 200 bytes by `utf8bytelength`; that one does not have to be rewritten when a sentence moves.

**WHAT THE FLIPPED ROW TRADED, SAID PLAINLY.** It used to leave `quench-ingredients` untouched behind a non-default preset. That arm is gone here and is covered in the mirror instead, on the chain pair, where a dropdown decides through an untouched text. What replaced it is the harder case and the one the round is about: a dropdown deciding through a text the language REFUSED, which is the narrow property the whole player-fallback decision rests on, measured on a real engine for the first time.

### The goldens, and the one that did not move

- `testdata/mirror/transcript.golden`: two lines, the `extend#22` op and the `FINAL` snapshot, the 243-byte element becoming 172 and 71. Nothing else in the file was within 28 bytes of the budget, so nothing else moved.
- `testdata/ingame/flipped.golden.dat`: 689 to 755 bytes, one more stored value.
- `testdata/ingame/dump-sha256.txt`: the flipped row's data hash `8cf5985e3d860b84` to `77af9873b0edc726`.
- **THE DEFAULT ROW'S DATA HASH DID NOT MOVE**, `e824cd8302c00fd0` on both sides of this change, and the settings hash did not move on either row, `457e7d68e6fec8ce`. That is the claim in two numbers: a load where nothing fell back composes no note, so it emits what it always did, and the settings side was not touched at all.

### Gates

Every gate in `CLAUDE.md`'s Gates block, at every commit of this round: `gofmt -l` clean, `go vet`, `go test`, `go test -race`, the `notext` vet, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo test`, `clippy` with `-Dwarnings`, the wasm workspace build, `scripts/run-mirror.sh` and `scripts/run-ingame.sh`, all exit 0. The wasm builds ran under `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.6`.

### What this round leaves open

- **The client render, again and one element wider.** Carried forward from fix round 2 unchanged in substance: where the trailing line wraps in a real crafting tooltip is unmeasured. The splitter adds a question of its own that no dump can answer, which is whether the client's own renderer treats a chunk boundary as anything at all. It should not, because the engine concatenates parameters, and the engine's own dump shows the pieces separately either way; nothing here has hovered one.
- **A bare string in `localised_name` is unmeasured.** The engine's sentence calls the offender a key, which suggests the rule applies to the bare form too, and suggesting is not measuring. This library's one writer always emits the table form, so only a consumer's `Extra` could reach the gap, and `Extra` refuses both field names outright. The stand-in passes a bare string rather than guessing.
- **Author-declared length is now unbounded by construction and that is a decision.** A consumer's `Description` and `DisplayName` are chunked like everything else, so neither has a length a consumer has to keep under, up to the depth ceiling above. The alternative was a declared length bound, which would have been a refusal on an author input where a chunker costs nothing.

### The migration gap, which is the round's third item

A consumer's test named `TestAnUnreachedFallbacksPackIsNeverProbed` went from green to a REFUSED LOAD across a library upgrade, and the upgrade note it should have been matched against did not describe the path it fell into. Two bullets of `docs/migration.md`'s upgrade list were each true and neither said the thing that mattered: one said a copied research cost now drops a science pack the game does not have, the other said a copied cost that keeps no pack falls back to the one you declared. What neither said is that those two are the SAME PATH, so filtering a copied unit's own packs is what can make a declared `Fallback` reachable from an arm where a source answered. A fixture that removes every `tool` from the game used to leave the `Fallback` unasked, because a source carrying a unit ended the pack question; it now empties the tier's cost, reaches the `Fallback`, finds its rungs absent too, and refuses.

**THE CODE SAID IT BACKWARDS AS WELL, WHICH IS WHY THE DOCUMENT COULD NOT BE READ ROUND.** `go/data.go`'s `CostBy` arm carried "THE FALLBACK IS RESOLVED ONLY HERE, which is the point: its packs are probed when the fallback is what applies, and never when a source answered", and the Rust twin said "ONLY HERE" too. Both clauses are false at this head: the fallback is resolved in TWO arms, the second being the packless-source case a little below it, and "never when a source answered" is exactly the case a consumer hit. Both comments now name the second arm and say that a source carrying a unit does not end the pack question.

**WHAT `docs/migration.md` SAYS NOW**, in one paragraph under the list and in three corrected bullets: every ladder in this library stops at its first answer and nothing past it is asked about, which covers an ingredient ladder's rungs, a declared pack ladder's rungs and a `CostBy` source ladder's sources alike; `CostChoices.Fallback` is read in two steps, its NUMBERS from the declaration alone with no question put to the game (so they refuse whether or not any ladder lands on it) and its PACKS in exactly two places, when no source carries a unit and when a source carried one whose every pack is absent; and a fixture that takes every `tool` out of the game exercises the second of those rather than the unreached one. The packless-refusal bullet gains the order clause `docs/usage.md` already carried, since the sentence names both sets and the list is what a suite compares. And the section ABOVE the list, where a migrating consumer meets `CostBy` for the first time, said the opposite outright: that a fallback naming a science pack the game does not have is refused before anything is emitted. That is the sentence the wrong mental model most plausibly came from, it is false (a pack is a ladder, an exhausted ladder is dropped with a line, and only a unit left with no pack at all refuses, at resolution rather than at validation), and the round's own adversarial review is what found it still standing while the correction was written 180 lines below it.

Nothing executable changed in this commit. The property the paragraph describes is the one this round and the rounds before it already implemented, and it is held up by `TestFallbackPacksAreProbedOnlyWhenTheFallbackApplies` in the Go half, whose fixture keeps the tier's own pack in the game so the fallback really is unreached, and whose witness is the QUESTION: it drives a recording `World` and asserts the pack was never put to it. THE RUST SIDE IS NOT A TWIN AND THIS ROUND DID NOT MAKE IT ONE: `an_unreached_fallback_is_never_resolved` asserts the transcript only, so it holds up the outcome and not the question. Both tests' doc comments said a source answering ends the pack question, which is the same generalisation this commit took out of the library's own comments; both now name the second half of the condition.

## Fix round 3

The trigger is the consumer's THIRD migration assessment, BetterBeltBalancer's `agents/migration-assessment-3.md` at that repository's commit `313f9d3`, 2026-09-14. It is the first assessment graded against the written threat model rather than against a reviewer's sense of scope, and it reported two findings still BLOCKED, 13 and 14, both scope F, both about one outcome: the load stopping on a mod set the player did not assemble, from a dialog the client cannot leave without resetting every startup preference they have. Each decision lands as its own commit and appends its own subsection here.

### Decision 1: an environment in scope never stops the load, except where degrading would clobber, invent or rewrite somebody else's tree

**WHAT THE ASSESSMENT MEASURED.** Finding 13's fixture moves `automation-science-pack` out of `data.raw.tool` and into `data.raw.item`, with a second fixture repairing base's own technologies around it so the base game still loads; base alone with the fixture and without the consumer is exit 0, which is what puts the row in scope F rather than in case I. Under it, the consumer's DEFAULT setting exited 1 with no dump and no game, and the whole of what a player read, in the log and inside the client's own dialog alike, was

```
Failed to load mod "better-belt-balancer": fklua: at the data stage, fkrecipes: the technology bbb-balancer has no science pack the game has; research takes at least one, and none of automation-science-pack is a science pack here
```

which names the mod, the technology, the rule and every name the walk tried, and contains no action a player can take. Finding 14 is the other half of the same event: `fallbackFact`'s added sentence is correct in all three directions the assessment measured, and it says nothing to do; the dialog it is read in offers Disable listed mods, Disable all mods, Manage mods, Restart, Exit and a Reset mod settings checkbox, `Manage mods` has no Mod settings button and its `Back` returns to the same dialog, `Restart` reproduces itself over a file whose sha256 has not moved, and the one escape costs every startup preference in the file and the mod with it. THE SECOND ASSESSMENT'S CLIENT WALK IS WHAT STANDS AS THAT MEASUREMENT; finding 14 says plainly that the dialog was NOT re-measured this round, and nothing here re-measured it either.

**WHAT CHANGED, PER SITE.**

- *`go/data.go`, `rust/src/data.rs`: the packless refusal is deleted.* `checkResolvedPacks` / `check_resolved_packs` is gone, with the `res.packless` / `packless_names` carrier it read. In its place `packlessAt` / `packless_at` is what a technology priced in no science pack at all earns, WHERE IT IS FOUND rather than at a post-resolution gate: one ERROR line, one note on that technology's own `noteTarget`, and one record in `res.packlessSaid` so the pair can be retracted by the one caller that can retract it, a player's typed pack list writing over the very unit that went packless. The unit is emitted with an empty `ingredients` list. THE LINE AND THE NOTE ARE PER TECHNOLOGY, where a refusal could only ever be one and named the first in declaration order.
- *The same two files: the copied unit in neither engine form degrades in two shapes.* `unreadableUnitPhrase` / its Rust twin is the fact both lines open with, and the two arms differ in what the library did next. Behind a `CostBy` tier, where the author declared a `Fallback`, `unreadableSourceLine` and `unreadableSourceNote` price the technology at the declared cost and the prerequisite and the level cap stay. Behind a bare `CostOf`, where nothing is declared, `unreadableCopyLine` and `unreadableCopyNote` keep the unit and replace its `ingredients` with an empty list, so the count, the time, a `count_formula` and every field this library has never heard of survive untouched. The two notes are DELIBERATELY DIFFERENT and may not be merged: on this path the list was never decoded, so the note may say only that it could not be read, never that the game has none of those packs.
- *`go/cycle.go`, `rust/src/cycle.rs`: the walk resolves.* `checkCycles` takes `*resolution` now and loops: build the overlay, walk for a ring, and where the ring holds an edge this plan made, drop it and walk again. `dropOwnedEdge` takes the FIRST edge in the RING'S OWN ORDER that this plan made, because the ring is what both halves have in front of them and a rule keyed on declaration order would need each half to agree about a second ordering it did not compute. An edge this plan owns is one of exactly two things: an entry in one of its own technologies' resolved prerequisite lists, or a SPLICE, which is this plan's own emitted name sitting inside another technology's rewritten list. TERMINATION IS BY CONSTRUCTION: every pass either drops one of a finite number of edges this plan made, or refuses and returns.
- *`dropSplice` puts the anchor back.* A splice REPLACES the technology it was inserted after, so a SECOND splice into the same target built its record on a list the anchor was already out of; deleting the dropped name from that later record would emit a prerequisite list with a base-game edge silently gone. The record that MADE the splice is marked `dropped` rather than renumbered, because `resolvedTech.rewrite` is a 1-based index into that slice. An APPENDED splice replaced nothing, carries no anchor, and is deleted.
- *The World is asked once and the plan every pass.* `currentPrereqs` was two halves, the last non-dropped rewrite record for a technology and the game's own list; only the first moves between passes, so the second is hoisted out of the loop. It is a HOST CALL and not a map lookup, which is what makes the hoist worth making: measured by the adversarial review of this decision on a 275-technology world, 275 calls across the wasm boundary would have become 14,025 at 50 plan technologies and 75,900 at 400. The graph each pass computes is unchanged.
- *`go/customize.go`, `rust/src/data.rs`: one informational line.* The research-cost line rendered an emitted EMPTY pack list through the language's own renderer, whose answer for an empty list is the reserved word `none`, which is the word a PACKS field refuses; printing it invited the player to paste back the one text the field will not take. It reads `packs no science pack` in that case. The substitution is the LINE'S, at the line's own composer, and neither the renderer nor `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` moves for it.
- *`go/source_test.go`, `rust/src/tests/source.rs`: three rows leave the classification tables and one is narrowed.* `checkResolvedPacks` / `check_resolved_packs` and `resolve`'s copied-unit row are deleted from both `refusalClasses` / `REFUSAL_CLASSES` and `refusalPolicy` / `REFUSAL_POLICY`; `checkCycles`' row keeps its class and its exception and its `why` narrows to the one ring it still refuses. The doc comment explaining why a second, independently written list exists cited `checkResolvedPacks` as the function a relabel could hide behind; it cites `checkResolvedCraftTimes` now, because the hole is a property of any item that takes a resolution rather than a `World` and not of the one function that demonstrated it.
- *`go/lib.go`: the `Pack` doc comment.* It promised that a unit whose every pack drops is refused. It says what happens instead, and why.

**THE EXACT NEW TEXTS.**

```
fkrecipes: ERROR: <tech>: none of <names> is a science pack this game has, so the research is emitted with no science pack and completes for free
fkrecipes: ERROR: <tech>: the unit of <source> holds a table this library cannot copy faithfully, so this mod's own declared cost applies instead
fkrecipes: ERROR: <tech>: the unit of <source> holds a table this library cannot copy faithfully, so the research is emitted with no science pack and completes for free
fkrecipes: ERROR: <tech>: requiring <name> would loop this game's technology tree (<ring>), so the prerequisite is dropped
fkrecipes: ERROR: <tech>: making it a prerequisite of <before> would loop this game's technology tree (<ring>), so the splice is dropped
```

And five trailing lines in an emitted prototype's own `localised_description`, through the same `noteOn` mechanism fix round 2's decision C installed:

```
This game has none of the science packs this research names, so it takes no science pack at all. The reason is in the log.
The <source> cost this research copies cannot be read in this game, so that cost was not used to price this research. The reason is in the log.
The <source> cost this research copies cannot be read in this game, so it takes no science pack at all. The reason is in the log.
Requiring <name> would loop this game's technology tree, so this research was left without that prerequisite. The reason is in the log.
Making this research a prerequisite of <before> would loop this game's technology tree, so it was left out of it. The reason is in the log.
```

THE WHOLE RING IS IN THE LINE AND NOT IN THE NOTE. An author reading the log needs the path to see which mod closed it; a player hovering a technology cannot act on a list of prototype names, and the 200-byte-per-element ceiling every composition is held to is a second reason not to put one there.

**THE ONE SENTENCE THAT SURVIVES, AND IT IS NARROW.** `fkrecipes: a prerequisite cycle: <ring>`, unchanged, for a ring holding NO edge this plan made. That is the game's own technology tree looping without this mod in it, which the engine refuses on its own, which the threat model puts out of scope (I), and where there is no edge of ours to take back, so the only alternative to the sentence is rewriting somebody else's tree.

**WHAT THIS DECISION DELIBERATELY DID NOT DO.** The `validate` family's two named-prototype probes (`place_result`, `ResultNamed`) and the six `CostOf(...)` sentences are still refusals and are still environmental. There is nothing declared behind a bare name, so degrading would mean INVENTING a value the author never wrote, into the author's own prototype. The change that would empty the `CostOf` row out of the table is the one fix round 2's own review priced and did not pay for, giving `CostOf` the `Fallback` shape `CostBy` and the pack lists already have; it is additive surface, it mirrors a shape the library carries twice, and this round did not take it either. It is carried forward on the open list below.

**AND IT OVERTURNS SOMETHING FIX ROUND 2 EXPLICITLY KEPT.** That round's decision B listed the packless refusal as a survivor under the enumerated exception "degrading would invent a value the author never declared", and the free-research measurement was what justified keeping it. What that graded was the MECHANISM, which is a claim about our own code; what the threat model grades is the OUTCOME, and the outcome of the refusal was a player in a dialog with no route back into their game. The rubric says so in as many words: an in-scope situation that leaves the player unable to reach the game is BLOCKED, whatever the reasoning behind the sentence. Both the second and the third assessment graded it BLOCKED, and the second round's own ownership row said "FkRecipes, closed", which was true of the diagnosis and false of the outcome. Grading the outcome is also what makes the exception list checkable by somebody outside this repository, which "degrading would invent a value" never was.

### The gate grew an arm, which is where this is measured on an engine rather than argued

`scripts/run-ingame.sh` gains a DEMOTE ARM: a third mod set, not a third golden row, one engine run per language, under a Lua-only fixture committed at `testdata/ingame/demote/`. `data.lua` moves `automation-science-pack` from `data.raw.tool` to `data.raw.item` keeping every other field; `data-final-fixes.lua` takes the demoted pack out of every technology's unit EXCEPT the guest's own, by prefix, and out of every lab's `inputs`, so the base game still loads and the row sits in scope F rather than scope I. The packaged guest's `info.json` gains `? fkrecipes-demote`, appended to whatever `fklua` wrote rather than assigned over it, so the fixture's data stage runs BEFORE the guest's; the order is the engine's rather than alphabetical luck.

THE ARM ASSERTS, in order: exit 0 (the run's own failure text names findings 13 and 14 and says the load must never stop); that the fixture actually demoted the pack; both `fkrecipes: ERROR: ` lines in BOTH languages' engine logs, plus the neighbouring copied-unit drop line fix round 2 landed; both emitted units empty, through a term that names the prototype, because `null | length` is 0 in jq and a bare length check passes over a technology the dump does not hold; both tooltips pinned WHOLE, for the same reason a phrase search would not do; and that the two languages agree on the normalised dump.

THERE IS NO GOLDEN ROW, and that is a decision rather than an omission. The golden's rows are keyed by the mod set that produced them and this arm runs a second mod, so a hash here would describe a world no other row describes. What is compared instead is the two LANGUAGES against each other, which is a property a golden row could not have added. It also uses `refuse` where the hash rows use SKIPPED: a mod-set difference is environmental for a HASH, which is a function of every mod that ran, and this arm asserts nothing against a golden while the pack it demotes is base's own, so its claim holds whatever DLC the machine owns.

`jqassert` moved up beside `fail`, because the demote arm runs before the section that used to define it and a function defined after its caller is a runtime error rather than a failing assertion.

### The red proofs

Every one was taken by breaking the guarded code, running the test, reading the designed failure and reverting the break. Four were taken at the engine gate and are quoted whole below; the host-suite proofs are quoted by their observed text where the text is what says the guard bites, and the table further down records what each of the sixteen guards holds.

*The host-suite proofs, as observed.* Each line is the failure the test printed with the break in place, and each break was reverted before the gates were run.

| the `CostOf` unreadable arm writes `packlessNote` | `TestACopiedUnitWhosePackListIsInNeitherFormIsEmptied` | `got: ..."This game has none of the science packs this research names, so it takes no science pack at all. ..."` against `want: ..."The steel-processing cost this research copies cannot be read in this game, so it takes no science pack at all. ..."` |

Each break was taken in BOTH halves and each printed the same shape; the Rust twins' names are in the table further down.


*The arm's own proof, and it is the round's headline in one line.* With the packless degradation broken in the Go guest (a deliberate `fkrecipes: RED PROOF: the packless degradation is broken` raised where the empty unit is composed), the engine refused the load and the arm refused with it:

```
Error Util.cpp:81: Failed to load mod "fkrecipes-example": fklua: at the data stage, fkrecipes: RED PROOF: the packless degradation is broken
run-ingame: go: the engine refused the load under the demote fixture.
  That is the lock-out findings 13 and 14 measured: a pack that demotes one
  science pack must degrade with a line and a tooltip, never stop the load.
```

*The fixture's proof, which is what stops the arm being vacuous.* With the demotion disabled in the fixture's own `data.lua`, the run still exits 0 and still produces two agreeing dumps, which is exactly why the assertion has to exist:

```
  FAIL: the fixture demoted the science pack ... (the dump says false)
  FAIL: a technology left with no science pack logged no ERROR line in the engine's own log
  FAIL: the custom-cost technology left with no science pack logged no ERROR line in the engine's own log
  FAIL: a copied unit that dropped one pack logged nothing in the engine's own log
```

*Two proofs the fixture itself failed, each found by the gate refusing the load rather than by reading the Lua.* A `data.lua` snapshot of the technologies that existed before the fixture ran misses every one a mod loading LATER adds, and space-age's `data.lua` runs after this fixture's: `Error Util.cpp:81: Technology advanced-asteroid-processing: there is no lab that will accept all of the science packs this technology requires.` The guest's prefix is what names exactly the prototypes under test, and the lab loop is the second half: without it the same sentence names `fkrecipes-example-chain-forging`. Both are recorded in the fixture's own comments, because a fixture that stops being right silently is worse than no fixture.

*The sixteen host-suite guards, by name and by what each one holds.* Go in `go/cycle_test.go` and `go/packladder_test.go`, Rust in `rust/src/tests/cycle.rs` and `rust/src/tests/data.rs`. Fifteen have a Rust twin; the ordering pair below is Go-only, as the refusal-ordering pair it replaces was before this commit.
