### What moved in the goldens, and why

NOTHING MOVED, and that is the interesting fact rather than a gap to apologise for.

| golden | this commit |
|---|---|
| `testdata/mirror/transcript.golden` | unmoved. The stand-in demotes `military-science-pack`, which the copied-unit filter fix round 2 landed already handles, and no technology in either example guest is left with no pack at all or closes a ring, so nothing in the mirror reaches a sentence this commit added |
| `testdata/ingame/dump-sha256.txt` | unmoved, both rows, both hashes: `e824cd83...` default data, `77af9873...` flipped data, `457e7d68...` settings on both. On a stock 2.0.77 install every pack the examples name is a real tool, so nothing degrades there |
| `testdata/ingame/flipped.json`, `flipped.golden.dat` | unmoved; the `.dat` compared clean before any engine ran |
| `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` | unmoved, which is now six commits in a row. No message the LANGUAGE builds changed: `packs no science pack` is the LOG LINE's substitution and the renderer's own answer for an empty list is untouched |
| `testdata/locale/findings.golden`, `example.cfg` | unmoved. No locale rule changed |

WHY THAT IS THE RIGHT ANSWER AND NOT A HOLE. Every sentence this commit adds is reached only by a mod set that broke something, and the two existing gates run against mod sets where nothing is broken. That is precisely the coverage gap the demote arm exists to close, and it closes it by running a SECOND mod set rather than by moving a golden taken under the first. A commit that moved either hash while adding a degradation would have been saying that a stock install degrades now, which would be a defect.

### Gates

Run at this commit, exit codes read directly and never through a pipe, all 0: `gofmt -l .` (no output), `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./...`, the `go/examples/notext` vet, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo test`, `RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --workspace`, `scripts/run-mirror.sh` and `scripts/run-ingame.sh`. The wasm builds and both scripts ran under `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.6`, because the host's go1.27.1 is past what TinyGo 0.41.1 accepts. `FACTORIO_USERDIR` stayed at its `/tmp/fkrecipes` default.

The engine gate's own timing, from its output: three runs in 14s for go and 13s for rust on the golden's mod set, and two runs in 9s for the demote arm, so the arm costs the gate about nine seconds of engine time. The whole gate is 49.9 seconds wall at this commit (`time scripts/run-ingame.sh`), and `CLAUDE.md` says about 50 seconds now, where it said about 35.

THE JUMP ROWS MOVED AND THE LUA STILL COMPILES, which is worth a line because the mirror is the only gate that can see it. Go's `(*Lib).PlanData` widest span went from 966,597 bytes to 982,140, 147 percent to 150 percent of the 655,355-byte limit, relayed through 12 stations either way; Rust's `plan_data` went from 397,040 to 394,341, 61 percent to 60 percent, no relay needed. Both figures are the packaging report's own, read off `scripts/run-mirror.sh` before and after. The register ceiling fix round 2 hit is not reached, and the margin is smaller rather than larger: `afterResolution` LOST a check (the packless one) while `PlanData` itself went from 52 lines to 60, so the Go guest is nearer that ceiling than it was and not further from it. What the two numbers do NOT say is which change bought the Go jump row's 15,543 extra bytes; nothing here attributed them. The rule fix round 2 wrote still stands and is why the packaged Lua still compiles: the post-resolution gate stays in `afterResolution` and `PlanData` stays thin.

### The round's ownership table

A row per finding this commit touched, its grade in the consumer's third migration assessment, and who owns the outcome AFTER it. THE OWNER IS WHOEVER CAN CHANGE THE OUTCOME BY CHANGING THEIR OWN SOURCE, and a finding may have two owners with the split stated.

| # | was | owner after | why |
|---|---|---|---|
| 13 | BLOCKED | FkRecipes, closed; BBB, an item of its own that is now an IMPROVEMENT rather than a repair | the load no longer stops on that mod set: a technology left with no usable science pack is emitted with an empty unit, one ERROR line per technology, and one line in the technology's own tooltip, so both disclosures are where a player looks and the engine gate's demote arm measures all of it. BBB's half is a `Fallbacks` rung on `FallbackUnit()`'s single pack, which is the one-line change the assessment itself names, and its value CHANGED RATHER THAN DISAPPEARING: without the rung its research no longer stops the load in that modpack, it goes FREE, which is a balance change its own players did not choose and will not report as a bug. The comment at `guest/go/tune/plan.go:261` arguing that a second rung would be a guess dressed as a ladder still has to move in the same commit as the rung |
| 14 | BLOCKED | engine for the dialog; FkRecipes, closed, for the path into it | the sentence was already honest and the round before this one closed it. What closed here is the PATH IN: the refusal that led a player to that dialog on a scope-F mod set does not exist any more. The dialog is still the engine's, and this round did not re-measure it; the second assessment's client walk stands as its measurement, as finding 14 itself says |
| 19 | MISLED | FkRecipes, closed | added by decision 2. The recipe tooltip, the settings tooltip, the ERROR line and the refusal's added sentence all said a set-aside value fell back to the mod's own default, choice, list or declaration, which the assessment measured false on five of six presets. All four now say the field behaved as though it had been left alone, which is the library's own rule and is true beside a dropdown, on every preset, where no dropdown exists and of a number setting as well as a text one. The BEHAVIOUR is unchanged: the fallback still lands on the dropdown's currently chosen preset, and the two tests that pin that were already there |
| 20 | AWKWARD | FkRecipes, closed | added by decision 3. The `CostBy` arm where no source in the chosen ladder carries a unit logged a line, called no note recorder, and emitted a technology with `prerequisites` absent and `localised_description` absent: the research moved to the ROOT of the technology tree, researchable from the first minute. It now records `unpricedSourceNote` / `unpriced_source_note` after the fallback is resolved, so the technology's own tooltip says it has no prerequisite and no copied cost. The three enumerations the finding cited as evidence that the omission was never argued for (`go/data.go`'s note block, `docs/usage.md`'s fenced block, `agents/customizer-design.md` line 56) now carry all ten notes, and the SET is held by a source property, `TestEveryNoteCallSiteIsAccountedFor` / `every_note_call_site_is_accounted_for`, so the enumerations are derived rather than load-bearing. The half of the finding recorded rather than graded, that the consumer's composed `bbb-tech-cost` tooltip renders a renamed technology's base locale key as a name for a prototype that no longer exists, is BBB's and the engine's between them and is untouched here |
| 21 | AWKWARD | FkRecipes, closed; BBB has nothing it must do | added by decision 4. A note with no declared `Description` was emitted as `{"", "<note>"}`, and a prototype's own `localised_description` field wins over the `[recipe-description]` or `[technology-description]` entry of the same name, so the sentence telling a player what the technology DOES was gone for the whole of that load and the note stood in its place. The composition now opens with a reference to that prototype's own entry followed by a newline, behind an empty alternative, so the engine renders the author's sentence ABOVE the note where they defined the entry and the note alone where they did not. BBB'S HALF IS NOTHING RATHER THAN ONE LINE, which is the difference between this row and the assessment's two proposed repairs: its `[technology-description]` entry survives a fallback with no change on its side, it does not have to move its description into `TechSpec.Description`, and the library does not have to ask. The assessment's other repair, a sentence in `docs/usage.md` telling an author to declare `Description` in the plan if they want it kept, was NOT taken: it would have moved the cost onto every consumer for a defect the library could answer by construction. `docs/usage.md` documents the behaviour instead |
| 22 | MISLED | FkRecipes, closed; BBB chooses what to do with its own locale entry | added by decision 5. The scope document's scope F sentence was false on three clauses and its own supporting quote named a sentence this library never wrote. Scope F now states the class claim without a total claim, cross-references `CLAUDE.md`'s enumerated refusals rather than copying them, separates an AUTHOR's ladder (disclosed on the settings screen, logs nothing) from an ENVIRONMENT's degradation (disclosed on the prototype and in one `ERROR:` line), and says what CHANGED rather than what was substituted, because two of this round's degradations substitute nothing. The disclosure the design leaned on is composed by the library now, in three vocabularies. BBB MAY KEEP OR DELETE ITS OWN SENTENCE and neither is owed: `bbb-recipe-cost`'s `[mod-setting-description]` entry still renders ABOVE the library's composed lines, so keeping it costs a player one more line saying the same thing in the consumer's words, and deleting it loses nothing, because the rule, the merge and the "can be a shorter list" warning are all in the library's own sentence now. THE MERGE ARRIVED LATE: the sentence this decision first shipped had two clauses and not three, so for as long as that draft stood this row was false on its own middle term, and two ladders landing on `iron-plate` emitted one ingredient of amount 5 with nothing anywhere saying so below the clamp ceiling. What BBB may NOT do any more is treat that entry as load-bearing for the library's design |
| 15 | CLEAN on the mechanism, NOT REACHABLE on the render, with a cosmetic residual | FkRecipes, DECLINED, which is not the same thing as open | added by decision 7. The mechanism closed two rounds ago and did not move here: every composed key rides in the alternatives form, so `technology-name.logistics-2`, which nothing defines because the engine composes that name itself at runtime, degrades to `logistics-2` and the tooltip survives whole. THE RESIDUAL IS THE RENDER, `Logistics 2: cost of logistics-2` where the game's own name would read `Logistics 2`, and the assessment's remedy (e), copying the technology prototype's own `localised_name`, IS NOT AVAILABLE TO THIS LIBRARY AT ANY PRICE rather than merely unpriced. Measured on 2.0.77 (build 84539, mac-arm64, steam) this round: the settings stage, where the line is composed, sees `data.raw` as an EMPTY table with `technology` nil; a setting prototype is not in `data.raw` at the data stage and declaring one there refuses the load with `No loader found for "string-setting"`; `mod-settings-dump.json` is written during the settings stage, before the data stage runs, and survives a refused data stage; and `logistics-2` carries NO `localised_name` at the data stage at all (3 of 275 technologies do, and all three hold another key table). DECLINED means measured, recorded and not owed: it is not on this round's open list, and a future round that reopens it has to move an engine fact first. The part that stays NOT REACHABLE is the part that always was, whether the row draws an info icon and a tooltip on a client, and that belongs to the owed client round |

### What this round leaves open on this side

- **One note per prototype, first in walk order.** Carried forward unchanged from fix round 2, and this commit adds four more note producers to compete for the one slot. A technology that lost a science pack AND lost a prerequisite says one of the two things in its tooltip; the ERROR lines carry both, and the log is not where a player looks.
- **A note that loses the slot is gone rather than queued, and the retraction path can leave a prototype with no note at all.** `noteOn` keeps the FIRST writer and DROPS every later one, so a technology that degraded environmentally AND had a stored value fall back says only the first of the two. Where the first is later taken back, the second is not promoted, because it was never recorded: the tier arm writes a degrade note, a research number's fallback then finds the slot taken and is dropped, and a player's typed pack list restores the pre-tier snapshot through `restoreNote`, leaving a technology whose stored value really was set aside carrying nothing in its tooltip. This is PRE-EXISTING and neither commit of this round created it: the same outcome followed from the first-wins rule before the retraction was rewritten as a snapshot. It is narrow to reach: the research numbers are the only fallbacks that can land after the tier has priced, and a number can only fall back through a stored value of the WRONG TYPE or a second mod declaring the same setting name, because the engine resets an out-of-range number before any stage runs (measured). Fixing it means a note QUEUE per prototype rather than a slot, which is a shape change and a decision of its own rather than an amendment. Not fixed here.

  **AND THE SAME SLOT IS WHY THREE SENTENCES WERE REWORDED RATHER THAN RETRACTED IN THE COMMIT ABOVE.** `restoreNote` / `restore_note` fires on a typed pack LIST alone, so a player who overrides only the COUNT or the SECONDS through a `CostFrom` beside a `CostBy` tier keeps whatever the tier arm wrote while a number in the emitted unit is theirs (`TestASettingOverridesTheFallbackTier` emits `count=45` where the declared `Fallback` is 200). Retracting on a number too would mean retracting a sentence that is still true of the packs, and queueing a corrected one would need the queue this bullet is about. What was done instead is narrower and needs neither: `packlessSourceNote`, `unreadableSourceNote` and `unpricedSourceNote` now state the ENVIRONMENTAL FACT and name no price, so they are true under a partial override and under none. The QUEUE is still what the slot costs, and the CLAMP path is where that cost is now visible: a technology that both lost its prerequisite and clamped a merged pack discloses only the clamp, so the player loses the one sentence that would have mentioned tree position. That is accepted for the reason the ordering is what it is, recorded beside the call site in both halves.
- **`CostOf` still has no ladder.** Carried forward unchanged. It is the one change that would empty the `CostOf` row out of `CLAUDE.md`'s exception table, it is additive surface, and three rounds have now priced it and not paid for it.
- **The client render of the composed trailing line at its longest.** Carried forward, and one class wider: the two cycle notes are new prose in a TECHNOLOGY's description, and where a technology's description wraps in the tech tree is still unmeasured. No gate here can see a render.
- **Whether a recipe's own `localised_description` suppresses the client's item-description fallback.** Decision 4 below composes `[recipe-description].<emitted name>` beside `[technology-description].<emitted name>`, and the technology arm is measured while the recipe arm is not. `[recipe-description]` is NOT where a Factorio author normally writes a recipe's description: a crafting tooltip's description comes from the RESULT ITEM, through a fallback in the client's tooltip builder rather than through the prototype. On 2.0.77 (build 84539, mac-arm64, steam) `base/locale/en/base.cfg` defines ZERO `[recipe-description]` entries against 29 `[item-description]` ones (`awk '/^\[recipe-description\]/{f=1;next} /^\[/{f=0} f&&NF' base.cfg | wc -l` gives 0; the same awk over `[item-description]` gives 29), the consumer's `mod-data/locale/en/better-belt-balancer.cfg` is the same shape with no `[recipe-description]` section at all, and a `localised_print` probe on a recipe prototype with only `[item-description]` defined renders `Unknown key: "recipe-description.probe-widget"`, so the RUNTIME PROTOTYPE VALUE carries no item fallback of its own. WHETHER THE CLIENT'S OWN FALLBACK IS SKIPPED ONCE THE PROTOTYPE HAS A `localised_description` AT ALL IS NOT MEASURABLE HEADLESS AND WAS NOT MEASURED. If it is skipped, the recipe arm closes nothing for the very consumer that raised finding 21, and that consumer's own sentence, "the recipe has nothing to lose, because this mod ships no `[recipe-description]` entry", rests on the same unmeasured assumption. It is PRE-EXISTING rather than created here: a recipe carrying a note has emitted a `localised_description` since fix round 2, so at the prototype layer the recipe arm can only HELP (an author who does write the entry keeps it) or be NEUTRAL (the empty alternative and the note alone, byte for byte the old shape). It joins the owed client round.
- **A ring that only a second pass can find is unwitnessed on an engine.** `TestTwoRingsAreBothResolved` and its twin hold the loop in both host suites, and neither gate reaches a cycle at all: the mirror's stand-in and both in-game guests declare trees with no ring in them. Adding one would mean example content whose whole purpose is to be wrong, which is a decision rather than an amendment, and it is the same shape as the five gaps fix round 2 recorded.
- **The demote arm walks one shape of degradation and not three.** It measures the packless pair on two technologies and the copied-unit drop line beside them. The unreadable-pack-list arms and the cycle drops are held by unit tests in both halves and by nothing on an engine, because reaching them needs a fixture that rewrites another mod's `unit.ingredients` into a shape the copier cannot read, or a fixture that closes a ring through the guest's own splice.
- **A recipe with NO SETTING AT ALL has nowhere a player looks to carry the ladder's disclosure.** Added by the amendment to decision 5. The sentence is composed onto a setting's description, so a recipe declared with a plain `Ingredients` list, no `IngredientsFrom` and no `IngredientsBy`, has no field for it: on that plan the resolve-or-drop contract is disclosed to the AUTHOR in `docs/usage.md` and nowhere else. It is bounded rather than open, which is why it is a recorded gap and not a finding: a ladder that leaves the recipe with SOMETHING to craft is the advertised contract and the emitted ingredient rows are what the game shows, and a ladder that empties the recipe completely says so on the recipe's own tooltip on every plan, through decision 6. What is left uncovered is a substitution or a drop a player cannot compare against anything, on a plan whose author chose to offer no setting. Closing it would mean a note on a prototype for the contract itself, which decision 5 argues against and which would fire on every modpack that renames one item. Not fixed here.
- **No gate reaches either new shape on a packaged guest.** Neither example guest declares a bare ingredient dropdown, so the mirror and the engine gate walk the ladder line on the with-text shape only; and neither declares a recipe whose every ladder can run out on the gate's mod set, so the engine gate's DATA hashes cannot move on decision 6 and did not. Both are held by unit tests in both halves. Adding either to an example guest is content whose purpose is to be wrong on a real mod set, which is a decision rather than an amendment, and it is the same shape as the gaps above.
- **The FkLua checkout drift.** Carried forward unchanged: `rust/Cargo.lock` pins the fkdata dependency at `b88965d` while both gate scripts build the `fklua` binary out of whatever `FKLUA_CHECKOUT` holds. Every measurement in this round used that sibling checkout, and syncing this repository onto the newer head is still owed as its own round.

### Decision 2: every sentence about a set-aside value says the field behaved as though it had been left alone

**WHAT THE ASSESSMENT MEASURED.** Finding 19 of the consumer's third assessment, MISLED, scope B ("the recipe or technology tooltip states the outcome"), owner FkRecipes. With `better-belt-balancer-recipe-ingredients` holding the typo `2 iron-plat`, one run per preset of `bbb-recipe-cost`, six presets: vanilla gave `4 iron-plate, 2 iron-gear-wheel, 2 transport-belt`, cheap `2 iron-plate, 1 transport-belt`, belt-fast `4 iron-plate, 2 iron-gear-wheel, 2 fast-transport-belt`, belt-express `4 steel-plate, 2 iron-gear-wheel, 2 express-transport-belt`, splitter `1 splitter, 2 iron-plate`, splitter-express `1 express-splitter, 2 steel-plate`. Six ingredient lists and ONE byte-identical note on the recipe saying this mod's own choice applied. The fallback target is the DROPDOWN'S CURRENTLY CHOSEN PRESET, and the mod's own declared list only where the dropdown happens to sit on the declared one. The two lines a player can see contradict each other on one screen, through the engine's own resolver: with the dropdown on Cheap the setting tooltip reads `default: 4 iron-plate, 2 iron-gear-wheel, 2 transport-belt` while the recipe the game built is `2 iron-plate, 1 transport-belt`.

**THE BEHAVIOUR WAS NOT THE DEFECT.** `TestARefusedTextBesideADropdownFallsBackToTheDropdown` / `a_refused_text_beside_a_dropdown_falls_back_to_the_dropdown` pinned it before this round and still do. What changed is six sentences, and nothing about what the library emits into a recipe, a technology or a setting other than the words in a description.

**THE FIVE COMPOSED SENTENCES AND THE THREE DOCUMENTS, BEFORE AND AFTER.**

| site | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| `fallbackNote` / `fallback_note`, the trailing line in a recipe's or technology's own `localised_description` | `The stored value of <setting> could not be used, so this mod's own choice applies instead. The reason is in the log.` | `The stored value of <setting> could not be used, so the game loaded as though that setting had been left alone. The reason is in the log.` |
| `textFallbackLine` / `TEXT_FALLBACK_LINE`, the line on a text setting's own composed description | `\nA text this mod cannot use is set aside and that default applies instead; the reason is in the log, or in the load error if the load stops anyway.` | `\nA text this mod cannot use is set aside and the field behaves as though it said default; the reason is in the log, or in the load error if the load stops anyway.` |
| `playerFallback` / `player_fallback`, the ERROR log line | `fkrecipes: ERROR: <reason>. The mod loaded with its own default instead; fix the <field> under Settings > Mod settings > Startup, then restart.` | `fkrecipes: ERROR: <reason>. The mod loaded as though that <field> had been left alone; fix the <field> under Settings > Mod settings > Startup, then restart.` |
| `fallbackFact` / `fallback_fact`, the sentence a REFUSAL carries when a stored value fell back on the way to it | `. The stored value of <setting> could not be used, so the mod's own declaration applied.` | `. The stored value of <setting> could not be used and was set aside, so what applied is what that field gives when it is left alone.` |
| `parseIngredientList` / `parse_ingredient_list`, the empty-text refusal, whose middle clause is the `<reason>` half of the ERROR line | `... write the ingredients as "2 iron-plate, 3 copper-cable", the word default for the mod's own list, or the word none for a recipe with no ingredients`, and the packs shape the same without `none` | `... the word default to leave this field alone, ...`, both shapes. One log line said `default` meant the mod's own list and then that the field had been left alone, which beside a dropdown are two different lists |
| `docs/ingredient-list.md` | "the text is set aside, the mod's own list applies instead" | "the text is set aside, the field behaves as though it still said `default`", and the page's SEVEN other statements of what `default` MEANS gained the same two arms: the table row, the rules bullet, the "Leaving the text on default" paragraphs, the "always means the mod's own list" sentence under the ERROR line, the numeric twin beside it, and the description of the composed fallback sentence |
| `docs/usage.md` | "your declared list is what the fallback lands on" | "a fallback lands where the field's reserved word would have landed, which is the preset the dropdown beside it is currently on where you declared one and your declared list where you did not" |
| `docs/migration.md` | "The whole typed list is set aside, your declared list applies" | "The whole typed list is set aside, the field decides as though it had been left alone (the preset the dropdown beside it is currently on where you declared one, your declared list where you did not)" |

**WHY THE WORDING DIFFERS PER SITE, since all four could have taken one string.** `textFallbackLine` says "behaves as though it said default" and not "that default applies instead" because "that default" is deictic and its nearest antecedent on the settings screen is the composed `default:` line three rows above, which renders the author's declared list and nothing else; "behaves as though it said default" points at the SWITCH LINE `textSwitchLine` / `text_switch_line` composes immediately above it, which already says which field decides. `fallbackFact` may not say "the mod loaded" in any form, because it decorates a REFUSAL and nothing loaded; it says what happened to the value and stops. Its adverbial hangs off the OUTCOME and not off the setting-aside, which is the one way it is built differently from its three siblings: nothing about the setting-aside is conditional, the value went, and "left alone" describes what then APPLIED, so the sentence names the two in the order they happened. `playerFallback` carries the field word twice now and KEEPS ITS ROUTE, because it is written on a load that succeeded and the settings screen really is reachable from there, which is the whole difference between it and `fallbackFact`. Each of those reasons is in the composer's own doc comment in both halves, because none of the four wordings is interchangeable with the others.

**THE COMMENTS AND MODULE DOCS MOVED WITH THE SENTENCES, and the first pass of this commit left four behind.** `resolveTextList`'s doc comment still opened "FOUR WAYS IN AND THREE OF THEM LAND ON THE AUTHOR'S OWN LIST" and asserted the declared list three more times, on the function that implements the behaviour, while its Rust twin `read_text_ingredients` already said it correctly; both parse results (`parsedList.isDefault`, `ListText::Default`) and both module docs said the reserved word means the mod's own list; and `rust/tests/fixture_world.rs` quoted the retired sentence as current where its two parallel comments in `testdata/mirror/standin.lua` and `scripts/run-mirror.sh` had already gained the scoping clause "because this field has no dropdown beside it". All of them now say the two-armed thing or carry the scope, and the two halves say it the same way.

**NAMING THE PRESET WAS PRICED AND NOT PAID FOR.** `playerFallback(reason, field, tail)` and `fallbackNote(setting, destroysInputs)` have no slot for a preset name, and the composer runs BEFORE `readDropdown` / `read_dropdown`, which `resolve` keeps deliberately late. The honest generic is free and is the library's own rule, already in `CLAUDE.md`: a stored value the library cannot use behaves exactly as if the player had left the field alone. Each sentence is checked against four cases: true beside a dropdown, true on every preset, true where NO dropdown exists (there the declared list IS what a field left alone gives), and true of a NUMBER setting as well as a text one, since `playerFallback` serves both.

**THE CORPUS MOVED FIRST, WHICH IS WHERE THE BOUNDARY ACTUALLY RUNS.** `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` owns the language's own sentences, which is the `<reason>` half of the ERROR line; everything after `<reason>` is the customize layer's. The first pass of this commit read that boundary as putting the whole correction on the customize side, and it was wrong: the empty-text refusal is a language sentence and it CONTAINS the claim, so a real log line said `default` meant the mod's own list and then, four words later, that the field had been left alone. The corpus moved first, alone, both suites went red naming the sentence, and only then did the two halves follow it. `docs/ingredient-list.md`'s reference table of messages carries the same sentence and moved with them.

### The red proofs, decision 2

Four composers, both halves, eight proofs. Each break was reverted and the suite re-run green before the next.

| broken | test | observed |
|---|---|---|
| the corpus's empty-text refusal moved to `the word default to leave this field alone` with BOTH halves left saying `for the mod's own list` | `TestIngredientListCorpus` | `3 of 242` cases named, each printing `want:` the moved sentence against `got:` the old one, at `cases.txt:217`, `:220` and `:752` |
| the same corpus move, Rust half | `the_corpus_is_the_contract` | `3 of 242 corpus cases disagree with this half`, the same three lines with the same want/got pair |
| `fallbackNote`'s outcome clause to `so BROKEN` | `TestFallbackNoteShape` | `got: The stored value of mymod-parts could not be used, so BROKEN. The reason is in the log.` against `want: ... so the game loaded as though that setting had been left alone. ...` |
| `fallback_note`'s outcome clause to `so BROKEN` | `fallback_note_shape` | `left: "The stored value of mymod-parts could not be used, so BROKEN. The reason is in the log."` against `right: "... so the game loaded as though that setting had been left alone. ..."` |
| `textFallbackLine`'s clause to `and BROKEN;` | `TestTheComposedTextLinesAreTheStatedOnes` | `got: "\nA text this mod cannot use is set aside and BROKEN; the reason is in the log, ..."` against the stated line |
| `TEXT_FALLBACK_LINE`'s clause to `and BROKEN;` | `the_composed_text_lines_are_the_stated_ones` | `left: "\nA text this mod cannot use is set aside and BROKEN; ..."` against `right: "... and the field behaves as though it said default; ..."` |
| `playerFallback`'s outcome clause to `The mod loaded BROKEN` | `TestPlayerFallbackLineShape` | `got: fkrecipes: ERROR: mymod-parts is not text. The mod loaded BROKEN; fix the text under ...`, and the number arm the same, so BOTH field words are pinned |
| `player_fallback`'s outcome clause to `The mod loaded BROKEN{}` | `player_fallback_line_shape` | `left: "fkrecipes: ERROR: mymod-parts is not text. The mod loaded BROKENtext; fix the text under ..."` |
| `fallbackFact`'s outcome clause to `so BROKEN.` | `TestPlayerFieldsFallBackWhileTheAuthorChannelStillRefuses` | `got: fkrecipes: a prerequisite cycle: logistics-2 -> logistics-3 -> logistics-2. The stored value of steelworks-forging-time could not be used, so BROKEN.` |
| `fallback_fact`'s outcome clause to `so BROKEN.` | `player_fields_fall_back_while_the_author_channel_still_refuses` | `left: Some("fkrecipes: a prerequisite cycle: ... could not be used, so BROKEN.")` against the stated sentence |

THE TWO GATE-LEVEL PINS ARE GOLDEN COMPARISONS and their proof is the diff below: the mirror transcript refused before it was re-recorded, naming the first differing line, and the engine gate refused on the quench recipe's four pinned description ELEMENTS and on both hash rows.

### What moved in the goldens, decision 2

Five text-setting descriptions (`extend#9`, `#10`, `#12`, `#13`, `#16`) on the fallback line alone; the `LOG fkrecipes: ERROR:` line on the outcome clause; `extend#22`, the quench recipe's own note, on the outcome clause AND on the chunk boundary, which moved from after `Changing a recipe empties an ` to after `Changing a recipe `; and the `FINAL` settings dump, which carries the same five descriptions again |

SETTINGS hash `457e7d68...` to `0e62073f...`, because a text setting's composed description is a setting PROTOTYPE and the settings dump holds prototypes |
| `testdata/ingame/dump-sha256.txt`, flipped row | DATA hash `77af9873...` to `1cd61e0b...`, because the row's two refused texts put the reworded note on a recipe and a technology. SETTINGS hash the same `0e62073f...` as the default row, which is correct: a stored value moves the data dump without moving a prototype |

TWO PINS IN THE GATE SCRIPTS MOVED WITH THE CHUNK BOUNDARY rather than with a sentence, and they are worth naming because neither is a golden file. `scripts/run-mirror.sh`'s note grep and `scripts/run-ingame.sh`'s four-element quench assertion both spell the split, and the reworded note is longer, so the 180-byte fill lands one phrase earlier. The note with the destruction tail and an empty name slot went from 208 bytes to 229, and on `fkrecipes-example-quench-ingredients` from 244 to 265, which is 245 to 266 as the ELEMENT the engine measures, with the newline that joins it onto the author's own description. `CLAUDE.md`'s ceiling rule carries the empty-slot figure, `testdata/ingame/flipped.json` the note alone beside the element, and `scripts/run-ingame.sh` and the in-game golden's own header the element; each says which of the two it is. The general assertion beside the specific one, that no element of any localised string in either dump exceeds 200 bytes, passed unchanged and is the one that does not have to be rewritten when a sentence moves.

### Gates, decision 2

Run at this commit, exit codes read directly and never through a pipe, all 0: `gofmt -l .` (no output), `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./...`, the `go/examples/notext` vet, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo test`, `RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --workspace`, `scripts/run-mirror.sh` and `scripts/run-ingame.sh`, the last two after `--update` for the four composed sentences and WITHOUT it for the corpus one: the empty-text refusal is reachable only from a hand-edited file, neither harness stores an empty text, and both goldens compared clean on the re-run. The engine gate exits 0 with the research priced on the chosen tier's own packs, the quench recipe on the preset its dropdown names, exactly two `fkrecipes: ERROR: ` lines, and the demote arm agreeing across both languages. THE JUMP ROWS MOVED A LITTLE AND THE LUA STILL COMPILES, which is the only thing the mirror can see about them. Go's `(*Lib).PlanData` widest span went from decision 1's 982,140 bytes to 991,008, 150 percent to 151 percent of the 655,355-byte limit, and the relay from 12 stations to 13; Rust's `plan_data` went from 394,341 to 394,345, 60 percent either way, no relay needed. Nothing in this commit added a statement to either function: the spans move because the composed literals are longer, and the register ceiling fix round 2 hit is not reached.

### Decision 3: the degradation that moves a technology to the root of the tree says so too, three notes stop naming a price, and the note set is a test

**WHAT THE ASSESSMENT MEASURED.** Finding 20 of the consumer's third assessment, AWKWARD, scope F. Its fixture is a pack that RENAMES the base technology `logistics` to `renamed-logistics` before this mod's data stage and repoints every base reference at data-final-fixes; base alone with the fixture is exit 0, which is what puts it in scope F. Eight rows measured, every one exit 0, the load never stops, and the library writes exactly one line:

```
fkrecipes: bbb-balancer: no source for the logistics cost carries a unit, so the fallback cost applies and the technology has no prerequisite
```

The emitted technology on all eight rows: `unit {count 20, time 15, 1 automation-science-pack}`, `prerequisites` ABSENT ENTIRELY, `localised_description` ABSENT ENTIRELY. The balancer research moves to the ROOT of the technology tree, researchable from the first minute, and reprices from whatever the chosen tier charged to base's Logistics floor. Both halves mirrored each other exactly, so it was a design gap rather than a language divergence.

**WHY IT IS THE ONE THIS ROUND OPENED WITH.** Its two neighbours in the same `switch` both call a note recorder (`res.noteOn(tgt, packlessSourceNote(source))` and `res.noteOn(tgt, packDroppedNote(filtered.dropped[0]))`), and both notes were measured arriving on the technology under the demote fixture. Those two move a PRICE; this arm also removes the PREREQUISITE, which makes it the only one of the set that changes where a technology sits in the tree. That is a distinctive fact and not a ranking, and an earlier draft of this subsection called it "the largest player-visible consequence of the set" while the ordering three paragraphs down yields its slot to the packless sentence. The two claims cannot both stand; the distinctive fact is the one that does. Decision 1's own criterion for a note, presence a player cannot check anywhere, applies to it at least as strongly as to a dropped science pack. The consumer's `bbb-tech-cost` dropdown description does state the RULE ("a tier your mods leave unpriced steps down to the next belt tier, and where that runs out the research takes 20 units of automation science at 15 seconds each with no prerequisite"), so a player who reads that tooltip and then notices their research at the root can work it out. The rule was stated and the STATE was not, which is the structure this whole cycle exists to remove.

**WHAT CHANGED.** One composer and one call per half, four enumerations, and (in the amendment that followed the adversarial review of this commit) two inherited sentences, two tests that had no path under them, one that was two compositions short, and one new source property.

- *`go/data.go`, `rust/src/data.rs`: `unpricedSourceNote()` / `unpriced_source_note()`, beside `packlessSourceNote` and `unreadableSourceNote`.* It takes no argument, for the reason `packlessNote` takes none: there is no source to name, because not one of them answered.
- *The same two files: `res.noteOn(tgt, unpricedSourceNote())` / `res.note_on(tgt, unpriced_source_note())` in the `source == ""` arm*, AFTER the fallback is resolved.
- *The note block comment over the composers in both halves* said "the three notes an ENVIRONMENTAL degradation leaves" and listed none of them. It says ten and names every one, in the order each file defines them, with `cycle.go` / `cycle.rs`'s two last.
- *The byte-budget comment over `appendLocalised` / `append_localised`* said "the three notes a recipe can carry are 208, 229 and 246 bytes". The 208 was stale twice over: it was the pre-round wording, and decision 2 of this round replaced that wording. Re-measured from the source literals, the three are 229, 229 and 246, each a sentence of 128, 128 or 145 bytes plus one space plus the 100 bytes of `recipeChangeSentence` / `RECIPE_CHANGE_SENTENCE`. **AND THE FIRST REPAIR OF THAT COMMENT SAID A RECIPE'S NOTE "ALWAYS CARRIES" THE DESTRUCTION SENTENCE, WHICH IS FALSE.** The tail rides on what MOVED and not on the prototype kind, which is `withDestruction`'s whole rule: a recipe whose CRAFTING TIME fell back takes `fallbackNote` with `destroysInputs` false, so its note is the bare 128 bytes and no tail at all, and `TestFallbackNoteShape`'s own comment says so in as many words two files away. The three figures are therefore MAXIMA rather than fixed widths, and the comment says that now, in both halves and in this bullet. The arithmetic itself was right and stands.
- *`docs/usage.md`* enumerated three sentences in a fenced block. It enumerates all ten, and the paragraph under it names what each of them is presence, arithmetic or tree shape about. The clause justifying the ingredient ladder's exclusion is untouched.
- *`agents/customizer-design.md` line 56*, "AND EACH OF THE THREE SAYS SO WHERE THE PLAYER LOOKS", keeps its dated text and gains an AMENDED paragraph under it listing the ten.

**THE EXACT SENTENCE**, 168 bytes, taking no name at all, so ONE element on every mod set and never near the 200-byte ceiling:

```
No technology this research takes its cost from carries a cost this mod can use here, so this research has no prerequisite and no copied cost. The reason is in the log.
```

**AND NEITHER OF ITS CLAUSES IS WHAT THIS COMMIT FIRST WROTE.** The first draft read `No technology this research takes its cost from carries a cost in this game, so it has no prerequisite and is priced at this mod's own declared cost.`, 175 bytes, and the adversarial review found both halves false somewhere:

- *"carries a cost in this game"* is false wherever the branch is reached by a source that EXISTS and hands over a unit this library cannot copy: not a dictionary, or holding a subtree the read dropped. `unreadableSourceNote`'s own doc comment already refuses exactly that move one level down, where a sentence saying the game has none of those packs "would be stating something the library does not know", and the draft made it one level up. `a cost this mod can use here` is true of an ABSENT source, a `research_trigger` source and a PRESENT BUT UNCOPYABLE one alike, which is every way the walk gets here. The composer's doc comment says so in both halves.
- *"is priced at this mod's own declared cost"* is finding 19 one arm over. A `CostFrom` beside the `CostBy` tier lets a player set the COUNT or the SECONDS while leaving the pack text at `default`; `restoreNote` fires on a typed PACK LIST alone, so the note survives and a number in the emitted unit is the player's. Measured in this commit's own pinned transcript: `TestASettingOverridesTheFallbackTier` emits `unit={count=45, time=30, ...}` where the declared `Fallback` count is 200. The sentence states the ENVIRONMENTAL FACT and names no price, which is true whatever the settings beside it say.

**AND THE TWO INHERITED SENTENCES MOVED WITH IT**, for the second reason and not the first: the same partial override sits beside them. `packlessSourceNote` and `unreadableSourceNote` both ended `so this mod's own declared cost applies` and now end `so that cost was not used to price this research`, 132 and 135 bytes at an empty source slot, up from 123 and 126.

**THE ERROR LINES BESIDE THEM DID NOT MOVE**, and the line and the note now differ on purpose. `packlessSourceLine` and `unreadableSourceLine` still end `, so this mod's own declared cost applies instead`: they are AUTHOR-FACING and are written where the decision was made, before any player field is read, so at that point in the walk the declared cost is exactly what the library did next. The note is PLAYER-FACING and is read after the whole resolution, beside numbers the player may have moved. Same event, two readers, two moments; a comment on each composer says which is which.

**IT NAMES NO DROPDOWN VALUE**, and that is the one place the sentence differs in shape from its two neighbours, which both name their `<source>`. `chosen` is a raw setting string an author picked for a settings file; this sentence goes into a player's tooltip, where every other note is English prose. The ERROR line beside it already carries that value for an author reading a log, which is the reader a raw value is a name for. The doc comment on the composer says so in both halves.

**IT IS RECORDED AFTER `resolveUnit` RETURNS**, which is the ordering the two arms beside it already had. `noteOn` / `note_on` keeps the FIRST note per prototype, and the `Fallback` this arm resolves can leave the technology with NO SCIENCE PACK AT ALL (`packlessAt` / `packless_at`) or CLAMP two of its own pack ladders landing on one name above the item ceiling (`mergePack` / `merge_pack`), so offering this sentence last hands the one slot to whichever of those happened.

**WHAT THE ORDERING BUYS, AND WHAT IT COSTS.** The first draft of the comment beside the call said both of those are "worse outcomes than a prerequisite this technology no longer has", which is a ranking this repository states the other way round two files away, where the prerequisite removal is the degradation "with the largest consequence". Neither claim is what the ordering rests on, and the comment says this now, in both halves and in `docs/usage.md`:

- The PACKLESS case is not a trade. A research with no science pack completes for free the moment it is queued, so a player reading only "no prerequisite and no copied cost" would be told about the tree and left to discover the price by watching the research finish. That sentence is strictly the more urgent of the two, and the ordering is what gets it written.
- The CLAMP case IS a trade, and the player loses something real: the clamp sentence names an amount capped at a ceiling and says nothing about tree position, so a technology that both lost its prerequisite and clamped a merged pack discloses only the clamp, and the ONE sentence that would have mentioned the tree is the one dropped.
- That is ACCEPTED, and the reason is the SLOT and not a ranking. With two degradations and one note per prototype, something is lost whichever order is chosen. A wrong price is what a player builds around; a prerequisite they no longer need shows in the technology screen the first time they open it. The fix is a note QUEUE, which is in this round's open list; reordering is not a fix.

**AND THE PACK TEXT'S SNAPSHOT STILL GOVERNS IT.** A player who TYPES a pack list over the tier gets everything the tier arm wrote taken back in one `restoreNote` call, this sentence included, because the technology is then priced in the player's own list. That claim had NO WITNESS when it was first written: `TestATypedPackListTakesBackTheTiersPacklessSentence` exercises the packless-SOURCE arm, not this one. `TestATypedPackListTakesBackTheUnpricedTierSentence` / `a_typed_pack_list_takes_back_the_unpriced_tier_sentence` is the twin, red-proven in both halves. The LOG line is not retracted there and stays: it has no named retraction beside `packlessSourceLine` and `unreadableSourceLine`, it is author-facing, and its second clause, that the technology has no prerequisite, is still true of the emitted prototype. A player who types only a COUNT or a SECONDS keeps the note, which is the same rule the packless and unreadable arms beside it follow and is why all three sentences were REWORDED rather than retracted: `TestASettingOverridesTheFallbackTier` / `a_setting_overrides_the_fallback_tier` is the transcript that measures it, and both halves carry the reason in a comment now.

**AND THE PRESENT-BUT-UNCOPYABLE PATH HAD NO TEST.** `TestATierWhoseSourcesCarryNoCostSaysSoOnTheTechnology` and its Rust twin exercise an ABSENT source only, which is exactly the path on which the first draft's false clause happens to be true, so nothing went red over it. `TestATierWhoseOnlySourceCarriesAnUnusableCostSaysSoToo` / `a_tier_whose_only_source_carries_an_unusable_cost_says_so_too` puts a present `steel-processing` in the world with a NIL unit, so the ladder steps past it on the SHAPE term rather than on absence, and asserts the whole `localised_description`. Red-proven by deleting the shape term, which leaves the absent-source test green and this one red.

**AND THE NOTE SET IS A SOURCE PROPERTY NOW.** Finding 20 was "a degradation that writes no note", and the first answer to it was four hand-maintained prose enumerations that nothing read, which is the same shape as the defect. `TestEveryNoteCallSiteIsAccountedFor` / `every_note_call_site_is_accounted_for` sits beside the refusal-classification pair in `go/source_test.go` and `rust/src/tests/source.rs`: it walks the library's own files for every composer handed to `noteOn` / `note_on` (looking THROUGH `withDestruction`, which composes no sentence of its own), and holds that set to a table carrying each sentence's byte length at an EMPTY argument slot and one line saying what the degradation is. It fails naming the composer in BOTH directions and pins each length, so a sentence that moves is one failure rather than a silent drift. ELEVEN ROWS: the ten environmental notes and `fallbackNote`, which is a PLAYER's fallback and is in the set anyway, marked, because a set with a hand-written exclusion in it has a hole where the exclusion is. 13 call sites in Go, 13 in Rust. The pinned lengths, in table order: 128, 84, 132, 168, 122, 135, 121, 128, 145, 129, 131.

**AND THE ELEMENT-CEILING WALK WAS TWO COMPOSITIONS SHORT.** `TestNoCompositionReachesTheElementCeiling` / `no_composition_reaches_the_element_ceiling` claims, in `CLAUDE.md` and in its own header, to walk every composition this library can produce with 200-byte names, counted at 24. Neither `unpricedSourceNote` nor `unreadableCopyNote` was in it: the first is this commit's own, and the second had been missing since it was written. The fixture now reaches both, with a Description and without, and the count is 28. A composition missing from that fixture is invisible to the fixture's own count, because the count is of what the fixture emits, which is why the note SET is held by the source property above instead.

### Decision 4: a note joins the author's own description instead of replacing it

**WHAT THE ASSESSMENT MEASURED.** Finding 21 of the consumer's third assessment, AWKWARD, new, scope B and F, owner FkRecipes for the rule and BBB for where its description lives. Measured through the engine's own resolver, one clean load and one fallback load of the same package: clean, `technology.bbb-balancer` resolved to `Balancers of any shape, built one tile at a time.`; on a refused pack text, to the note alone. The raw fields say why. Clean the prototype carries no `localised_description` at all and the engine resolves `{"technology-description.bbb-balancer"}`; on the fallback it carries `{"", "The stored value of ..."}` and the locale key is never consulted, because a prototype's own field WINS OVER the locale entry of the same name. The rule fix round 1's decision 4 wrote reads `TechSpec.Description`, a PLAN field; the consumer declares its description the ordinary Factorio way, as a `[technology-description]` entry, so the library saw "absent" and wrote the note alone.

**WHAT CHANGED.** `appendLocalised` / `append_localised` takes the prototype KIND and the EMITTED, PREFIXED name now, and its three cases are four. A note with NO declared `Description` is emitted as

```
{"", {"?", {"", {"<kind>-description.<emitted name>"}, "\n"}, ""}, <note chunks>}
```

out of a second wrapper, `descriptionRef` (`go/customize.go`) and `description_ref` (`rust/src/settings.rs`), written beside `localeRef` / `locale_ref` and deliberately not folded into it: that one's first alternative is a bare key table beside a raw PROSE fallback, and this one's is a concatenation GROUP carrying the key and the separator newline, beside a raw EMPTY fallback. Both keep the rule the source-property test enforces, the raw fallback LAST, for the same reason: a plain string alternative always resolves, so anything after it is never consulted.

The other three cases are unchanged, and one of them deliberately so. A declared `Description` beside a note stays `{"", <description chunks>, "\n" + <note chunks>}` and composes NO key: the author put their description in the plan, so that literal already takes the entry's place and composing both would print it twice. A declared `Description` with no note is unchanged. Neither emits nothing at all, so the engine resolves the author's entry on its own exactly as it always did. An ITEM never carries a note (`itemProto` / `item_proto` passes the empty string), so an item never reaches the new case and never composes a key.

**THE MEASUREMENT THAT PICKED THE SHAPE.** Factorio 2.0.77 (build 84539, mac-arm64, steam), headless, a Lua-only probe mod whose `control.lua` calls `localised_print` under `--create`, with its own `locale/en/probe.cfg` defining `[technology-description] probe-defined=AUTHOR TECH DESC` and `[recipe-description] probe-defined=AUTHOR RECIPE DESC`, plus a second probe hanging each shape on a base prototype at `data-final-fixes` and running `--dump-data`. Exit codes read directly. The table is in `agents/customizer-design.md` under fix round 3's decision 4; the crux row is that a CONCATENATION GROUP holding an undefined key is ITSELF A FAILED ALTERNATIVE, so the separator newline rides inside the alternative and dies with it. That is what makes this shape possible rather than the flatter one, which leaves a dangling leading newline on every consumer who wrote no entry.

**THE KEY LENGTH, AND WHAT THE LIBRARY DOES ABOUT IT.** The engine polices the KEY SLOT at 200 bytes like every other string element, and a key is ONE element by definition: it cannot be chunked, so the splitter that answers every other composition cannot answer this one. The engine's own prototype-name ceiling is 200 bytes (`Name field is too large. Max allowed size is: 200.`) and nothing in this library refuses a shorter one, so the case IS reachable: `technology-description.` at 23 bytes over a 200-byte emitted name is a 223-byte element, and `recipe-description.` at 19 over the same name is 219. Either would refuse the whole load, which is precisely the lock-out the note exists to prevent. `descriptionRef` therefore returns nothing above the length that fits and the note is emitted alone, exactly as it was before this wrapper: on those two names the author's entry is displaced again, which is a tooltip and not a load failure. THE FITTING LENGTHS ARE 181 BYTES of emitted recipe name and 177 of technology name.

**WHICH TEST IS THE GUARD, said exactly, because the framing above invites the wrong answer.** THE GUARD IS `TestTheDescriptionKeyIsDroppedWhereItWouldNotFit` / `the_description_key_is_dropped_where_it_would_not_fit`: it walks one byte either side of the fitting length on both kinds and asserts that the fitting side composes a key of exactly 200 bytes and the byte over composes nothing. THE ELEMENT-CEILING WALK IS NOT A SECOND GUARD ON IT. Its two new fixtures are named at EXACTLY the fitting length, so the key element they carry is exactly 200 bytes, and the walk can never go red on a composed key from ANY input: `descriptionRef` / `description_ref` refuses anything longer before the walk ever sees it. What the walk proves is that a 200-byte key element is EMITTED AT ALL, which is worth having (it is the widest single element this library hands the engine) and is not the boundary. The fixture's own comment says this correctly; this paragraph is where the notes say it too.

**AND THE REACHABILITY ARITHMETIC IS RIGHT BUT IMPRECISE FOR A RECIPE IN A SPACE AGE INSTALL.** The 200-byte prototype-name ceiling was re-measured this round (`Name field is too large. Max allowed size is: 200.` at 201). A RECIPE in an install with `quality` hits a shorter one first, because that mod derives a `-recycling` recipe per recipe and composes a `localised_name` over the source name: `Localised string key is too large: 210 > 200 (limit). in property tree at ROOT.recipe.np-qqq...-recycling.localised_name[1][0]`, which puts the effective recipe-name ceiling near 190 rather than at 200. That is still ABOVE the 181-byte fitting length, so the drop arm stays reachable for a recipe (182 to about 190) and for a technology (178 to 200), and the guard stays necessary in both installs. Recorded rather than acted on: nothing in this library refuses a name at either ceiling.

Both boundaries are pinned one byte either side in both halves; `worstCasePlan`'s other names are all 200 bytes, where the drop is what it sees.

**THE LOCALE CHECKER GAINED NOTHING, AND THAT IS A DECISION.** Fix round 1's decision 5 says a composed key INSIDE the mod's prefix is REQUIRED and one outside it is an ADVISORY, and the library composed exactly three key shapes. It composes five now, and the two new ones are inside the prefix and MUST NOT be required: they are the author's own OPTIONAL entry, referenced precisely so an author who wrote one keeps it, and requiring them would make every consumer owe a description for every recipe and technology they emit, in a report `docs/migration.md` tells them to assert is EMPTY. They are not advisory either, because an advisory says "this key is not yours"; these two are. `CheckLocale` walks SETTINGS and never recipes or technologies, so the silence is structural and `testdata/locale/findings.golden` did not move. The pin on the count of three was in the two source-property tests rather than in the checker: each half now keeps a SETTINGS list and a PROTOTYPE list, and the walk asserts each stage composes its own and NONE of the other's, which is a stronger claim than the merged list could make. THE MERGED LIST IS DERIVED FROM THOSE TWO IN BOTH HALVES AND TYPED OUT IN NEITHER (`composedLocaleSections` appends them; `composed_locale_sections` chains them, and `COMPOSED_LOCALE_SECTION_COUNT` is their two lengths added, which is the width of the per-section tally). A hand-written merged list was the Rust half's shape until this amendment, and the failure it admits is silent: a section added to one of the two lists and forgotten in the merged one is a section the classifier never recognises, so its keys read as ordinary prose, the fault walk stays quiet over them, and the tally has no slot that could be zero.

**THE SOURCE-PROPERTY WALK LEARNED THE NEW SHAPE.** `localeRefFaults` / `locale_ref_faults` refused a key table that was not directly a wrapper's first alternative, and the new shape buries the key one level deeper. It now carries a `guarded` flag: a key table anywhere under a wrapper's non-last alternative is as safe as one sitting in that slot, which is the crux row above and not a loosening. The fixture both halves share gained a recipe and a technology carrying a note with no `Description`, so the data half of the walk is no longer asserting zero over a stage that composes nothing.

AND ONE PROOF MORE, TAKEN AGAINST THE TEST ITSELF RATHER THAN AGAINST THE LIBRARY. The Rust boundary test nested four `if let` bindings with no `else` on any of them, so a `description_ref` returning a differently shaped value AT THE FITTING LENGTH made every assertion under them vacuous and the test still passed; the Go twin indexes straight down and panics. Measured: with `description_ref` made to return `{"?", "DELIBERATE BREAK", ""}` at exactly `LOCALISED_ELEMENT_CEILING`, the nested-`if let` version passed, and so did the whole Rust suite (`249 passed; 0 failed`). The bindings are unconditional now, each with an `else` that names the value it got, and over the same break the test goes red: `recipe, the longest name whose key fits: the wrapper's second slot is Str("DELIBERATE BREAK"), not the concatenation group`. The break was reverted.

AND THE `guarded` RELAXATION IN THE FAULT WALK HAS NO RED PROOF OF ITS OWN AND CANNOT HAVE ONE, which both halves now say in the walk's own comment rather than leaving it looking checked. It is load-bearing (recursing with a flat `false` goes red on both prototype sections, in both halves) but it is a pure WIDENING, and widening it further cannot be caught: recursing with a flat `true` leaves the whole suite green in both halves, because the only thing it exempts is a key table under a wrapper's non-last alternative, which is the measured-safe slot. THE DETECTOR AROUND IT IS PROVED TWO WAYS in each half, both with `guarded` exactly as it stands: `localeRef` / `locale_ref` returning a bare key table goes red naming all three settings sections (`mod-setting-description.`, `string-mod-setting.`, `technology-name.`), and `descriptionRef` / `description_ref` returning one goes red naming both prototype sections (`recipe-description.`, `technology-description.`).

THE FOURTH PROOF IS WHY THE KIND IS A PARAMETER RATHER THAN A SWITCH IN THE COMPOSER. A fixture holding one kind would have passed the constant break, which is why `noteFixture` / `note_fixture` carries a recipe and a technology of each shape and the shared wrapper fixture carries one of each too.

|---|---|
| `testdata/mirror/transcript.golden` | TWO LINES, both the same prototype. Line 32, the `extend#22` op, and line 49, the FINAL dump that holds the same recipe: `fkrecipes-example-steel-rivet` declares a `DisplayName` and NO `Description`, and its ingredient text is the one the mirror's stand-in refuses, so its note is exactly the arm this decision changed. `"localised_description"={1="",2="The stored value of ...` became `"localised_description"={1="",2={1="?",2={1="",2={1="recipe-description.fkrecipes-example-steel-rivet"},3="\n"},3=""},3="The stored value of ...`, and the second chunk moved from index 3 to index 4. Nothing else in the file moved: the technology that carries a note in the mirror, `fkrecipes-example-hardened-tips`, DECLARES a description, so its shape is the unchanged three-parameter one |
| `testdata/ingame/dump-sha256.txt` | UNMOVED, both rows, both hashes, which was the prediction and is now the measurement: `e824cd83...` default data, `1cd61e0b...` flipped data, `0e62073f...` settings on both rows. The flipped row's two noted prototypes both DECLARE a `Description` (`fkrecipes-example-hardened-steel-plate-quenching` and `fkrecipes-example-hardened-tips`, read out of the run's own dump), so neither changed shape; the settings hash could not move because nothing on the settings side changed |
| `testdata/ingame/flipped.json`, `flipped.golden.dat` | unmoved; the `.dat` compared clean before any engine ran |
| `testdata/ingredient-list/cases.txt` | unmoved. No message the LANGUAGE builds changed |
| `testdata/locale/findings.golden`, `example.cfg` | unmoved, and that is the decision above rather than a coincidence: the two new keys are neither required nor advisory |

THE ENGINE ARM THAT DOES WALK THE NEW SHAPE IS THE DEMOTE ARM. `fkrecipes-example-steel-riveting` and `fkrecipes-example-chain-forging` carry packless notes and declare no `Description`, and `scripts/run-ingame.sh` pins both descriptions WHOLE. Both assertions moved to the new shape, taken out of a real dump (`jq -c '.technology[...].localised_description' tmp/ingame/raw-data-rust-demote.json`) rather than typed, and the comment beside them says the key is the author's own optional entry and that the engine renders it ABOVE the note where it exists. The arm exits 0 and the go and rust demote dumps still hash equal.

HOST TESTS MOVED IN BULK, which is not a golden but is the bigger half of the diff. Every transcript expectation carrying a note with no declared `Description` gained the wrapper: thirteen `noteIn` / `note_in` call sites (the helper takes the kind and the emitted name now), and about thirty hard-coded `localised_description=["", "..."]` expectations across `go/cycle_test.go`, `go/data_test.go`, `go/migration_test.go`, `go/packladder_test.go` and their Rust twins, each through one shared spelling, `descriptionRefIn` / `description_ref_in`. The two ceiling-walk fixtures gained a recipe and a technology at the fitting name length and their pinned count moved 28 to 30. The two shared wrapper fixtures gained a recipe and a technology carrying a note.

### Gates, decision 4

Run at this commit, exit codes read directly and never through a pipe, all 0: `gofmt -l .` (no output), `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./...`, the `go/examples/notext` vet, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo test` (249 unit, 2 in the public-surface witness, up from 245 by this decision's four new tests), `RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets`, `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.6 cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --workspace`, `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.6 scripts/run-mirror.sh` and `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.6 scripts/run-ingame.sh`. The mirror ran WITH `--update` once, after its diff was read and found to be the two lines above, and clean afterwards; the engine gate ran without `--update` throughout and moved no golden. `GOTOOLCHAIN` is pinned on the two harness lines because tinygo refuses a go1.27 toolchain. The binary was re-asked its version first: `2.0.77 (build 84539, mac-arm64, steam)`.

`LOCALISED_ELEMENT_CEILING` LOST ITS `#[cfg(test)]` GATE in the Rust half, because `description_ref` is a production reader of it now: the constant was test-only while the only thing comparing against it was the ceiling walk. The doc comment says so, and clippy with `-Dwarnings` over every target is what would have caught leaving the gate on.

THE JUMP ROWS. Go's `(*Lib).PlanData` widest span is 993,867 bytes, 152 percent of the 655,355-byte limit, relayed through 13 stations, with the widest block 13,029 bytes in `copyValue` and 314,648 bytes of block room; up 1,226 bytes from decision 3. Rust's `plan_data` is 399,414 bytes, 61 percent, no relay, widest block 3,800 bytes in `not_text` with 323,877 bytes of block room. The Lua register ceiling fix round 2 hit is not approached: the new arm adds one `if` and one prepend to `appendLocalised` and nothing to `PlanData` itself.

### Decision 5: the ladder's disclosure is a sentence this library composes

**THE DEFECT HAD TWO HALVES AND ONE CAUSE.** (This section is written as of the AMENDMENT that followed the adversarial review of the first draft: the sentences carry three clauses rather than two, a bare ingredient dropdown composes the ladder line where it used to compose nothing, and scope F opens on what was closed rather than on a universal. Each of those is marked where it lands.) The consumer's third assessment, finding 22, MISLED, scope F. Half one: the resolve-or-drop ingredient ladder gets no note on the emitted prototype, deliberately, and `agents/customizer-design.md` line 56, `docs/usage.md`, `go/data.go`'s note block and `rust/src/data.rs`'s twin all justified that by quoting a disclosure, "the dropdown's own composed description already discloses it (where one names something your mods do not have, the nearest thing they do have is used instead)". Grepping this repository for that sentence finds comments and documents and no literal. The live text was `mod-data/locale/en/better-belt-balancer.cfg:203`, the PILOT CONSUMER'S own entry, which a consumer may write differently or not at all. Half two: `agents/threat-model.md`'s scope F sentence was false on three clauses at once. One cause under both: a design decision whose evidence was outside the repository, written down as if it were inside it.

**THE THREE SENTENCES, AND WHERE EACH SITS.** All three are plain-string parameters, no table and no depth.

```
dropdownLadderLine  \nWhere an option names something your mods do not have, the next name it offers is used instead; an entry it offers nothing for is left out, and two that land on one name have their amounts added, so what you craft can be a shorter list than the one shown.
textLadderLine(t)   \nWhere a list this mod chose names something your mods do not have, the next name it offers is used instead; an entry it offers nothing for is left out, and two that land on one name have their amounts added, so what you craft can be a shorter list than the one shown.
textLadderLine(f)   \nWhere a list this mod chose names a science pack your mods do not have, the next name it offers is used instead; a pack it offers nothing for is left out, and two that land on one pack have their amounts added, so the research can take fewer packs than the list shows.
```

256, 268 and 269 bytes with the newline. An INGREDIENT dropdown's composition is now the consumer's key, the preset lines, `listWrapLine`, `dropdownLadderLine`, `dropdownSwitchLine`. A TEXT setting's is the consumer's key, the default line, `listWrapLine`, `textLadderLine`, `textFormatLine`, the switch line, `textFallbackLine`: seven parameters where it was six. A COST dropdown is unchanged.

**THE MERGE WAS THE THIRD CLAUSE AND THE FIRST DRAFT HAD TWO.** The sentences above are the amended ones. The draft this decision first shipped ended `and where it offers none that entry is left out, so what you craft can be shorter than the list shown`, which says what the ladder SUBSTITUTES and what it DROPS and nothing about what it MERGES. Measured on the amendment: two declared entries whose ladders both land on `iron-plate` emit ONE ingredient of amount 5, a number in no tooltip and in no declaration, and `mergeIngredient` / `merge_ingredient` records a note only where the sum crosses a ceiling, so below the ceiling the merge was disclosed nowhere at all. The consumer's own entry had it (`and where that leaves one item named twice the two amounts are added`), which made the ownership row above false for as long as the two-clause draft stood. The three sentences each carry all three clauses now, and each is over 200 bytes for it.

**AN INGREDIENT DROPDOWN WITH NO TEXT SETTING BESIDE IT WAS THE SHAPE THE FIRST DRAFT MISSED ENTIRELY.** `settingDescriptions` / `composed_dropdown_presets` stepped past it with the comment "A DROPDOWN WITH NO TEXT SETTING BESIDE IT COMPOSES NOTHING", so every claim this decision wrote into `go/data.go`, `rust/src/data.rs`, `agents/threat-model.md` and `docs/usage.md` was unqualified and false on it. It is a first-class shape here: `go/examples/notext` and `rust/examples/notext` are exactly it, with a three-rung ladder in the plan. It composes `{"", <the consumer's own description key, wrapped>, dropdownLadderLine}` now and nothing else. No preset lines, because rendering one needs a language and only `IngredientsSetting` / `ingredients_setting` installs one; the ladder line is a CONSTANT and links nothing, so "a plan with no text setting links no PARSER, no RENDERER, no AMOUNT FORMATTER and no CUSTOM-COST RESOLVER" still holds and `cd go/examples/notext && go vet .` still passes. No wrap line, because nothing typeable is rendered. No switch line, because there is no second field to name. In Rust the shape change is `Presets::Ingredients`'s text index becoming an `Option<usize>` and `presets_beside_text` being renamed `composed_dropdown_presets`, because a function named for a text setting beside the dropdown now answers for the case where there is none.

**THE LOCALE CHECKER GAINED THE SHAPE AND A SECOND SENTENCE.** A composed description makes the `[mod-setting-description]` entry REQUIRED, so `dropdownsWithComposedDescription` / `composed_dropdown_presets` marks a bare ingredient dropdown too. Its finding may not be the existing one, because "the library composes its preset list onto that entry" would name a list that dropdown composes nothing of; it is `the dropdown setting <name> has no [mod-setting-description] entry, and the library composes onto that entry the line saying what a name this game does not have costs the list`. The Go half's `dropdownsWithComposedDescription` returns a three-valued `dropdownComposition` for it rather than a `bool`. That is a NEW OBLIGATION on a consumer shipping a bare ingredient dropdown and `docs/migration.md`'s upgrade list names it with its one-line remedy. THE PILOT CONSUMER IS NOT AFFECTED: both of BetterBeltBalancer's dropdowns have a text setting beside them (`bbb-recipe-cost` beside `better-belt-balancer-recipe-ingredients`, `bbb-tech-cost` beside `better-belt-balancer-tech-packs`), so both were already on the required list and neither finding changes for it.

**THE HOLE THAT REMAINS.** A recipe declared with a plain `Ingredients` list and no setting at all has NO setting anywhere to carry the sentence, so on that plan the ladder is disclosed to the AUTHOR in `docs/usage.md` and nowhere a player looks. Every claim is scoped to "wherever this library composes a description" for that reason, and the shape is named in `agents/threat-model.md` scope F, in `docs/usage.md` and on this round's open list. It is bounded rather than open: a recipe whose ladders empty it COMPLETELY says so on its own tooltip on every plan, which is decision 6.

**THE PLACEMENT, AND WHY IT IS NOT WHAT THE BRIEF'S FIRST CLAUSE ASKED FOR.** The brief asked for the dropdown's line "after the last preset line and BEFORE the wrap line", and in the same sentence said the ladder line "must not come between a preset and the sentence about copying it" and to place it so `listWrapLine`'s doc comment stays true. Those two are not both satisfiable and the second is the operative one: `listWrapLine` names a RELATIONSHIP between a rendered list and the line under it ("the continuation is part of the same list"), so the lines directly above it have to be the lists it is about. A ladder sentence wedged between the last preset and the wrap line would leave that sentence pointing at prose. So the ladder line goes UNDER the wrap line, in both compositions, which is also literally what the brief asked for on the text side ("directly under the `default:` line's wrap line") and makes the two shapes read the same way: list, wrap, ladder. `textFormatLine`'s "as the default line above does" is now three lines up rather than two, which is as true; its doc comment says three.

**THE DEVIATION, RECORDED.** The brief asked for the dropdown line only where a choice carries `Fallbacks`. It is unconditional instead. A list with no ladder anywhere in it still DROPS an entry the game does not have, which is the half of the sentence that is always true, so conditioning would leave exactly the plans that can ONLY drop saying nothing at all. It is also what lets the locale checker's guard ask one composition function for one shape, as it already does for the other four lines, rather than having to know which choices carry ladders.

**A COST DROPDOWN GETS NOTHING, AND THE SOURCE LADDER WAS ASKED ABOUT SEPARATELY.** The dropdown is `listWrapLine`'s own case: a cost preset is a localised label followed by a localised technology name, so there is no rendered list of internal names for a ladder to shorten. The SOURCE ladder gets no sentence either, and not by inheritance: a preset line names ONE technology, the head of its `Sources` ladder, so a settings-screen sentence about the next rung would be about rungs the tooltip never rendered. What a source ladder actually does to a player is already disclosed where it happens, on the technology's own tooltip, by `packDroppedNote`, `packlessSourceNote`, `unreadableSourceNote` and `unpricedSourceNote`. The ingredient case is the opposite shape, which is why it is the one that gets a line: there the tooltip renders the whole list, and the list is what moves.

**THE 200-BYTE QUESTION, ANSWERED BY THE EXEMPTION AND THEN MEASURED.** All three sentences are over the engine's 200-byte-per-element ceiling. That ceiling is a DATA-STAGE prototype's, and a SETTING prototype is exempt, which fix round 3's chunking work measured at 201, 400, 1000, 2000 and 5000 bytes. Nothing here is chunked, and nothing may be: `localisedCarries` / `localised_carries` looks for a whole composed line inside ONE `Str` element, so chunking a settings line would make `CheckLocale` report every composed line as missing. The engine gate now emits a 269-byte setting element on a real 2.0.77 and reads it back whole out of `mod-settings-dump.json`; the two `utf8bytelength > 200` walks in `run-ingame.sh` are over `$FDUMP` and `$DDUMP`, the DATA dumps, and are untouched by this. THE CLAIM IS ABOUT THE LIBRARY'S OWN COMPOSED CONSTANTS AND NOT ABOUT SETTING ELEMENTS IN GENERAL: a consumer's declared list has always been able to render a default line past 200 bytes, with no ceiling short of the language's own 2000 characters, so "the first setting element over 200 bytes this library has shipped" would have been false. What is true is that these are the widest elements this library's own composed constants produce.

### What moved in the goldens, decision 5

(These three paragraphs are AS OF THE FIRST DRAFT. The amendment below moved two of the three again; the section after it says how.)

`testdata/mirror/transcript.golden` gained the ladder line on SEVEN settings, twice each (once in the `TRANSCRIPT extend` line and once in the `FINAL` `data.raw` dump), for fourteen occurrences where there were none: two ingredient dropdowns (`quench-medium`, `chain-links`), three ingredient text settings (`rivet-ingredients`, `quench-ingredients`, `chain-ingredients`) and two packs text settings (`tips-packs`, `chain-packs`). The cost dropdown `tips-research-tier` gained nothing, which is the negative the gate now asserts by name. The switch line's parameter index moved from 6 to 7 on a text setting and from 6 to 7 on an ingredient dropdown, and the fallback line's from 7 to 8; nothing else in the file moved.

`testdata/ingame/dump-sha256.txt`: the SETTINGS hash moved on both rows, `0e62073f23d9e904ac54b187651cb44c9a40dee9022d328c93b6e3027d9b923f` to `5cf08473463bfa8363e9f541cc395d82488fd619ee9c43f09394d9a7f5dadff5`, and NEITHER DATA HASH MOVED: `e824cd8302c00fd07c3c3416ad8d9a5eea5612bce5ee455f1cf9669eee1685cd` on the default row and `1cd61e0bdf9de34e2e76c9d74103a33636a82d95d6b4fb6584ca78b810aa1644` on the flipped one, both byte-identical to the previous commit's. That is the shape this change is supposed to have: nothing the data stage emits changed, only what the settings stage says about it. `testdata/ingame/flipped.golden.dat` did not move either (the gate reported "byte golden unchanged"), because no stored value changed.

`testdata/locale/findings.golden` DID NOT MOVE, and that is the right outcome rather than a missing update. `composedTextLinesMissing` / `composed_text_lines_missing` gained a fifth row in the same commit as the composition gained its fifth line, so a healthy plan reports nothing new. The proof that the golden is live rather than blind is the red proof above: dropping the line from the composition alone puts the new finding at the head of `check_locale_matches_the_golden`'s output. `testdata/locale/example.cfg` did not move, because the new line is the library's own prose and composes no locale key.

### The two gates, and what each gained

(As of the first draft; the amendment adds one more negative to `run-ingame.sh` and re-records both hash rows.)

`scripts/run-mirror.sh` pins the whole text description and the whole packs description as single `grep -qF` strings, so both grew their new parameter in place and the switch and fallback indices moved with them. Added beside them: one grep per vocabulary asserting the line is in the transcript at all, the dropdown's wrap-line pin extended to `5=wrap,6=ladder,7=switch` so the ORDER is pinned on a packaged guest as well as in the suites, a per-setting negative that no packs setting carries the ingredient vocabulary and no ingredient setting the packs one, and a second negative beside the existing one that the cost dropdown carries no ladder line.

`scripts/run-ingame.sh` gained the same shape in jq, against the SETTINGS dump: one `jqassert` per vocabulary naming the exact string, a walk over every `-packs` setting asserting the ingredient vocabulary is absent, and the cost dropdown negative beside the wrap one it already had. Read out of a real dump after the run, the three lines are present on `fkrecipes-example-rivet-ingredients`, `fkrecipes-example-chain-packs` and `fkrecipes-example-quench-medium`, and absent from `fkrecipes-example-tips-research-tier`.

Run at the first draft, exit codes read directly and never through a pipe, all 0: `gofmt -l .` (no output), `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go test -race ./...`, the `go/examples/notext` vet, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo test`, `RUSTFLAGS=-Dwarnings cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --workspace`, `scripts/run-mirror.sh` and `scripts/run-ingame.sh`.
